D_{\Box}(V)\simeq D_{\Box}(V(n))=D_{\Box}(V)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}D_{\Box}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{\otimes n}
of
F_{\lambda}
-spaces such that

D_{\Box}^{1}(V)
is identified with
D_{\Box}^{1-n}(V(n))
,
\Box=cris
or
dR
.
Under these identifications the strongly divisible
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}
-lattice associated with
T
is identified
with that associated with
T(n)
.

Remark 2.12
.

If
g
is ordinary, then the notion of
\omega\in S_{F}
being good defined in §
2.2.6
agrees with that
in
[
79
, §17.5]
for the lattice
T_{0}
. To see this, note that
\omega
is good in the former sense if and only if
\omega
is identified with an
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}
-generator of
D_{\lambda}^{1}(T_{0})=D_{\lambda}(T_{0})\cap D^{1}_{cris}(V)
, which is identified with

D_{\lambda}^{0}(T_{0}(1))=D_{\lambda}(T_{0}(1))\cap D^{0}_{cris}(V(1))
(see Remark
2.11
).
Here
D_{\lambda}(T_{0}(n))
is the strongly divisible lattice corresponding to
T_{0}(n)
. But when
g
is ordinary,
the latter is naturally isomorphic to
D(T_{0}^{-}(1))=(T_{0}^{-}(1)\otimes W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}))^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}}
, where

T_{0}^{-}=T_{0}\cap V^{-}
and

W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is the ring of Witt vectors of the algebraic closure
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
of
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
(this is naturally
identified with the completion
\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}
of the ring of integers of the maximal unramified extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
).
Being good in the sense of
[
79
, §17.5]
just means being identified with an
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}
-basis
of
D(T_{0}^{-}(1))
. In particular, if (irr
Q
) holds, then
\omega
being good in the sense of §
2.2.6

is the same as being good for the lattice
T
in the sense of
[
79
, §17.5]
.

2.4.2.
Coleman maps

Let
\alpha
be a root of
x^{2}-a_{g}(p)x+p
such that

{\mathrm{ord}}_{p}(\alpha)<1.

(2.7)

Let

[\cdot,\cdot]:D_{dR}(V(2))\times(F_{\lambda}(\alpha)\otimes_{F_{\lambda}}D_{cris}(V))\rightarrow F_{\lambda}(\alpha)\otimes_{F_{\lambda}}D_{cris}(F_{\lambda}(1))=F_{\lambda}(\alpha)

(2.8)

be the canonical pairing coming from (
2.5
) together with the canonical pairing
V(2)\times V\rightarrow F_{\lambda}(1)
.
(Here we have used that there is a canonical identification
V(2)\simeq V^{*}(1)
, arising from Poincaré duality as well as an identification
D_{cris}(F_{\lambda}(1))=F_{\lambda}

as in Remark
2.11
.) For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
, let
\eta_{\omega}\in F_{\lambda}(\alpha)\otimes_{F_{\lambda}}D_{cris}(V)
be the unique element such that
(i)
\varphi_{cris}(\eta_{\omega})=\alpha\cdot\eta_{\omega}
, and (ii)
[\omega,\eta_{\omega}]=1

(cf.
[
79
, Thm. 16.6]
). Here we view
\omega\in D_{dR}^{1}(V)\simeq D_{dR}^{-1}(V(2))
via (
2.4
)
(see also Remark
2.11
).

For
h\geq 1
, let

\mathfrak{K}_{h,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}=\bigg\{\sum_{n\geq 0}c_{n}T^{n}\in F_{\lambda}(\alpha)[\![T]\!]\bigg|\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}|c_{n}|_{p}\cdot n^{-h}=0\bigg\}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mathfrak{K}_{\infty,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}=\cup_{h\geq 1}\mathfrak{K}_{h,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}.

Under the identification of
\Lambda
with
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\![T]\!]
as in §
2.1.5
,

\mathfrak{K}_{h,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}
is a
\Lambda
-module.
Let

Col_{\eta_{\omega},{\mathcal{G}}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}})\rightarrow\mathscr{H}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}=\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\Delta]

be the Coleman map, which is the composition

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(2)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathfrak{L}_{\eta_{\omega}}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{H}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)},

for
\mathfrak{L}_{\eta_{\omega}}
as in
[
79
, Thm. 16.4]
(due to Perrin-Riou).
Here
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\subset G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
acts on the Iwasawa algebra
\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}
via the inverse of the canonical character
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}
(see (
2.1
))
and the first map in the composition is the isomorphism induced by the
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-isomorphism
V(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V(2)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}
,
given by

v\otimes\gamma\mapsto(v\otimes(\zeta_{p^{n}}))\otimes\epsilon^{-1}(\gamma)\gamma
for

\gamma\in{\mathcal{G}}
.

Here

(\zeta_{p^{n}})_{n}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))
is a fixed compatible system of
p^{n}
th roots of unity (this same choice is used in the definition of
\mathfrak{L}_{\eta_{\omega}}
).
The map
\mathfrak{L}_{\eta_{\omega}}
is a
\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}
-morphism, but

Col_{\eta_{\omega},{\mathcal{G}}}
is not due to the presence of the Tate twist in its definition. Instead,

Col_{\eta_{\omega},{\mathcal{G}}}
satisfies

Col_{\eta_{\omega},{\mathcal{G}}}(\lambda\cdot)=\epsilon^{-1}(\lambda)Col_{\eta_{\omega},{\mathcal{G}}}(\cdot),\ \ \lambda\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}.

Let
\iota_{\epsilon}:\Lambda\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda,\ \ \gamma\mapsto\epsilon(\gamma)\gamma
.
Restricting
Col_{{\eta_{\omega}},{\mathcal{G}}}
to the direct summand
corresponding to
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{(0)}
(which is canonically identified
with
\Lambda
) and then composing with the isomorphism obtained by
tensoring with
\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda
yields a

\Lambda
-module Coleman map

Col_{\eta_{\omega}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes\varepsilon_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle id\otimes 1}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes\varepsilon_{1})\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda\simeq\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda.

(2.9)

Recall that in (
2.9
),
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
is acting on
\Lambda
via the inverse
of the canonical character
\Psi
.

The ordinary case.

If
g
is ordinary, then we define

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)={\mathrm{Im}}(H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)).

(2.10)

We also put

H^{1}_{/{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=\frac{H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)}{H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)}.

(2.11)

For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
, the Coleman map
Col_{\eta_{\omega}}
induces an injection

Col_{\eta_{\omega}}:H^{1}_{/{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow F_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda\subset\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda,

(2.12)

and if (irr
Q
) holds and
\omega\in S_{F}
is good then the image is contained in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
with finite index. This follows from the corresponding properties for
Col_{\eta_{\omega},{\mathcal{G}}}
from

[
79
, Prop. 17.11]
.

The supersingular case.

In this case, for
\circ=\pm
let

H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\subset H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)

be the
signed
submodule defined as in
[
92
, §4.4]
(see also
[
88
, §7]
).
As in
[
79
]
, the definitions and constructions in
[
92
]
and
[
88
]
are made with

\Lambda
replaced by
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}
. The module above is then the summand corresponding
to the factor
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{(0)}=\Lambda
of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}
.
We also put

H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=\frac{H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)}{H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)}.

(2.13)

For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
there is a signed
Coleman map

Col^{\circ}_{\omega}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\rightarrow F_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda,

defined in
[
92
, §3.4.2]
. This is a
Frac(\mathfrak{K}_{\infty,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)})
-linear
combination of the Coleman maps
Col_{\eta_{\omega}}
associated with the two roots
\alpha=\pm\sqrt{-p}
of the Hecke polynomial
x^{2}+p
at
p
.
By
[
92
, Cor. 4.11]

the kernel of
Col^{\circ}_{\omega}
is
H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
, and there is an induced injection

Col^{\circ}_{\omega}:H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow F_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda

(2.14)

with image in
\lambda^{s_{\circ}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
for some
s_{\circ}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
If
\omega
is good, then the image is contained in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
with finite index
(cf.
[
28
, Thm. 2.14 and Rmk. 2.15]
). As in the ordinary case, these results just follow from the corresponding
results with
\Lambda
replaced by
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}
. We emphasize that
Col^{\circ}_{\omega}
is a
\Lambda
-module map.

2.5.
Newforms: Quadratic twists

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N)
be a newform as in §
2.2
.
In this subsection we compare many of the previous definitions and constructions for
g
and certain of its quadratic twists.
Here in particular the conventions of §
2.2.12
will be in use.

2.5.1.
The Imaginary quadratic field
L

Throughout,
L\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
will denote an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{L}<0
.

Write
N=N_{L}N_{L}^{\prime}
where
N_{L}
(resp
N_{L}^{\prime}
) is a product of primes that divide (resp. do not divide)
D_{L}
.
We will always suppose that

p
splits in
L
:
(p)=v\overline{v}
with
v
determined via
\iota_{p}

(2.15)

and that

N_{L}
is squarefree.

(2.16)

While neither condition is always needed, each is an essential feature of some of our later arguments.

Let
\chi_{L}:{\mathbb{Z}}/D_{L}{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}
be the primitive quadratic character of conductor
D_{L}
associate with
L
. We also write

\chi_{L}
for the quadratic character of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
associated with
L
. These characters are identified by the reciprocity map of class field theory.

The chosen embedding
\iota_{p}
determines an isomorphism
L_{v}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, using which we may take
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
to be the chosen separable algebraic closure of
L_{v}

and
\iota_{p}
as the chosen embedding of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
into
\overline{L}_{v}
. In particular,
G_{L_{v}}\subset G_{L}
is identified with
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\subset G_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Similarly, the composite
\iota_{p}\circ\tau:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
determines an isomorphism
L_{\bar{v}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, using which we identify
G_{L_{\bar{v}}}\subset G_{L}
with

\tau G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\tau^{-1}\subset G_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

2.5.2.
The quadratic twist
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}

Let
g^{\prime}
be the twist of
g
by
\chi_{L}
, which has
q
-expansion

g^{\prime}(q)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\chi_{L}(n)\cdot a_{f}(n)q^{n}.

Since
N_{L}
is squarefree,

g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
is a newform of level
\mathrm{lcm}(N,D_{L}^{2})=N_{L}^{\prime}D_{L}^{2}
, weight
2
, and trivial Nebentypus.
The Hecke field of
g^{\prime}
is the same as that of
g
.

2.5.3.
Quadratic twists I: periods

We record the following relation between the periods of
g
and
g^{\prime}
.

Lemma 2.13
.

(i)

We have

\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\cdot\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{\pm}\sim_{F^{\times}}\Omega_{g}^{\mp}.

(ii)

Suppose that (irr
Q
) holds.
Then

\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\cdot\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{\pm}\sim_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}}\Omega_{g}^{\mp}.

Here, as before, ‘
\sim_{\Box}
’ denotes equality up to an element in
\Box
.

Proof.

Let
\ell\nmid ND_{L}
be a prime. By Theorem
2.9
there exists
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\ell}^{cyc}
such that

L(1,g^{\prime},\chi)\neq 0.

As
L(1,g^{\prime},\chi)=L(1,g,\chi_{L}\chi)
, it then follows from Theorem
2.8
(i) applied to both

L(1,g^{\prime},\chi)
and
L(1,g,\chi_{L}\chi)
together with the factorisation

\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L}\overline{\chi})=\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\mathfrak{g}(\overline{\chi})
of Gauss sums that

\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\cdot\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{+}\sim_{F_{\chi}^{\times}}\Omega_{g}^{-}.

Let
\ell^{\prime}\nmid ND_{L}\ell
be another prime.
The same argument as above shows that there exists
\chi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\ell^{\prime}}^{cyc}
such that

\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\cdot\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{+}\sim_{F_{\chi^{\prime}}^{\times}}\Omega_{g}^{-}.

As
F_{\chi}\cap F_{\chi^{\prime}}=F
,
it follows that
\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\cdot\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{+}\sim_{F^{\times}}\Omega_{g}^{-}
.

That
\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\cdot\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{-}\sim_{F^{\times}}\Omega_{g}^{+}
can be seen by
reversing the roles of
g
and
g^{\prime}
in the preceding argument and noting that

L^{\{q\mid D_{L}\}}(1,g,\chi)=L(1,g^{\prime},\chi_{L}\chi)
,
so
L(1,g,\chi_{\ell})\sim_{F_{\chi}^{\times}}L(1,g^{\prime},\chi_{L}\chi_{\ell})
, and that
\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{2}=\pm D_{L}
.

Suppose now that (irr
Q
) holds.
Let
\varepsilon
be a sign.
By Theorem
2.10
, there exists a Dirichlet character
\chi
of sign
-\varepsilon
such that
(pND_{L},\mathrm{cond^{r}}(\chi))=1
and

v_{p}\bigg(\mathfrak{g}(\overline{\chi})\cdot\frac{L(1,g^{\prime},\chi)}{\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{-\varepsilon}}\bigg)=0.

Since by Theorem
2.8
(ii),

\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L}\overline{\chi})\cdot\frac{L(1,g,\chi_{L}\chi)}{\Omega_{g}^{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{O}_{F_{\chi},(\lambda_{\chi})},

and since
L(1,g^{\prime},\chi)=L(1,g,\chi_{L}\chi)
and
\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L}\overline{\chi})=\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\mathfrak{g}(\overline{\chi})
,
it follows that

\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\cdot\frac{\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{-\varepsilon}}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{g}}\in\mathcal{O}_{F_{\chi},(\lambda_{\chi})}.

Part (ii) then follows upon reversing the roles of
g
and
g^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon
and
-\varepsilon
in this argument and using that
\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{2}=\pm D_{L}\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
.
∎

Remark 2.14
.

For an alternative approach to proving the preceding lemma, see
[
132
, Lem. 9.6]
.

Remark 2.15
.

If
g
corresponds to the isogeny class of an elliptic curve
E_{\bullet}
as in Remark
2.3
, then
the conclusion of part (ii) of Lemma
2.13
still holds even when (irr
Q
) does not.
This is a consequence of the main result of
[
110
]
.

2.5.4.
Quadratic twists II: identifications and rigidifications

In view of the characterising properties of the
\lambda
-adic Galois representations
V_{g}

and
V_{g^{\prime}}
,
there is an isomorphism

V_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\simeq V_{g^{\prime}}

(2.17)

of
F_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules.

If (irr
Q
) holds, then any two
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}
lattices in
V_{g}
(or
V_{g^{\prime}}
) are scalar multiples of one another. In particular,
there is an isomorphism

T_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\simeq T_{g^{\prime}}

(2.18)

of
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules.

For subsequent arguments it will be convenient to choose an isomorphism (
2.17
) or even an isomorphism (
2.18
) (which then induces an isomorphism (
2.17
)).

Rigidifications.

The choice of an isomorphism (
2.17
) can be rigidified as in the following lemma.

Lemma 2.16
.

(a)

Let

S_{F,g}\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{F,g^{\prime}}

(2.19)

be a fixed isomorphism of one-dimensional
F
-vector spaces. There exists a unique isomorphism

V_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{g^{\prime}}

(2.20)

of
F_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{F,g}\otimes_{F}F_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}_{dR}(V_{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{F,g^{\prime}}\otimes_{F}F_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}_{dR}(V_{g^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

commutes.
Here the horizontal isomorphisms are induced via (
2.4
), and the first (resp. second) vertical isomorphism is induced via (
2.19
) (resp. (
2.20
)).

(b)

Suppose that (irr
Q
) holds. Let

S_{\mathcal{O},g}\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{\mathcal{O},g^{\prime}}

(2.21)

be a fixed isomorphism of rank one
\mathcal{O}
-modules. There exists a unique isomorphism

T_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}T_{g^{\prime}}

(2.22)

of
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{O},g}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\lambda}^{1}(T_{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{O},g^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}_{\lambda}(T_{g^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

commutes.
Here the horizontal isomorphisms are induced via (
2.6
), and the first (resp. second) vertical isomorphism is induced via (
2.21
) (resp. (
2.22
)).

Proof.

Let
h:V_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{g^{\prime}}
be any isomorphism of
F_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules.
Then
h
induces

D^{1}_{dR}(V_{g})\simeq D^{1}_{dR}(V_{g^{\prime}})

and hence an isomorphism

S_{F,g}\otimes_{F}F_{\lambda}\simeq S_{F,g^{\prime}}\otimes_{F}F_{\lambda}

by (
2.4
). As these are all one-dimensional
F_{\lambda}
-spaces, this last isomorphism must be

a
times the one induced via (
2.19
) for some
a\in F_{\lambda}^{\times}
.
Then
a^{-1}\cdot h
is the desired isomorphism. The uniqueness follows from
V_{g}
being an irreducible
F_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-module.
This proves part (a).

In light of (
2.6
) and and the fact that (irr
Q
) implies that all
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable lattices in
V_{g}
are scalar multiples of one another,
essentially the same argument proves part (b).
∎

Remark 2.17
.

Lemma
2.16
is a variant of
[
79
, Lem. 15.11(1)]
.

Optimal rigidifications.

Let
c_{g}
and
c_{g^{\prime}}
be fixed generators of the congruence ideals of
g
and
g^{\prime}
, respectively.
In the rest of this paper, we choose the isomorphism (
2.19
) so that

S_{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{F}^{\prime},\ \ \frac{\omega_{g}}{c_{g}}\mapsto\frac{\omega_{g^{\prime}}}{c_{g^{\prime}}}.

(2.23)

By Lemma
2.16
this choice determines an isomorphism

Tw:V_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{g^{\prime}}.

(2.24)

If (irr
Q
) also holds, then by Lemma
2.5
both
\frac{\omega_{g}}{c_{g}}
and
\frac{\omega_{g^{\prime}}}{c_{g^{\prime}}}

are good in the sense defined of §
2.2.6
, and so the map (
2.23
) is determined by an
an isomorphism (
2.21
). In this case, the map

Tw
arises from an isomorphism (
2.18
).

2.5.5.
Quadratic twists III: Coleman maps

We assume that (
2.19
) has been fixed (say as in (
2.23
)).
Let
Tw:V\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{g^{\prime}}
be the isomorphism as in (
2.24
) (so as in Lemma
2.16
(a)).

Since
p
splits in
L
by hypothesis,
a_{g}(p)=a_{g^{\prime}}(p)
and we may choose

\alpha=\alpha_{g}=\alpha_{g^{\prime}}

to be a root of the Hecke polynomial at
p
as in §
2.4.2
.

For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F,g}
, let
\eta_{\omega}\in F_{\lambda}(\alpha)\otimes_{F_{\lambda}}D_{cris}(V_{F_{\lambda},g})
be the element in §
2.4.2

Let
\omega^{\prime}\in S_{F,g^{\prime}}
be the image of
\omega
under the fixed isomorphism (
2.19
).
Then the pair
(\alpha,\omega^{\prime})
gives rise to

\eta_{\omega}^{\prime}=\eta_{\omega^{\prime}}\in F_{\lambda}(\alpha)\otimes_{F_{\lambda}}D_{cris}(V_{F_{\lambda},g^{\prime}})

as in §
2.4.2
.
The isomorphism
Tw
induces an identification

D_{cris}(V_{g})\simeq D_{cris}(V_{g^{\prime}})
, and
\eta_{\omega}^{\prime}
is just the image of
\eta_{\omega}
under this identification.

The isomorphism
Tw
also induces isomorphisms

Tw_{*}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},(T_{g}(n)\otimes\chi_{L})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T_{g^{\prime}}(n)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},

and even without tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
if
Tw:T_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}T_{g^{\prime}}
.
The Coleman maps for
g
and
g^{\prime}
are related via
Tw_{*}
as follows.

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
.

(a)

We have
Col_{\eta_{\omega}}=Col_{{\eta}_{\omega}^{\prime}}\circ Tw_{*}
.

(b)

In the supersingular case,
Col^{\circ}_{{\omega}}=Col^{\circ}_{{\omega}^{\prime}}\circ Tw_{*}
.

Proof.

Part (a) easily from the commutativity of the diagram in Lemma
2.16
(a) and the definition of the Coleman maps.
The key point is that
\mathfrak{L}_{\eta_{\omega}}=\mathfrak{L}_{\eta_{\omega}^{\prime}}\circ{Tw_{*}}
,
which follows from the functorial properties of Perrin-Riou’s logarithm and the choices of
\eta_{\omega}
and
\eta_{\omega}^{\prime}
.
Part (b) follows from part (a) and the definition of the signed Coleman map
Col_{\omega}^{\circ}
(resp.
Col^{\circ}_{\omega^{\prime}}
)
as a
Frac(\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)})
-linear
combination of the Coleman maps
Col_{\eta_{\omega}}
associated with the two roots of the Hecke polynomial at
p
.
∎

We have corresponding identifications of the ordinary and signed submodules.

Lemma 2.19
.

The isomorphism
Tw_{*}
induces an isomorphism

Tw_{*}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T_{g}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\ \xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T_{g^{\prime}}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\ \ \text{if $g$ is ordinary,}

and an isomorphism

Tw_{*}:H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T_{g}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T_{g^{\prime}}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\ \ \text{if $g$ is supersingular.}

If
Tw:T_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}T_{g^{\prime}}
, then these are isomorphisms without tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Proof.

This is an easy consequence of the definitions of the cohomology groups and the map
Tw_{*}
. Alternatively, this
follows from Lemma
2.18
together with these subgroups being the kernels of the corresponding Coleman maps.
∎

3.
Beilinson–Kato elements

In this section, we recall the Beilinson–Kato elements of Kato
[
79
]
, introduce a variant defined with respect to an auxiliary imaginary quadratic field,
and describe their connections to
p
-adic
L
-functions as well as related results about Iwasawa cohomology groups.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a newform as in §
2.2
and let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field as in §
2.5.1
.
As before, let
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
be the twist of
g
by
\chi_{L}
.
We work with the rigidifications associated with the choice of isomorphism (
2.23
) and in particular with the consequential
isomorphism (
2.24
).

3.1.
Some Iwasawa cohomology groups

For
K
a number field with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
M=T=T_{g}
,
T_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}
, or
T_{g^{\prime}}
, we define

H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K}[\frac{1}{p}],M(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=H^{1}(G_{K,\Sigma},M(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)

for any finite set
\Sigma
of places of
K
that contains all the primes dividing
pND_{L}\infty
(just those dividing
pN
is enough for
M=T_{g}
).
Here
G_{K}
acts on
\Lambda
via the inverse
\Psi^{-1}
of the canonical character (see §
2.1.5
).
This is a finitely-generated
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module.
It is a torsion-free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module if (irr
L
) holds.

Remark 3.1
.

As the notation indicates,
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K}[\frac{1}{p}],M(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is independent of the choice of
\Sigma
,
and the classes in
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K}[\frac{1}{p}],M(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
are unramified at all finite places not dividing
p

(cf. the proof of
[
79
, Lem. 8.5]
). The key point is that
H^{1}(G_{K,\Sigma},M(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}({\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\Sigma}/K(\zeta_{p^{n}})),M(1))^{(0)}
, where the superscript
(0)
denotes the

\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}
-summand on which
\Delta
acts trivially.

The natural action of
\tau
(which restricts to the non-trivial automorphism of
L
)
induces a decomposition

H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{+}\oplus H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{-},

(3.1)

where
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{\pm}
is the
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-submodule on which

\tau
acts as multiplication by
\pm 1
. Then restriction to
G_{L}
yields identifications

H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{+}

(3.2)

and

H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],(T(1)\otimes\chi_{L})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{-}.

(3.3)

Since the rigidifications fix an isomorphism
Tw:V\otimes\chi_{L}=V_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{g^{\prime}}
,
we view an element of
H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T_{g^{\prime}}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
as an element of

H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],(T(1)\otimes\chi_{L})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and hence as an element of

H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{-}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
If (irr
Q
) holds, then the same holds without tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

3.1.1.
Local conditions

The fixed choices of decomposition groups at
w=v,\bar{v}
determine isomorphisms
\iota_{w}:H^{1}(L_{w},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
. For
w=v
this is just the identity map, corresponding to
G_{L_{v}}=G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\subset G_{\mathbb{Q}}
. For
w=\bar{v}
,
G_{L_{\bar{v}}}=\tau G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\tau^{-1}\subset G_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and
the isomorphism
\iota_{\bar{v}}
is that determined by the map on cycles
c\mapsto(\sigma\mapsto\tau^{-1}c(\tau^{-1}\sigma\tau))
.

Suppose
g
is either ordinary or in the supersingular case. Let

H^{1}_{\Box}(L_{w},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=\iota_{w}^{-1}\big(H^{1}_{\Box}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\big),\ \ \Box=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{ord}}&\text{$g$ is ordinary}\\
\circ&\text{$g$ is supersingular.}\end{cases}

We then define global Iwasawa cohomology groups with local restrictions:

H^{1}_{\Box}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=\bigg\{\kappa\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\kappa)\in H^{1}_{\Box}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\bigg\},

(3.4)

and

H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)=\bigg\{\kappa\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\kappa)\in H^{1}_{\Box}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\bigg\},

(3.5)

where
\Box
has the same meaning as above.

3.1.2.
Coleman maps at
v
and
\bar{v}

We transport the Coleman map to
H^{1}(L_{w},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
via
\iota_{w}
: For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
we let

Col_{\eta_{\omega},w}=Col_{\eta_{\omega}}\circ\iota_{w}.

Similarly, if
g
is supersingular we let

Col^{\circ}_{{\omega},w}=Col^{\circ}_{{\omega}}\circ\iota_{w}.

We make analogous definitions of Coleman maps on
H^{1}(L_{w},T_{g^{\prime}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
. These are related as follows,

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
.

(a)

We have
Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}=Col_{\eta^{\prime}_{\omega},v}\circ Tw_{*}
and
Col_{\eta_{\omega},\bar{v}}=-Col_{\eta^{\prime}_{\omega},\bar{v}}\circ Tw_{*}
.

(b)

In the supersingular case we have
Col^{\circ}_{{\omega},v}=Col^{\circ}_{{\omega^{\prime}},v}\circ Tw_{*}
and
Col^{\circ}_{{\omega},\bar{v}}=-Col^{\circ}_{{\omega^{\prime}},\bar{v}}\circ Tw_{*}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.18
(a) we have

Col_{\eta_{\omega},w}=Col_{\eta_{\omega}}\circ\iota_{w,g}=Col_{\eta^{\prime}_{\omega}}\circ Tw_{*}\circ\iota_{w,g}=Col_{\eta^{\prime}_{\omega},w}\circ\iota_{w,g^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ Tw_{*}\circ\iota_{w,g}.

Part (a) then follows from
\iota_{v,g^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ Tw_{*}\circ\iota_{v,g}=Tw_{*}
and
\iota_{{\bar{v}},g^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ Tw_{*}\circ\iota_{{\bar{v}},g}=-Tw_{*}
. The change in sign arises from the twisting by

\tau
in the definition of the maps
\iota_{{\bar{v}},g}
and
\iota_{{\bar{v}},g^{\prime}}
: the action of
\tau
on
V_{g^{\prime}}\simeq V_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}
is identified with
-1
times the action of
\tau
on
V_{g}
.

Part (b) follows from part (a).
∎

3.2.
Beilinson–Kato elements

We recall Kato’s zeta elements (the Beilinson–Kato elements) and define a variant over the imaginary quadratic field
L
.

3.2.1.
Over the rationals

In
[
79
]
Kato introduced a collection of elements

\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

(the Beilinson–Kato elements) associated with the newform
g
and elements
\gamma\in V
. Among their properties are the following:

(i)

The map

V\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\ \ \gamma\mapsto\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g),

is an
F_{\lambda}
-linear homomorphism.

(ii)

For
\gamma\in V
with
\gamma^{+},\gamma^{-}\neq 0
,

\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)\neq 0.

(iii)

If (irr
Q
) holds, then

\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\ \text{for}\ \gamma\in T.

A characterising property of the morphism
\gamma\mapsto{\mathbf{z}}_{\gamma}(g)
in terms of the Bloch–Kato dual exponential maps
is given in
[
79
, Thm. 12.5 (1)]
. Other properties are recalled below (cf. §§
3.2.2
and
3.4.2
).

More precisely, Kato defined elements
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}^{(p)}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}})
.
The element we denote
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)
is the image of Kato’s
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}^{(p)}
under the composition of
the maps induced by the isomorphism
T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}

and the projection
T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}\twoheadrightarrow T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{(0)}=T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda
(for the first isomorphism see the definition of the Coleman maps in §
2.4.2
).

Remark 3.3
.

Both
[
87
, Thm. 5.2 iv)]
and
[
146
]
address further the issue of
the integrality of the Beilinson–Kato elements
{\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(f)
(property (iii)).
In particular, if
g
corresponds to the isogeny class of an elliptic curve
E_{\bullet}
as in Remark
2.3
, then

\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
for
\gamma\in T
even if (irr
Q
) does not hold (see
[
146
, Thm. 13]
).

3.2.2.
Under Coleman maps

Let
\alpha
be as in (
2.7
). Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
.
For
\gamma\in V_{F}
let

L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)=Col_{\eta_{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(g)))\in\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda.

(3.6)

In the supersingular case, let

L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)=Col^{\circ}_{{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(g))).

(3.7)

As we recall in §
3.4
below, Kato’s explicit reciprocity law yields the following.

Lemma 3.4
.

(i)

If
\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0
, then
L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\neq 0
, and furthermore, if
g
is supersingular, then
we also have
L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)\neq 0
.

(ii)

If
g
is ordinary, then

L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\in F_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda
, and if
g
is supersingular,
then
L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)\in F_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda.

(iii)

Suppose (irr
Q
) holds and
\omega
is good. Suppose also that
\gamma\in T
.
If
g
is ordinary, then

L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
, and if
g
is supersingular,
then
L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
.

Proof.

Part (i) is a consequence of
{\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(g)
being non-trivial (property (i) of the Beilinson–Kato elements),
the relation of
{\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(g)
with twisted central
L
-values
L(1,g,\chi)
of
g

[
79
, Thm. 12.5 (2)]
,
and the interpolation property of the Coleman map
[
79
, Thm. 16.4 (i)]
. (This is made more explicit in §
3.4
below.)
Part (ii) is a consequence of the Coleman map being bounded (
2.12
), (
2.14
).
Part (iii) is a consequence of the integrality of
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)
(property (iii)) and the integrality of the Coleman maps (cf. §
2.4.2
).
∎

3.2.3.
Over
L

Let
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
. We choose
\alpha_{g^{\prime}}=\alpha_{g}=\alpha
to satisfy (
2.7
).
For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F,g}
, let
\omega^{\prime}\in S_{F,g^{\prime}}
be the image of
\omega
under the fixed isomorphism (
2.23
),
as in §
2.5.5
. Let
\gamma\in V=V_{g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}=V_{g^{\prime}}
.
To the quadruple
(\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})
we associate elements in the Iwasawa cohomology groups as follows.

The ordinary case.
Suppose
g
is ordinary. We let

\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)+L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\bigg)\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}.

Here we are using that
L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime}),L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
by
Lemma
3.4
(ii) and
that we can view the element
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})
as belonging to
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{-}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

as in §
3.1
.

The supersingular case.
Suppose
g
is supersingular. We let

\mathbf{z}^{\circ}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})=\mathbf{z}^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(L^{\circ}_{\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)+L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\bigg)\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}.

Here we are using that
L^{\circ}_{\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime}),L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
by
Lemma
3.4
(ii) and
that we can view the elements
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})
as belonging to
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)^{-}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

as in §
3.1
. Though the construction does not depend on the choice of
\alpha
, it is sometimes included in the notation for convenience.

It follows from property (iii) of the Beilinson–Kato classes along with Lemma
3.4
that

\mathbf{z}^{\Box}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\ \ \text{if (irr${}_{\mathbb{Q}}$) holds, $\omega$ is good, $\gamma\in T_{g}$, $\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime}}$,}\ \ \Box={\mathrm{ord}},\circ.

(3.8)

These elements belong to the global Iwasawa cohomology groups defined in §
3.5
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
. Let
\gamma\in V_{g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in V_{g^{\prime}}
. Then

\mathbf{z}^{\Box}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\ \ \ \Box=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{ord}}&\text{$g$ is ordinary}\\
\circ&\text{$g$ is supersingular.}\end{cases}

Furthermore, if (irr
Q
) holds,
\omega
is good, and
\gamma\in T_{g}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime}}
then this inclusion holds without tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}^{\Box}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
.
Suppose first that
g
is ordinary. We have

\begin{split}Col_{\eta_{\omega},\bar{v}}(\mathbf{z})&=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})Col_{\eta_{\omega},\bar{v}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)))+L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)Col_{\eta_{\omega},\bar{v}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})))\bigg)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})Col_{\eta_{\omega},\bar{v}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)))-L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)Col_{\eta_{\omega}^{\prime},\bar{v}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})))\bigg)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})Col_{\eta_{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)))-L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)Col_{\eta_{\omega}^{\prime}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})))\bigg)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)-L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\bigg)=0.\end{split}

In the first line (as in the definition of
\mathbf{z}
) we are viewing
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})
as belonging to
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
via
res|_{G_{L}}\circ Tw_{*}^{-1}
.
The second equality then follows from Lemma
3.2
. The third follows from the second by the definitions of the Coleman maps for
\bar{v}
.
This proves the lemma for
g
ordinary. The proof for the supersingular case is the same, replacing the Coleman maps with the signed Coleman maps.

The inclusion without tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
then follows from (
3.8
).
∎

Remark 3.6
.

Suppose
g
corresponds to the isogeny class of an elliptic curve
E_{\bullet}
as in Remark
2.3
but (irr
Q
) does not hold.
Even though
z_{\gamma}(g)
and
z_{\gamma}^{\prime}(g^{\prime})
are integral (see Remark
3.3
), we cannot immediately conclude the same of

\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha.\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
.
This is because we have not shown in this case that the rigidification
Tw
identifies
T_{g}
with
T_{g}^{\prime}
(an identification of
T_{g}^{\prime}
with a sublattice of
T_{g}
would be enough).
However, we will later see that the integrality of
\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha.\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
in this case is a consequence of the integrality of related Beilinson–Flach classes
(cf. Remark
5.27
).

3.3.
Consequences for Iwasawa cohomology

We explain some consequences of the non-vanishing of the Beilinson-Kato elements
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)
and
\mathbf{z}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g/L)
under the Coleman maps.

3.3.1.
Over the rationals

Kato
[
79
]
shows that the classes
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)
are essentially the base layer of an Euler system, from which he deduces a number of consequences
for the global Iwasasa cohomology groups
H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
.
The following theorem is an immediate consequence of
[
79
, Thm. 12.4]
and plays a crucial role in our subsequent arguments.

Theorem 3.7
.

(a)

The global Iwasawa cohomology group
H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is a torsion-free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module. Moreover,

H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is a free
(\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-module of rank one.

(b)

If (Van
Q
) holds, then
H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is a free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module of rank one.

Remark 3.8
.

If (Van
Q
) holds but not (irr
Q
) holds, then part (b) can be deduced from (a) just as in the argument at the end of the proof of
[
146
, Lem. 9]
.
As a consequence, if
g
corresponds to the isogeny class of an elliptic curve
E_{\bullet}
as in Remark
2.3
such that
E_{\bullet}[p]({\mathbb{Q}})=0
, then the conclusion of part (b) of Theorem
3.7
holds (see also the first paragraph of the the proof of
[
146
, Thm. 13]
).

3.3.2.
Over
L

Combining Theorem
3.7
for
T_{g}
and
T_{g^{\prime}}
with (
spl
), (
3.2
), and (
3.3
), we immediately conclude the following:

Theorem 3.9
.

(a)

The global Iwasawa cohomology group
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is a torsion-free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module. Moreover,

H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is a free
(\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-module of rank two.

(b)

If (Van
Q
) holds (so in particular if (irr
Q
) holds), then
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is a free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module of rank two.

We note that the hypotheses on
g
and
L
imply that if (Van
Q
) holds for
\overline{T}_{g}
, then
(Van
L
) holds for
\overline{T}_{g}
and (Van
Q
) holds for
\overline{T}_{g^{\prime}}
.

Remark 3.10
.

If (Van
Q
) holds for
\overline{T}_{g}
but (irr
Q
) does not, then
T_{g}\otimes\chi_{L}
may be not be identified with
T_{g^{\prime}}
. However, the freeness of

H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T_{g^{\prime}}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
as a
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
-module can be used along with (Van
Q
) for
\overline{T}_{g^{\prime}}

to conclude that
H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],(T_{g}(1)\otimes\chi)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is also a free
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
-module of rank 1, whence
the conclusion of part (b) of the theorem. The proof of this latter freeness is just as in Remark
3.8
.

As a consequence, if
g
corresponds to the isogeny class of an elliptic curve
E_{\bullet}
as in Remark
2.3

and
E_{\bullet}[p]({\mathbb{Q}})=0
, then the conclusion of part (b) of Theorem
3.9
holds.

Combining Theorem
3.9
with the non-vanishing of the Coleman maps on the zeta elements
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)

(see Lemma
3.6
)(i)), we can deduce an analog of Theorem
3.7
for
H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
.

Theorem 3.11
.

Suppose
g
is ordinary or in the supersingular case. Let
\Box={\mathrm{ord}}
or
\circ
, accordingly.

(a)

The global Iwasawa cohomology group
H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is a torsion-free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module. Moreover,

H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is a free
(\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-module of rank one.

(b)

If (Van
Q
) holds, then
H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is a free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module of rank one.

Proof.

Since
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
(resp.
\Lambda
) is a regular ring of dimension two (resp. a complete local regular ring of dimension two),
to prove part (a) (resp. part (b)) it suffices to show that

H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
is the kernel of a
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-morphism

C:H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
with
non-zero image.

Suppose first that
g
is ordinary. Then we can take the map
C
to be
Col_{\eta_{\omega},\bar{v}}\circ{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}
for some

0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
and
\alpha
as in (
2.7
). Replacing
\omega
with a non-zero scalar multiple if necessary,
it follows from (
2.12
) that
C
takes values in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
and that
H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)

is the kernel of
C
. For
\gamma\in V
with
\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0
, we have

C(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g))=L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)
, which is non-zero by Lemma
3.6
(i), so the theorem follows in this case.

Similarly, if
g
is in the supersingular case, then the map
C=Col^{\circ}_{{\omega},\bar{v}}\circ{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}
,
for a suitable
\omega
, has the desired properties.
∎

Theorem 3.12
.

Suppose
g
is ordinary or in the supersingular case. Let
\Box={\mathrm{ord}}
or
\circ
, accordingly.
Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
, let
\alpha=\alpha_{g}=\alpha_{g^{\prime}}
satisfy (
2.7
), and let
\gamma\in V_{g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in V_{g^{\prime}}
such that

\gamma^{\pm},(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}\neq 0
.

(a)

The class
\mathbf{z}^{\Box}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

is not
(\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-torsion.

(b)

The class
\mathbf{z}^{\Box}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}_{rel,\Box}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)

is not
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-torsion if (irr
{}_{\mathbb{Q}})
holds,
\omega
is good,
\gamma\in T_{g}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.11
it suffices to show that
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}^{\Box}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
is non-zero.
This follows by evaluating
{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(\mathbf{z})
under
Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}
if
g
is ordinary and under
Col^{\circ}_{{\omega},v}
in the supersingular case.
A similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
3.5

shows that

Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}(\mathbf{z})=L_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\neq 0,

which proves the ordinary case, and that in the supersingular case

Col^{\circ}_{{\omega},v}(\mathbf{z})=L^{\circ}_{\omega^{\prime}_{F},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)\neq 0.

∎

3.4.
Connections to
p
-adic
L
-functions

We recall the cyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-functions of
g
and
g^{\prime}
and their relations with the Beilinson–Kato elements.

3.4.1.
Characters in cyclotomic towers

For
\zeta
a primitive
p^{t}
-th root of unity, let

\psi_{\zeta}:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}

be the finite order character induced by
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\mapsto\zeta
.
For
t>0
, let
\psi_{\zeta}
also denote the Dirichlet character of
({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{t+1}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
of
p
-power order such that the image of
\epsilon(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})\in 1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
maps to
\zeta
.
Let

\phi_{\zeta}:\Lambda\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\zeta]\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}

be the homomorphism such that
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\mapsto\zeta
.
There is also an extension of this to a homomorphism
\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
sending
g(T)\in\mathfrak{K}_{1,{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
to the value of the power series
g(T)

at
T=\epsilon(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})\zeta-1

3.4.2.
p
-adic L-functions

Let
\alpha
be root of
x^{2}-a_{g}(p)x+p
satisfying (
2.7
). Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
and
\gamma\in V
with
\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0
,
and let
\Omega^{\pm}_{\omega,\gamma}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be the periods defined by
per(\omega)=\Omega_{\omega,\gamma}^{+}\cdot\gamma^{+}+\Omega_{\omega,\gamma}^{-}\cdot\gamma^{-}
as in §
2.2.7
.
Then there exists a cyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-functions as follows (cf.
[
79
, Thm. 16.2]
).

Theorem 3.13
.

There exists an unique

\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\in\mathfrak{K}_{1,{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}

such that

\phi_{\zeta}(\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g))=e_{p}(\zeta)\cdot\frac{L(1,g\otimes\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}{\Omega_{\omega,\gamma}^{+}},\ \ \ e_{p}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\alpha^{-(t+1)}\cdot\frac{p^{t+1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}&if $\zeta\neq 1$\\
(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})^{2}&else.\end{cases}.

Moreover, if
g
is ordinary,
\omega
is good, and
\gamma\in T
, then

\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}.

These
p
-adic
L
-functions are related to the Beilinson–Kato elements via the Coleman maps
[
79
, Thm. 16.6]
):

Theorem 3.14
.

Let
\alpha
be root of
x^{2}-a_{g}(p)x+p
satisfying (
2.7
). We have

Col_{\eta_{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(g)))=\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)

In other words,
L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)=\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)
.

Remark 3.15
.

This theorem summarizes the relation between the Beilinson–Kato elements and the
L
-values
L(1,g,\chi)

for
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbb{Q}},p}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
. In particular, the non-vanishing of
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)
,
and hence of
L_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)
by Theorem
3.14
, is an obvious consequence of Theorem
2.9

(due to Rohrlich).

Supersingular case.
Suppose
g
is supersingular. In this case,
there are two
signed

p
-adic
L
-functions
[
114
]
.

Theorem 3.16
.

Suppose
a_{p}(g)=0
. Let
\circ=\pm
.
There exists an unique

\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma}^{\circ}(g)\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
,
such that

\phi_{\zeta}(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma}^{\circ}(g))=e_{p}^{\circ}(\zeta)\cdot\frac{L(1,g\otimes\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}{\Omega_{\omega,\gamma}^{+}}

for

e_{p}^{+}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{\frac{t+2}{2}}\cdot\frac{p^{t+1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}\cdot\prod_{\text{odd }m=1}^{t-1}\Phi_{p^{m}}(\zeta)^{-1}&if $t>0$ even\\
2&if $t=0$.\end{cases}

and

e_{p}^{-}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{\frac{t+1}{2}}\cdot\frac{p^{t+1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}\cdot\prod_{\text{even }m=2}^{t-1}\Phi_{p^{m}}(\zeta)^{-1}&if $t>0$ odd\\
p-1&if $t=0$.\end{cases}

Here
\Phi_{p^{n}}(X)
is the
p^{n}
th cyclotomic polynomial.

These
p
-adic
L
-functions are also related to the Beilinson–Kato classes
[
92
, §3.3.1]
:

Theorem 3.17
.

We have

Col_{\omega}^{\circ}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(g)))=\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma}^{\circ}(g).

In other words,
L^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)=\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma}^{\circ}(g)
.
Furthermore, if (irr
Q
) holds,
\omega
is good, and
\gamma\in T
, then
\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma}^{\circ}(g)\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
.

The conclusion that
\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma}^{\circ}(g)\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
when (irr
Q
) holds,
\omega
is good, and
\gamma\in T

is just a consequence of the displayed relation together with property (iii) of the Beilinson–Kato elements and the
integrality of the image of
Col^{\circ}_{\omega}
when
\omega
is good (cf. §
2.4.2
).

Remark 3.18
.

The non-vanishing of
\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma}^{\circ}(g)
,
and hence of
L^{\circ}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)
by Theorem
3.17
, is also an obvious consequence of Theorem
2.9
.

3.4.3.
p
-adic L-functions over
L

Let
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
. We choose
\alpha_{g^{\prime}}=\alpha_{g}=\alpha
to satisfy (
2.7
).
For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F,g}
, let
\omega^{\prime}\in S_{F,g^{\prime}}
be the image of
\omega
under the fixed isomorphism (
2.23
),
as in §
2.5.5
. Let
\gamma\in V=V_{g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}=V_{g^{\prime}}
.
To the quadruple
(\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})
we associate a
p
-adic
L
-function:

\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})=\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\alpha)}\otimes_{\Lambda,\iota_{\epsilon}}\Lambda.

If
g
is ordinary, then
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
belongs to
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
,
and even to
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
if (irr
Q
) holds,
\omega
is good ,and
\gamma\in T_{g}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime}}
.

If
g
is supersingular, then we similarly, define

\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})=\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma}(g)\cdot\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\omega^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}.

If (irr
Q
) holds,
\omega
is good ,and
\gamma\in T_{g}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime}}
, then
\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
.

Combining Theorems
3.14
and
3.17
with the definitions of the Beilinson–Kato elements
\mathbf{z}^{\Box}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})

easily yields the following:

Theorem 3.19
.

(a)

If
g
is ordinary, then

Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}({\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})))=\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L}).

(b)

If
g
is supersingular, then

Col^{\circ}_{{\omega},v}({\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(\mathbf{z}^{\circ}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})))=\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L}).

Remark 3.20
.

In view of Theorem
3.13
and Theorem
3.16
, the cyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-functions

\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
and
\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}(g_{/L})

are characterised by an analogous interpolation property with respect to the
L
-values
L(1,g,\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})L(1,g,\chi_{L}\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})
.
It can be thus viewed as a cyclotomic
p
-adic L-function for the base change of the newform
g
to the imaginary quadratic field
L
.

Remark 3.21
.

The cyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-functions
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
and

\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}(g_{/L})
are non-zero since the factors in their definition are non-zero
(see Remarks
3.15
and
3.18
).

4.
CM Hida families

In this section we study the
\Lambda
-adic Tate-module associated with certain Hida families of CM newforms.

4.1.
The families

We define the CM Hida families of interest after a few preliminaries.

4.1.1.
Imaginary quadratic fields, again

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field as in §
2.5.1
. Let
c\in{\mathrm{Gal}}(L/{\mathbb{Q}})
be the non-trivial automorphism.
Let
w_{L}
be the number of roots of unity in
L
and let
h_{L}
be the class number of
L
.
Let
N_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}}:L^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
be the norm map.
Let
{\mathbb{A}}
(resp.
{\mathbb{A}}^{(\infty)}
) be the adeles (resp. finite adeles) over
{\mathbb{Q}}
, and let
{\mathbb{A}}_{L}={\mathbb{A}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}}L
(resp.
{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{(\infty)}={\mathbb{A}}^{(\infty)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}L
) be
the adeles (resp. finite adeles) over
L
.
For a place
w
of
L
, let
\varpi_{w}\in L_{w}^{\times}
be a uniformiser.
Let
{\mathrm{rec}}_{L_{w}}:L_{w}^{\times}\rightarrow G_{L_{w}}^{{\mathrm{ab}}}
be the reciprocity map of local class field theory normalised so that uniformisers map to lifts of the arithmetic Frobenius
{\mathrm{Frob}}_{w}
.
Similarly, let
{\mathrm{rec}}_{L}:{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{\times}/L^{\times}\rightarrow G_{L}^{{\mathrm{ab}}}
be the reciprocity map of global class field theory normalised so that
{\mathrm{rec}}_{L}|_{L_{w}^{\times}}={\mathrm{rec}}_{L_{w}}
.
For
x\in{\mathbb{A}}_{L}
and
\ell
a prime of
{\mathbb{Q}}
, let
x_{\ell}=(x_{w})_{w\mid\ell}
be the
\ell
-component.
For each non-zero fractional ideal
\mathfrak{a}
of
L
, let
x_{\mathfrak{a}}\in({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{(\infty)})^{\times}
be a finite idéle so that
{\mathrm{ord}}_{w}(x_{\mathfrak{a},w})={\mathrm{ord}}_{w}(\mathfrak{a})
for each finite place

w
with
{\mathrm{ord}}_{w}(\mathfrak{a})\neq 0
and
x_{\mathfrak{a},w}=1
for all other places
w
.

4.1.2.
Hecke characters

Let
\psi:{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{\times}/L^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be an algebraic Hecke character over
L
.
By this we mean that there is a pair of integers
(m,n)
such that the restriction of
\psi
to the identity component of
(L\otimes{\mathbb{R}})^{\times}

is given by the composition
\rho_{\infty}
of the algebraic character
\rho:(L\otimes{\mathbb{R}})^{\times}\rightarrow(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes{\mathbb{R}})^{\times}
,
\rho(x\otimes r)=(x\otimes r)^{m}(\bar{x}\otimes r)^{n}
for
\bar{x}=c(x)
,
with the homomorphism
(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes{\mathbb{R}})^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
induced by
\iota_{\infty}
.
We call the pair
(m,n)
the infinity type of
\psi
.
The values of the character

{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},\text{ $\alpha\mapsto\rho_{\infty}(\alpha_{\infty})^{-1}\psi(\alpha)$},

generate a number field
F_{\psi}
. In what follows, we regard
F_{\psi}\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
via the embedding
\iota_{\infty}
.
Let

\psi^{c}=\psi\circ c
be the conjugate Hecke character. The infinity type of

\psi^{c}
is
(n,m)
.

Let
\rho_{p}
be the composition of the algebraic character
\rho:(L\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{\times}\rightarrow(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{\times}
,
\rho(x\otimes r)=(x\otimes r)^{m}(\bar{x}\otimes r)^{n}
, with the homomorphism
(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{\times}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
induced by
\iota_{p}
.
The
p
-adic Galois character associated to
\psi
is given by

\rho_{\psi}:G_{L}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times},\text{ $\rho_{\psi}(\sigma)=\rho_{p}(\alpha_{p})\rho_{\infty}(\alpha_{\infty})^{-1}\psi(\alpha)$ for $\sigma={\mathrm{rec}}_{L}(\alpha)$}.

Here we view
\rho_{\infty}(\alpha_{\infty})^{-1}\psi(\alpha)
as belonging to
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
by regarding
F_{\psi}

as a subfield of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
via the embedding
\iota_{p}
.
This representation is Hodge–Tate at each place
w\mid p
of
L
: the Hodge-Tate weight
9
9

9

We adopt geometric conventions for Hodge–Tate weights. In particular, the Hodge–Tate weight of the

p
-adic cyclotomic character
\epsilon
is
-1
.

at
v
is
m
and the Hodge–Tate weight at
\bar{v}
is
n
.

Serre
[
125
]
proved that
\psi\mapsto\rho_{\psi}
is a bijection between the algebraic Hecke characters of
{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{\times}
and the
p
-adic Galois characters of
G_{L}
that are unramified outside a finite set of places and are Hodge–Tate at the places above
p
.

If
\psi
has infinity type
(m,n)
, then the restriction of
\psi
to
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{\times}
equals

\epsilon_{\psi}|\cdot|^{m+n}
for some finite order character
\epsilon_{\psi}
.

4.1.3.
CM modular forms

Let
\psi
be an algebraic Hecke character over
L
with infinity type
(1-k,0)
for some integer
k\geq 1
and conductor
\mathfrak{f}_{\psi}
.
Let
N_{\psi}=D_{L}N_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{f}_{\psi})
and let
\chi_{\psi}=\chi_{L}\epsilon_{\psi}
(which we view as a Dirichlet character modulo
N_{\psi}
).
Let
\theta_{\psi}\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N_{\psi}),\chi_{\psi})
be the corresponding CM modular form which has
q
-expansion

\theta_{\psi}(q)=\sum_{0\neq\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathcal{O}_{L},(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{f}_{\psi})=1}\psi(x_{\mathfrak{a}})q^{N_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{a})},

(4.1)

and so

L(s,\theta_{\psi})=L(s,\psi).

(4.2)

The Hecke field
F_{\theta_{\psi}}
is a subfield of
F_{\psi}
.

Suppose
(\mathfrak{f}_{\psi},p)=1
. Then
\theta_{\psi}
is
p
-ordinary:
The coefficient of
q^{p}
is
\psi_{v}(p)+\psi_{\bar{v}}(p)
and the valuation of
\psi_{v}(p)
(with respect to
\iota_{p}
) is
k-1
while the valuation of
\psi_{\bar{v}}(p)
is
0
. Note that if
k=1
, then the coefficient of
q^{p}
need not be a
p
-adic unit, but the Hecke polynomial at
p
has a
p
-adic unit root at the prime above
p
determined by
\iota_{p}
.

Let
\lambda\mid p
be the place of
F_{\psi}
determined by the embedding
\iota_{p}
.
The representation
\rho_{\psi}
takes values in
F_{\psi,\lambda}
.
If
V_{F_{\psi,\lambda},\theta_{\psi}}
is the
p
-adic Galois representation associated to

\theta_{\psi}
as in
[
79
, §§6.3,8.3,15.10]
(denoted
V_{F_{\psi,\lambda}}(\theta_{\psi})
in
op. cit.
), then

V_{F_{\psi,\lambda},\theta_{\psi}}^{\vee}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{G_{L}}(\rho_{\psi}).

(4.3)

4.1.4.
Some
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extensions of
L

Let
L_{\infty}
be the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}
-extension of
L
. Let
L_{\infty}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}\subset L_{\infty}
(resp.
L_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ac}}\subset L_{\infty}
) be the cyclotomic (resp. anticyclotomic)
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
L
.
For a prime
w\mid p
, let
L_{\infty}^{w}\subset L_{\infty}
be the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension unramified away from
w
.
For
\Box=\emptyset
,
{\mathrm{cyc}}
,
\mathrm{ac}
, or
w
, let
\Gamma_{L}^{\Box}={\mathrm{Gal}}(L_{\infty}^{\Box}/L)
.
Then
\Gamma_{L}=\Gamma_{L}^{\emptyset}\cong{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}
and

\Gamma_{L}^{\Box}\cong{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
if
\Box\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\overline{\Gamma}_{L}^{w}\subset\Gamma_{L}^{w}
be the image of
G_{L_{w}}\subset G_{L}

under the canonical projection
G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{w}
. Let
h_{p}\geq 0
be the integer such that

\overline{\Gamma}_{L}^{w}=(\Gamma_{L}^{w})^{p^{h_{p}}}
.
If
p\nmid h_{L}
, then
h_{p}=0
.

The group
{\mathrm{Gal}}(L/{\mathbb{Q}})=\{c,1\}
acts on
\Gamma_{L}
via conjugation by any lift of
c
to
{\mathrm{Gal}}(L_{\infty}/{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Let
\Gamma_{L}^{\pm}\subset\Gamma_{L}
be the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-summand of
rank one on which this action is just multiplication by
\pm 1
. The subgroup
\Gamma_{L}^{+}
(resp.
\Gamma_{L}^{-}
) is mapped
isomorphically onto
\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
(resp.
\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}
) via the canonical projection from
\Gamma_{L}
.

Let
\gamma_{\Box}\in\Gamma_{L}^{\Box}
,
\Box=\pm
,
{\mathrm{cyc}}
,
\mathrm{ac}
, or
w
, be a topological generator.
In light of the canonical isomorphisms
\Gamma_{L}^{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{L}^{-}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}
we can and do choose these so that
\gamma_{+}\in\Gamma_{L}^{+}

and
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{L}
are
identified with the previously chosen
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\in\Gamma
,
\gamma_{-}
is identified with
\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}
,

c\gamma_{v}c^{-1}=\gamma_{\bar{v}}
,
\gamma_{+}
maps to
\gamma_{w}^{p^{h_{p}}/2}
(
w=v,\bar{v}
), and

\gamma_{-}
maps to
\gamma_{v}^{1/2}
and to
\gamma_{\bar{v}}^{-1/2}
.
Note that
\gamma_{w}^{p^{h_{p}}}
is a topological generator of
\overline{\Gamma}_{L}^{w}
.

4.1.5.
More Iwasawa algebras

For
\Box=\emptyset
,
\pm
,
{\mathrm{cyc}}
,
\mathrm{ac}
, or
w
, let

\Lambda^{\Box}_{L}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\![\Gamma^{\Box}_{L}]\!].

Similarly, for a
p
-adically complete
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra
R
let
\Lambda_{L,R}^{\Box}=R[\![\Gamma_{L}^{\Box}]\!]
.

For
\Box\neq\pm
, let

\Psi_{L}^{\Box}:G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{\Box}

be the canonical projection. We view these as
(\Lambda^{\Box}_{L})^{\times}
-valued characters of
G_{L}
(the canonical characters).
The canonical projections
\Gamma_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{\Box}
induce ring homomorphisms
\Lambda_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{\Box}
.
Similarly, the canonical projection
\Gamma_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma
induces a homomorphism
\Lambda_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda
.
The canonical projection
\Gamma^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma
is an isomorphism, inducing an identification
\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda
;
the projection
\Lambda_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda
factors through this isomorphism.
Canonical characters map to canonical characters under
all these ring homomorphisms.

There are identifications

\Lambda_{L,R}^{\Box}\xrightarrow{\sim}R[\![T_{\Box}]\!],\ \ \gamma_{\Box}\mapsto 1+T_{\Box}.

Under these, the canonical
identification of
\Lambda_{L,R}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
with
\Lambda_{R}
becomes the isomorphism
R[\![T_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]\simeq R[\![T]\!]
,
T_{\mathrm{cyc}}\mapsto T
.

Let
(\Box,\Box^{\prime})=({\mathrm{cyc}},w)
or
({\mathrm{cyc}},\mathrm{ac})
. Then the canonical projections induce
\Gamma_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{L}^{\Box}\times\Gamma_{L}^{\Box^{\prime}}
,
and hence an isomorphism of rings

\Lambda_{L,R}\simeq\Lambda^{\Box}_{L,R}\hat{\otimes}_{R}\Lambda^{\Box^{\prime}}_{L,R}.

(4.4)

These induce isomorphisms

\Lambda_{L,R}\simeq R[\![T_{\mathrm{cyc}},T_{\mathrm{ac}}]\!]\ \ \text{and}\ \ \Lambda_{L,R}\simeq R[\![T_{\mathrm{cyc}},T_{w}]\!].

Note that with respect to the first (resp. second) of these isomorphisms, the
projection
\Lambda_{L,R}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{R}
is just the map

T_{\mathrm{cyc}}\mapsto T
,
T_{\mathrm{ac}}\mapsto 0

(resp.
T_{w}\mapsto 0
) on power series rings.

The inclusion

\Gamma_{L}^{+}\times\Gamma_{L}^{-}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{L}

induces an isomorphism

\Lambda_{L,R}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{R}\Lambda_{L,R}^{-}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{L}

and hence an isomorphism

R[\![T_{+},T_{-}]\!]\simeq\Lambda_{L,R}.

The isomorphism
\Lambda_{L,R}\simeq R[\![T_{\mathrm{cyc}},T_{\mathrm{ac}}]\!]
is then the map

T_{+}\mapsto T_{\mathrm{cyc}}
,
T_{-}\mapsto T_{\mathrm{ac}}
on power series rings.
Similarly, the isomorphism
\Lambda_{L,R}\simeq R[\![T_{\mathrm{cyc}},T_{v}]\!]
is the map

T_{+}\mapsto(1+T_{\mathrm{cyc}})(1+T_{v})^{p^{h_{p}}/2}-1
,
1+T_{-}\mapsto(1+T_{v})^{1/2}
.

4.1.6.
A
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-family of
CM
forms

Let

\Theta_{v}:{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{\times}\rightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}

(4.5)

be the composition of the reciprocity map
{\mathrm{rec}}_{L}:{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{\times}\rightarrow G_{L}^{{\mathrm{ab}}}
with the canonical projection
G_{L}^{{\mathrm{ab}}}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}
.
Let
{\bf{h}}_{v}
be the
family of CM forms associated to
\Theta_{v}
, with
q
-expansion given by

{\bf{h}}_{v}(q)=\sum_{n\geq 1}{\bf{b}}(n)q^{n}\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}[\![q]\!],\ \ {\bf{b}}(n)=\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathcal{O}_{L},{v}\nmid\mathfrak{a},N_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{a})=n}}\Theta_{v}(x_{\mathfrak{a}}).

We refer to
{\bf{h}}_{v}
as the canonical CM family with CM by
L
.

For a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra homomorphism
\psi:\Lambda_{L}^{v}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}

such that
\psi(\gamma_{v}^{h_{p}})=\epsilon(\gamma_{+})^{m}
with
m\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ {p-1}
,
m\geq 0
,
the specialisation
h_{v,\psi}
with the
q
-expansion

h_{v,\psi}(q)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\psi({\bf{b}}(n))q^{n}

is the
p
-ordinary stabilisation of a CM newform of weight
k=m+1
and level
\Gamma_{1}(D_{L})
.
Indeed,
\psi|_{\Gamma_{L}^{v}}
– viewed as a character of
G_{L}
– is visibly unramified outside
p
and has Hodge–Tate weight
-m=1-k
(resp.
0
) at
v
(resp.
\bar{v}
). The corresponding algebraic Hecke character over
L
is readily seen to be unramified with infinity type
(1-k,0)
. Occasionally we also denote this algebraic Hecke character by
\psi
as well.
It follows that
{\bf{h}}_{v}
is a
p
-ordinary
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-adic modular form of tame level
D_{L}
(see Section
4.2.2
below).

4.2.
The Tate lattices

We analyse certain lattices in the Galois representation associated with the CM family
{\bf{h}}_{v}
.

4.2.1.
The Hida–Iwasawa algebras
\Lambda_{D}
and
\Lambda_{0}

Let

\Lambda_{D}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\![{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}]\!]\ \ \text{and}\ \ \Lambda_{0}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\![1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}]\!].

Since
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}=\mu_{p-1}\times 1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, there is a canonical identification

\Lambda_{D}=\Lambda_{0}[\mu_{p-1}]
. Then
\Lambda_{D}=\oplus_{i=0}^{p-2}\Lambda_{D}^{(i)}
, where

\Lambda_{D}^{(i)}
is the direct summand on which multiplication by the group element
[\zeta]\in\mu_{p-1}

acts as
\zeta^{i}
. In particular,
\Lambda_{D}^{(i)}=\delta_{i}\cdot\Lambda_{D}=\Lambda_{0}\cdot\delta_{i}
, where

\delta_{i}=\frac{1}{(p-1)}\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p-1}}\zeta^{-i}[\zeta]\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\mu_{p-1}]
.

We fix the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-isomorphism
\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{D}
sending
g\in{\mathcal{G}}
to
[\epsilon(g)]\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\subset\Lambda_{D}
.
This identifies
\Lambda
with
\Lambda_{0}
(via
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\mapsto[\epsilon(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})]
).
This also identifies the idempotent
\varepsilon_{i}\in\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}
with
\delta_{i}\in\Lambda_{D}
.
Via this identification the character
\Psi_{D}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\Lambda_{D}
,
g\mapsto[\epsilon(g)]\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
is
identified with the canonical character
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}
.

4.2.2.
Hida’s Hecke algebras

Let
M
be a positive integer such that
p\nmid M
.
Let

{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}=\varprojlim_{r}{\mathbb{T}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})}^{{}^{\prime}{\mathrm{ord}}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}=\varprojlim_{r}{\mathbb{H}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})}^{{}^{\prime}{\mathrm{ord}}}

be Hida’s ordinary Hecke algebras, where
{\mathbb{T}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})}^{{}^{\prime}{\mathrm{ord}}}
(resp.
{\mathbb{H}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})}^{{}^{\prime}{\mathrm{ord}}}
) is the
p
-ordinary direct summand of the Hecke algebra

{\mathbb{T}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}^{\prime}
(resp.
{\mathbb{H}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})}^{\prime}
)
acting on cuspforms (resp. on modular forms).
Recall that there is a Hecke operator
U_{p}^{\prime}
in both
{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}

[
85
, Def. 2.4.3]
. The operator
U_{p}^{\prime}
projects to the
T_{p}^{\prime}
-operator in
{\mathbb{T}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})}^{{}^{\prime}{\mathrm{ord}}}

and
{\mathbb{H}}_{\Gamma_{1}(Mp^{r})}^{{}^{\prime}{\mathrm{ord}}}
.
Both
{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
are
\Lambda_{D}
-algebras, with the group element
[a]\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\subset\Lambda_{D}^{\times}
acting
on modular forms of level
Np^{r}
via the inverse of the diamond operator
\langle a^{\prime}\rangle
for
a^{\prime}=(a\ \mathrm{mod}\ p^{r},1)\in({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathbb{Z}}/M{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.
The inclusion of the spaces of cuspforms in the spaces of modular forms induces a surjection
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}

of
\Lambda_{D}
-algebras.
Moreover,
{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
are finite, free
\Lambda_{0}
-modules.

Let
M_{\Lambda_{D}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
be the space of
p
-ordinary
\Lambda_{D}
-adic modular forms over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
with tame level
M
,
and let
S_{\Lambda_{D}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}\subset M_{\Lambda_{D}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
be the subspace of
\Lambda_{D}
-adic cuspforms
[
85
, §7.4]
.
The former is an
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
-module and the latter a
{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
-module.
There is a canonical duality

{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\Lambda_{D}}({\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}},\Lambda_{D})\simeq S_{\Lambda_{D}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}

(4.6)

of
{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
-modules
[
107
, Thm. 2.5.3]
.

The canonical CM family
\bf{h}_{v}
belongs to the space
S_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}^{\mathrm{ord}}=S_{\Lambda_{D}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{D}}\Lambda_{L}^{v}
for
M=D_{L}
. Here we view
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
as a
\Lambda_{D}
-algebra via the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-homomorphism
\varphi_{v}:\Lambda_{D}\rightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{v}
sending

[\epsilon(\tilde{\gamma})]
to
\epsilon(\tilde{\gamma})^{-1}\gamma_{v}
for all all lifts
\tilde{\gamma}\in G_{L}
of
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{cyc}}
.
Note that
\varphi_{v}
factors through the projection
\Lambda_{D}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{D}^{(-1)}=\Lambda_{0}\cdot\delta^{(-1)}
.

4.2.3.
The
\Lambda_{D}
-adic Tate modules: generalities

Let

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})=\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(X_{1}(Mp^{r})_{/\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1))\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})=\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Y_{1}(Mp^{r})_{/\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1))

be the
\Lambda_{D}
-adic Tate modules.
Here
the subscript ‘
{\mathrm{ord}}
’ refers to the
p
-ordinary direct summand of the first étale cohomology arising from the dual
p
-ordinary idempotent (cf.
[
85
, §7.2]
). Note that
H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})

(resp.
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})
) has a natural structure of a
{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-module (resp. a
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-module).
We recall some of the fundemental properties of these Tate modules
[
108
]
,
[
109
]
.

Theorem 4.1
.

There exists a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{+}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{-}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{+}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{-}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

of
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-modules,
where the top and bottom lines are short exact sequences, the second vertical arrow is induced by the canonical inclusions
H^{1}(X_{1}(Mp^{r}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1))\hookrightarrow H^{1}(Y_{1}(Mp^{r}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1))
, and
furthermore:

(i)

The
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-action on
\mathcal{F}^{+}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})=\mathcal{F}^{+}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})
is such that
I_{p}
acts via the
character
\epsilon\Psi_{D}
.
Moreover,

\mathcal{F}^{+}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})=\mathcal{F}^{+}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})\simeq{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}

as
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
-modules.

(ii)

The
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-action on the quotients
\mathcal{F}^{-}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})
and
\mathcal{F}^{-}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})
is unramified with an arithmetic Frobenius acting via the Hecke operator
U_{p}^{\prime}
. Moreover, there exist isomorphisms

\mathcal{F}^{-}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})\simeq S_{\Lambda_{D}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mathcal{F}^{-}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(Mp^{\infty})\simeq M_{\Lambda_{D}}

of
{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
-modules and
{\mathbb{H}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
-modules, respectively, such that the third vertical arrow corresponds to the inclusion
S_{\Lambda_{D}}\hookrightarrow M_{\Lambda_{D}}
.

For
p\geq 5
, this theorem is explained in
[
52
, §§1.7-1.8]
. It is also proved in
[
108
,
109
]
(but see the remarks in
[
52
, §§1.7.15-16]
). See also
[
85
, Thm. 7.2.3]
. The arguments in these papers likely apply to the
p=3
case as well. The results of
[
30
]
explicitly covers some parts of the
p=3
cases. An alternate proof that also includes the
p=3
case is included in
[
124
]
.

From (a twisted) Poincare duality it is possible to define a perfect
\Lambda_{D}
-duality for
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Mp^{\infty})
:

Theorem 4.2
.

There exists a perfect pairing

(\cdot,\cdot):H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Mp^{\infty})\times H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Mp^{\infty})\rightarrow\Lambda_{D}

of
\Lambda_{D}
-modules satisfying
(t\cdot x,y)=(x,t\cdot y)
for all
x,y\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Mp^{\infty})
and
t\in{\mathbb{T}}_{Mp^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
.
Furthermore, the pairing
(\cdot,\cdot)
induces isomorphisms

{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\Lambda_{D}}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Mp^{\infty})^{\pm},\Lambda_{D})\simeq H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Mp^{\infty})^{\mp}

as
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{Mp^{\infty}}
-modules,
where the superscript
\pm
denotes the submodule on which the action of complex conjugation is by
\pm 1
.

The existence of the perfect pairing
(\cdot,\cdot)
is explained in
[
52
, §1.6]
, see especially
[
52
, §1.6.5]
. The consequence for the
\pm
-subspaces for the action of
c
follows from
[
52
, §1.6.3(3)]
(see also
[
85
, Thm. 7.2.3(v)]
).

4.2.4.
The Tate lattices for
{\bf h}_{v}

Let
\varphi:{\mathbb{T}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}\rightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{v}
be the
\Lambda_{D}
-homomorphism corresponding to the canonical CM family
{\bf{h}}_{v}
(which corresponds to a branch of a Hida family in the sense of
[
85
, §7]
). This extends the homomorphism
\varphi_{v}:\Lambda_{D}\rightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{v}
,
and it induces a
\Lambda_{D}
-homomorphism
{\mathbb{H}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}\rightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{v}
by composition with the canonical
\Lambda_{D}
-surjection

{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
. We continue to write
\varphi
for this last homomorphism.

The Tate lattices associated with the canonical CM family
{\bf{h}}_{v}
are the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules

{\mathbb{T}}=H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{T}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}},\varphi}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathbb{H}}={\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{H}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}},\varphi}\Lambda_{L}^{v}.

We similarly define the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-modules

{\mathbb{T}}^{\pm}=\mathcal{F}^{\pm}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{T}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}},\varphi}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathbb{H}}^{\pm}=\mathcal{F}^{\pm}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{H}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}},\varphi}\Lambda_{L}^{v}.

By Theorem
4.1
there is a commutative diagram
of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}}^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{H}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{H}}^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

(4.7)

with exact rows.
Furthermore,

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module of rank one.

(4.8)

Let
{\bf{F}}={\mathrm{Frac}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})
be the field of fractions of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
and let

{\mathbb{V}}={\mathbb{T}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\bf{F}}={\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\bf{F}}.

Comparing the traces of Frobenius elements

{\mathrm{Frob}}_{\ell}
,
\ell\nmid D_{L}p
,
shows that there is an isomorphism

{\mathbb{V}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}({\bf{F}}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))

(4.9)

of
{\bf{F}}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules.
In particular,
{\mathbb{T}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\bf{F}}
is an irreducible
{\bf{F}}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module, and the
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-action factors through
{\mathrm{Gal}}(L_{\infty}/{\mathbb{Q}})
.

Remark 4.3
.

The CM family
\bf{h}_{v}
corresponds to a branch of a Hida family

\bf{h}
in the sense of
[
85
, §7]
. In particular,
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
equals
\Lambda_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}
(in the notation
of
op. cit.
) and the module
{\mathbb{H}}
(resp.
{\mathbb{T}}
) then equals

M({\bf h})^{*}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\bf{h}}}\Lambda_{{\bf h}_{v}}

(resp.
M({\bf h})^{*}_{\mathrm{par}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\bf{h}}}\Lambda_{{\bf h}_{v}}
).
It follows that for
\psi:\Lambda_{L}^{v}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
as in §
4.1.6
,

{\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v},\psi}F_{h_{v,\psi},\lambda}\cong M_{F_{h_{v},\psi},\lambda}(h_{v,\psi})^{*}
,
where the right-hand side is as in
[
85
, §2.8]
. Comparing the normalizations in
[
85
, §2.8]
and
[
79
, §§6.3,8.3,15.10]

shows that
M_{F_{h_{v},\psi},\lambda}(h_{v,\psi})^{*}\cong V_{h_{v,\psi},\lambda}^{\vee}\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\psi\circ\Psi_{L}^{v})
, which implies (
4.9
).

The
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules
{\mathbb{T}}
and
{\mathbb{H}}
may not be torsion-free, so we let
