{\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{T}}_{{\mathrm{tor}}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathbb{H}}_{1}={\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{H}}_{{\mathrm{tor}}}.

These are just the respective images of
{\mathbb{T}}
and
{\mathbb{H}}
in
{\mathbb{T}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}\bf{F}
and
{\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}\bf{F}
. In particular,

{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\subseteq{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\subset{\mathbb{V}}.

Both
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
are torsion-free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank two (but not obviously free).
Let
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
be the quotient of
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
by the image of
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
, and let
{\mathbb{H}}^{-}
be the analogous quotient of
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
.
There are exact sequences

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\rightarrow{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}\rightarrow 0\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathbb{H}}^{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\rightarrow{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\rightarrow 0

of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-modules. It is not a priori clear that either
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
or
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}
is torsion-free.

Lemma 4.4
.

(i)

The
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-module morphisms
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
are non-zero and thus injective.

(ii)

The submodules
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}
are
G_{L}
-stable with
G_{L}
acting via
\Psi_{L}^{v}
.

(iii)

The action of
G_{L_{v}}
on the quotients
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}
is via
\Psi_{L}^{v,c}
.
Moreover, for
\sigma\in G_{L_{v}}
and
t\in{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
h\in{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
,

\sigma\cdot t-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\sigma)t\in{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \sigma\cdot h-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\sigma)h\in{\mathbb{H}}^{+}.

Proof.

Recall that
G_{L_{v}}=G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\subset G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
, so
I_{v}=I_{p}
.
It then follows from Theorem
4.1
(i) that
I_{v}
acts trivially on
{\mathbb{T}}^{-}
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{-}
. It follows from Theorem
4.1
(ii) and
the definitions of
\Psi_{L}^{v}
and the canonical CM family that
I_{v}
acts by
\Psi_{L}^{v}
on
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}
.
As it is also clear from (
4.9
) that
I_{v}
does not act trivially on all of
{\mathbb{V}}
,
the image of
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
in
{\mathbb{V}}
, and hence in
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
, must be non-zero. The same applies to the image of
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}
in
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
.
Injectivity then follows from (
4.8
). This proves part (i).

It follows from (
4.9
) that
{\mathbb{V}}
has a unique
\bf{F}
-submodule of rank one on which
G_{L}
acts via
\Psi_{L}^{v}
,
and the quotient of this module is unramified at
v
. It then follows from part (i) that
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}
are
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-lattices in this rank one submodule and hence necessarily

G_{L}
-stable (with
G_{L}
acting via
\Psi_{L}^{v}
). This is part (ii).

That
G_{L_{v}}
acts on
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}
via
\Psi_{L}^{v,c}
follows from Theorem
4.1
(i). Part (iii) follows from this.
∎

Questions.
It is natural to ask:

Does the top exact sequence of (
4.7
) split as an exact sequence of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
or
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-modules?

(Q1)

Are
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules (of rank two and one, respectively)?

(Q2)

Determining the answers to these questions occupies much of the rest of this section as well as the next.
As explained in the introduction, these answers lie at the heart of this paper (and many subsequent applications).
The answer to the analogous questions for CM Hida families satisfying a
p
-distinguished hypothesis is known to be ‘yes’ (see for example
[
92
, Thm. 3.4]
).

4.2.5.
Reflexive closure of the Tate lattices

Recall that for a finite
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module
M
, the reflexive closure
\widetilde{M}
of
M
is

\widetilde{M}=\bigcap_{P\in 0pt_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})}(M/M_{{\mathrm{tor}}})_{P}\subset M\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\bf{F}},

where
0pt_{1}(\cdot)
is the set of height one prime ideals
[
134
, Def. 23.1 & Lem. 23.18]
.
As
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
is a two-dimensional regular local ring,
\widetilde{M}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module.
There is a canonical morphism

\varphi_{M}:M\rightarrow\widetilde{M}

(4.10)

of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules. The kernel of
\varphi_{M}
is the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-torsion submodule
M_{\mathrm{tor}}
, and the cokernel is a pseudo-null
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module
(that is, its localization at any
P\in 0pt_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})
is zero). In particular, the cokernel has finite order.

Since
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
are torsion-free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank two, it follows that

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \widetilde{\mathbb{H}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{1},\ \text{and these are free $\Lambda_{L}^{v}$-modules of rank two.}

We also have

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{+}=\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{-}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{1}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}=\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}
are free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank one.

Note that
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
are
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L}]
-modules, with the
G_{L}
-action arising from that on
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
, respectively.
Similarly,
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
are
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L_{v}}]
-modules; clearly, both
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}
are isomorphic to
\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v,c})
.
The induced sequences
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}\rightarrow 0

and
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\rightarrow 0
of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L_{v}}]
-modules need not be exact.
However, we do have the following.

Lemma 4.5
.

For
t\in\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
,
h\in\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
, and
\sigma\in G_{L_{v}}
, we have

\sigma\cdot t-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\sigma)t\in\bigcap_{P\in 0pt_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}_{P}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}^{+}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \sigma\cdot h-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\sigma)h\in\bigcap_{P\in 0pt_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}_{P}=\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
4.4
(iii).

Lemma 4.6
.

For
P\in 0pt_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})
with
P\neq(T_{v})
, there is an isomorphism

\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{P}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))

of
\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules.

Here
T_{v}\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}
is the variable as in §
4.1.5
, and so
(T_{v})
is the kernel of the augmentation map
\Lambda_{L}^{v}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

Proof.

The claim in the lemma is equivalent to
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\simeq\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\oplus\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v,c})
as
\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}[G_{L}]
-modules.
Since this splitting holds over
\bf{F}
by (
4.9
), it also holds over
\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}
if
\Psi_{L}^{v}\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ \Psi_{L}^{v,c}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {P}
.

Let
\chi_{P}=\Psi_{L}^{v}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {P}
. If
\chi_{P}=\chi_{P}^{c}
, then the fixed field of the kernel of
\chi_{P}
is contained in
L^{v}\cap L^{\mathrm{cyc}}=L
. That is,
\chi_{P}
is trivial, and
so
P=(T_{v})
.
∎

More questions.
It is natural to ask:

Is
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
?

(Q3)

Or even

Is
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{+})
?

(Q4)

Answering
these questions is key to our answering questions (
Q1
) and (
Q2
) (and to subsequent applications).

4.2.6.
Saturation of
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}

A priori, the
G_{L}
-stable
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-submodule
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}
might not be saturated in
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
or
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
.
Let

{\mathbb{T}}_{v}=({\mathbb{T}}^{+}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\bf{F}})\cap\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathbb{H}}_{v}=({\mathbb{H}}^{+}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\bf{F}})\cap\widetilde{{\mathbb{H}}}

(4.11)

be their respective saturations.
Note that it is not a priori clear that
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
equals
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
.
Note also that
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
are
G_{L}
-stable submodules and that the
G_{L}
-action is via
\Psi_{L}^{v}
.
Let also

{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}}=c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{v}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{T}}_{v}.

We similarly define
{\mathbb{H}}_{\bar{v}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{v}
.
Both
{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{\bar{v}}
are
G_{L}
-stable submodules with
G_{L}
-action via
\Psi_{L}^{v,c}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

(i)

The submodules
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
are free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank one. In particular, both are isomorphic to
\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})
as

\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L}]
-modules.

(ii)

The submodule
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
(resp.
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
) is a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-summand of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
(resp,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
).
Consequently,
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{v}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{v}
are both free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank one, and both are isomorphic to

\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v,c})
as
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L}]
-modules.

(iii)

The image of
{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}}
(resp.
{\mathbb{H}}_{\bar{v}}
) in the quotient
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{v}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{v}
) is either

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{v}
or
T_{v}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{v}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{v}
or
T_{v}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{v}
).

Here again,
T_{v}\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}
is as in §
4.1.5
.

Proof.

Part (i) is clear if we can show that
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
are free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-submodules.
We have
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
. Since the quotient
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{v}/{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
is pseudo-null,
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
is contained in the saturation of
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
in
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
.
But
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
is saturated in
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
by definition. Hence
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
, and
so
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module. The same argument applies to
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
.

Let
\{e_{+},e_{-}\}\subset\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
be a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-basis such that

c\cdot e_{\pm}=\pm e_{\pm}.

Let
e_{v}
be a basis of
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
. Then
e_{\bar{v}}=c\cdot e_{v}
is a basis of the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module

{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}}=c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\subset\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}.

Note that by part (i),
{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v,c})
as a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L}]
-module.

There exist
a,b\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}
such that

e_{v}=ae_{+}+be_{-}\ \ \text{and}\ \ e_{\bar{v}}=c\cdot e_{v}=ae_{+}-be_{-}.

So for
\gamma\in G_{L}
, we have

(\gamma-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma))e^{+}=\frac{\Psi_{L}^{v}(\gamma)-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma)}{2a}\cdot e_{v}\ \ \text{and}\ \ (\gamma-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma))e^{-}=\frac{\Psi_{L}^{v}(\gamma)-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma)}{2b}\cdot e_{v}.

Since
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
is saturated, this means

a,b\mid(\Psi_{L}^{v}(\gamma)-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma))\ \text{for all}\ \gamma\in\Gamma_{L}.

We claim that the ideal
I=\langle\Psi_{L}^{v}(\gamma)-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma)\ :\ \gamma\in\Gamma_{L}\rangle\subset\Lambda_{L}^{v}
is just
(T_{v})
.
To see this, note that the character
\Psi_{L}^{v}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {I}
is just the composition

G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}\rightarrow(\Lambda_{L}^{v}/I)^{\times},

where the arrows are all the canonical projections. The kernel of this character contains
\Gamma^{-}
, so the fixed field of the kernel
is contained in
L_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\cap L_{\infty}^{v}=L
. This means that
I
must be the augmentation ideal
(T_{v})
of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
.

It then follows that
a,b\mid T_{v}
. On the other hand, since
e_{v}
is a basis of a saturated module,
a
and
b
cannot have a common divisor.
So at least one of
a
and
b
is a unit in
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
. This implies that
e_{v}
has non-trivial image in
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/\mathfrak{m}_{L}^{v}\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
,
where
\mathfrak{m}_{L}^{v}\subset\Lambda_{L}^{v}
is the maximal ideal, which implies part (ii) for
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
.

If both
a
and
b
are units, then
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/({\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}})=0
. While if only one of
a
and
b
is a unit, then the other is a unit times
T_{v}
,
whence
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/({\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}})\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L}^{v}
. This, together with part (ii), implies part (iii) for
{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{v}}
.

The same arguments applied to
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
yield the claims in (ii) and (iii) for
{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{\bar{v}}
.
∎

Since
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}\subseteq{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\subseteq{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
, and these are all free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank one, we have

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}=C_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v},\ \ {\mathbb{T}}_{v}=D_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{H}}_{v},\ \ {\mathbb{H}}^{+}=C_{v}D_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{H}}_{v},\ \ \text{for some}\ \ C_{v},D_{v}\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}.

We will eventually show that
C_{v}
is a unit (that is,
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}_{v}
) and
D_{v}\mid T_{v}
. As a first step we have the following.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
p^{h_{p}}
be the index of
\overline{\Gamma}_{v}
in
\Gamma_{v}
as in §
4.1.4
. Then
C_{v}D_{v}\mid((1+T_{v})^{p^{h_{p}}}-1)
.

Proof.

Arguing as in the proof of part (ii) of Lemma
4.7
, but replacing
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
with
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
and and using that
\sigma\cdot h-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\sigma)h\in{\mathbb{H}}^{+}
for all

\sigma\in G_{L_{v}}
and
h\in\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
by Lemma
4.5
, we find that

C_{v}D_{v}\mid(\Psi_{L}^{v}(\gamma)-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma))\ \ \text{for all $\gamma\in G_{L_{v}}$}.

Let
J=\langle\Psi_{L}^{v}(\gamma)-\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(\gamma)\ :\ \gamma\in G_{L_{v}}\rangle\subset\Lambda_{L}^{v}
and consider
\Psi_{L}^{v}\ \mathrm{mod}\ J
, which is just the composite character

G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}\rightarrow(\Lambda_{L}^{v}/J)^{\times}.

Since
\Psi_{L}^{v,c}(I_{v})=1
, it follows that
\langle\Psi_{L}^{v}(\gamma)-1\ :\ \gamma\in I_{v}\rangle\subset J
.
So the fixed field
L_{v}
of the kernel of
\Psi_{L}^{v}\ \mathrm{mod}\ J
is unramified at both
v
and
\bar{v}
, and so contained in
L_{\infty}^{v}\cap L_{\infty}^{\bar{v}}\subset L_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ac}}
.
It follows that for all
\gamma\in G_{L_{v}}
,
\gamma
equals
c\gamma c^{-1}
on
L_{v}
(by definition of
J
) and equals
c\gamma^{-1}c^{-1}
on
L_{v}
(since
L_{v}\subset L_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ac}}
).
Hence
G_{L_{v}}
fixes
L_{v}
. That is,
J
contains the ideal
\langle\gamma-1\ :\ \gamma\in\overline{\Gamma}_{v}\rangle=((1+T_{v})^{p^{h_{p}}}-1)
, from which the desired divisibility follows.
∎

To prove that
D_{v}\mid T_{v}
and
C_{v}
is a unit, we make use of some of the results proved in
[
15
]
and
[
16
]
.

4.2.7.
Some consequences of
[
15
]
and
[
16
]

Let
P\in 0pt_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})
be a height one prime dividing
(1+T_{v})^{p^{h_{p}}}-1
.
Then
P=(\Phi_{p^{r}}(1+T_{v}))
for some
0\leq r\leq h_{p}
, where
\Phi_{p^{r}}(X)
is the
p^{r}
th cyclotomic polynomial.
Let
\zeta_{p^{r}}
be a
p^{r}
th root of unity. Let
P_{r}\subset\Lambda_{L}^{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
be the prime above
P
that is the kernel of the homomorphism

\lambda_{r}:\Lambda_{L}^{v}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
,
T_{v}\mapsto(\zeta_{p^{r}}-1)
.
Note that
P_{r}\cap(\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
is just the prime
(X)
, and that the natural maps induce isomorphisms

\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]={(\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{(X)}\xrightarrow{\sim}{(\Lambda_{L}^{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{P_{r}},

where the superscript ’
\wedge
’ denotes completion.

Let
Q\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
be the kernel of the composition

\varphi_{r}:{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi\otimes id}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda_{r}}}{{\rightarrow}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}.

Note that the homomorphism
\varphi_{r}
factors through
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
(since
\varphi
factors through
{\mathbb{T}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
).
Moreover,
\varphi_{r}
gives the eigenvalues for the action of the Hecke operators on the specialization
g_{v,r}=g_{v,\lambda_{r}}
of the canonical CM family
\bf{h}_{v}
.
The specialization
g_{v,r}
is the
p
-stabilization of a weight one CM eigenform of level
D_{L}
.
If
r=0
,
g_{v,r}
is an Eisenstein series associated with
the pair of characters
1,\chi_{L}
. If
r\neq 0
,
g_{v,r}
is a CM cuspform associated to the Hecke character

\psi_{r}=\Psi_{L}^{v}\ \mathrm{mod}\ P_{r}
(note that
\psi_{r}^{c}=\psi^{-1}_{r}\neq\psi_{r}
).

Let

{\mathcal{R}}={({\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathcal{R}}^{0}={({\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q}.

These are
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
-algebras. Let also

M={(M_{\Lambda_{0}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q},\ \ \text{and}\ \ S={(S_{\Lambda_{0}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q}.

To apply results of
[
16
]
and
[
15
]
, we first explain the connection between these objects and those considered in
op. cit.

Suppose first that
r=0
. Then we take
N=D_{L}
and
\phi=\chi_{L}
in
[
16
]
.
The ring
{(\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{(X)}
(which is just
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
) is identified with the completion of the local ring of weight space
at the point corresponding to the character
1\times\chi_{L}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\times({\mathbb{Z}}/D_{L}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
, that is, the ring
\Lambda
of
[
16
, §3.4]
.
The ring
{\mathcal{R}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
)is just the ring denoted
{\mathcal{T}}^{\rm{full}}

(resp.
{\mathcal{T}}^{\rm{full}}_{\rm{cusp}}
) in
[
16
, §4.2]
(cf.
[
15
, §3.2]
). By
[
16
, Props. 4.4 & 5.5]
,
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\rm{full}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{\rm{cusp}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\rm{full}}_{\rm{cusp}}
,
so
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}={\mathcal{T}}_{\rm{cusp}}
.
The
{\mathcal{R}}
-module
M
is just the module
M^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}
of
[
16
, §4.2]
for
f=g_{v,0}
, and the
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-module
S
is just
S^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}
.
The canonical CM family
{\bf{h}}_{v}
belongs to
S
. There are two Hida–Eisenstein families
{\bf{E}}_{1},{\bf{E}}_{2}\in M
associated with the pair of characters
1,\chi_{L}
(denoted
\mathcal{E}_{1,\chi_{L}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\chi_{L},1}
in
[
16
]
).
From the results in
[
16
]
we then conclude the following.

Proposition 4.9
.

If
P=(T_{v})
, then

(i)

S=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cdot{\bf{h}}_{v}
,

(ii)

M=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cdot{\bf{h}}_{v}\oplus\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cdot\frac{{\bf{E}}_{1}-{\bf{h}}_{v}}{X}\oplus\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cdot\frac{{\bf{E}}_{2}-{\bf{h}}_{v}}{X}
.

(iii)

Let
I_{i}\subset{\mathcal{R}}
be the annihilator of
{\bf{E}}_{i}
. Then
I_{i}\cdot{\bf{h}}_{v}=X\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cdot{\bf{h}}_{v}
.

Proof.

Parts (i) and (ii) follow from
[
16
, Cor. 4.5 & Prop. 5.4(ii)]
.

Part (iii) follows from
[
16
, Prop. 4.2]
, after noting
that the analysis in
op. cit.
also applies with the Eisenstein family
\mathcal{E}_{\chi_{L},1}
replacing
\mathcal{E}_{1,\chi_{L}}
.
∎

Suppose now that
0<r\leq h_{p}
. We take
\psi=\psi_{r}
(the conductor is
\mathfrak{c}_{\psi}=(1)
)
and
f=g_{v,r}
in
[
15
]
. Again
{(\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{(X)}=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
is identified with the completion of the local
ring of weight space
{\mathcal{W}}
at the weight of
f
, that is, with the ring
\Lambda
as in
[
15
, §3.1]
.
The ring
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
is just the completion of the local ring of the full eigencurve
{\mathcal{E}}_{\rm{full}}
of tame level
N=D_{L}
at the point corresponding to
f
(cf. the first paragraph of

[
15
, §3.2]
), which we denote by
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{full}}
, adopting the superscript ‘full’ from
[
4
,
16
]
.
Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be the completion of the local ring of the eigencurve at the point corresponding to
f
.
The argument from
[
4
, §7]
is readily adapted to the case at hand to show that
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\rm{full}}
. That is,
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}={\mathcal{T}}
.

Proposition 4.10
.

If
P\neq(T_{v})
, then the minimal number of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-generators of
S
is at most
3
.

Proof.

We first note that
S\simeq{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}({\mathcal{R}}^{0},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])

as
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-modules. This follows from the duality (
4.6
). In particular, it suffices
to show that
{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}({\mathcal{R}}^{0},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])
is generated by at most
3
elements as an
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-module.
A description of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
is provided by
[
15
, Thm. B]
, and a straight-forward case-by-case analysis then yields the desired bound.
∎

From Proposition
4.9
we conclude the following:

Proposition 4.11
.

Suppose
P=(T_{v})
. The following hold:

(i)

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}_{P}={\mathbb{T}}_{v,P}
,
{\mathbb{T}}_{P}={\mathbb{T}}_{1,P}
and either
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}_{P}={\mathbb{H}}_{v,P}
or
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}_{P}=T_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{H}}_{v,P}
;

(ii)

({\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}/{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-})_{P}\simeq(\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}/P)^{s}
for
s=1
or
2
;

(iii)

If
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}_{P}={\mathbb{H}}_{v,P}
, then
({\mathbb{H}}_{1}/{\mathbb{T}}_{1})_{P}\simeq({\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}/{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-})_{P}\simeq(\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}/P)
;

(iv)

If
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}_{P}=T_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{H}}_{v,P}
, then
{\mathbb{T}}_{1,P}=T_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{H}}_{1,P}
.

Note that (i) means that
T_{v}\nmid C_{v}
.

Proof.

Let

{\mathcal{H}}={({\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q},\ \ H={(H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q},

{\mathcal{H}}^{\pm}=({{\mathcal{F}}^{\pm}{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q}\ \ \text{and}\ \ H^{\pm}={({\mathcal{F}}^{\pm}H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q}.

Note that
H^{+}={\mathcal{H}}^{+}\simeq{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
,
H^{-}\simeq S\simeq{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}({\mathcal{R}}^{0},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])
,

{\mathcal{H}}^{-}\simeq M
, and there exists a commutative diagram of
{\mathcal{R}}
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{H}}^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

induced by the commutative diagram in Theorem
4.1
. Since

{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]=\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!],\ H\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]=\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!],

{\mathcal{H}}^{\pm}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]=\widehat{\mathbb{H}}^{\pm}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!],\ \ \text{and}\ \ H^{\pm}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]=\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{\pm}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!],

applying
\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
to the preceding commutative diagram yields
a commutative diagram of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{+}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}^{+}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

The exactness on the left of the rows was established in Lemma
4.4
(i).

From Proposition
4.9
(i) it follows that
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}^{-}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\simeq S\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cong\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
.
It then follows from the exactness of the top row of the above
commutative diagram that
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
is a free
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
-module of rank two and that
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}^{+}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
is a direct summand, that is,

\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}^{+}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]=\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{v,P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
.
As
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
is faithfully flat over
\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}
and
so over
\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}
, it then follows
that
{\mathbb{T}}_{P}
is a free
\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}
-module of rank
2
and
{\mathbb{T}}_{P}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}_{v,P}
. Part (i) follows from this together with Lemma
4.8

From Proposition
4.9
(ii),(iii) it follows that

\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\simeq M\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\oplus\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
such that the image of

\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\simeq S\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}},\varphi_{r}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
is
X\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
. Hence

(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{P}/\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{P})\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cong\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{2}
and

(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{/{\mathrm{tor}},P}/\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{/{\mathrm{tor}},P})\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\cong\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.
It follows that
{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{P}/{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{P}\cong(\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L,P}^{v})^{2}

and
{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{/{\mathrm{tor}},P}/{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{/{\mathrm{tor}},P}\cong(\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L,P}^{v})
.
Since
{\mathbb{H}}^{-}/{\mathbb{T}}^{-}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{1}/{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{1}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{/{\mathrm{tor}}}/{\mathbb{T}}^{-}_{/{\mathrm{tor}}}
,
part (ii) follows.

Parts (iii) and (iv) follow from (i) and (ii) and the fact that
{\mathbb{H}}_{1,P}
is a free
\Lambda_{L,P}^{v}
-module of rank two.

∎

Using Proposition
4.10
we deduce:

Proposition 4.12
.

If
P\neq(T_{v})
, then
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}_{P}={\mathbb{T}}_{v,P}
.

Proof.

We will show that
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}^{+}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]=\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{v,P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
,
from which the proposition follows.
Since
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}^{+}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
, it suffices to show that the minimal number of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
-generators of
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
is
one more than the minimal number of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
-generators of
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{P}^{-}\otimes_{\widehat{\Lambda}_{L,P}^{v}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
.
This is equivalent to the minimal number of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-generators of
H
being one more than the minimal number of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-generators of
H^{-}
, where
H
and
H^{\pm}
are defined just as in
the proof of Proposition
4.11
.

Let
H^{c=\pm}={(H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})^{\pm}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}^{\wedge}_{Q}
. It then follows from Theorem
4.2
that

H=H^{c=+}\oplus H^{c=1}\ \ \text{and}\ \ H^{c=\pm}\simeq{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}(H^{c=\mp},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])

as
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-modules. Note that
H^{c=\pm}
is necessarily non-zero for both signs.

Suppose the minimal number of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-generators of
H
equals the minimal number of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-generators of
H^{-}
. Since
H^{-}\simeq S
, by Proposition
4.10
,
the latter is at most 3. Hence for some choice of sign
\epsilon
, the minimal number of generators of
H^{c=\epsilon}
is 1. Comparing
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]
-ranks then yields

H^{c=\epsilon}\cong{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
as
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-modules, and so
H^{c=-\epsilon}\cong{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}({\mathcal{R}}^{0},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])
as
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-modules. But this implies that the minimal
number of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-generators of
H
is one more than the minimal number of
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
-generators of
H^{c=-\epsilon}\cong{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!]}({\mathcal{R}}^{0},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[\![X]\!])\simeq S\simeq H^{-}
, a contradiction.
∎

Finally, from Propositions
4.11
and
4.12
we immediately deduce the following.

Proposition 4.13
.

The following hold:

(i)

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}_{v}
(that is,
C_{v}
is a unit);

(ii)

{\mathbb{H}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}_{v}
or
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}=T_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
(that is,
D_{v}\mid T_{v}
);

(iii)

If
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}_{v}
then
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}/(T_{v})
;

(iv)

If
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}=T_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
, then
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}=T_{v}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
.

4.2.8.
Some results on submodules of induced representations

Let
{\mathbb{V}}
be as in (
4.9
) and let
{\mathbb{L}}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
be a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-submodule of rank two. We will say that
{\mathbb{L}}
is an
induced module

if
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
.

Let
{\mathbb{V}}_{v}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
be the unique
\mathbf{F}
-line on which

G_{L}
-acts as
\Psi_{L}^{v}
and let
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}={\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{V}}_{v}
.
Since
{\mathbb{L}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}
as
{\mathbb{L}}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module,
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}=\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{V}}_{v}
and so
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
is saturated in
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}
,
from which it follows that
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}=\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
. In particular,
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module of rank one. So
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})
.

Let

{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{L}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{L}}_{v}\subseteq{\mathbb{L}}.

Then
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
is
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable. In particular,
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
, that is,
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
is an induced module.

Lemma 4.14
.

We have

(i)

{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
if and only if
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{L}}
, and

(ii)

if
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbb{L}}
, then

{\mathbb{L}}/{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L}^{v}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}=T_{v}{\mathbb{L}}+{\mathbb{L}}_{v}=T_{v}{\mathbb{L}}+{\mathbb{L}}_{v}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{L}}
then it is immediate that
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
.
On the other hand, if
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
then it follows
from the definition of
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
that
{\mathbb{L}}
is the
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-represenation induced from the
G_{L}
-stable submodule
{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
,
that is,
{\mathbb{L}}={\mathbb{L}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{L}}_{v}={\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
. This proves part (i) of the lemma.

The proof of part (ii) is similar to that of Lemma
4.7
.
Let
e_{+},e_{-}\in{\mathbb{L}}
be a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-basis such that
c
acts on
e_{\pm}
as
\pm 1
.
Let
e_{v}\in{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
be a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-generator. Then
e_{v}=ae_{+}+be_{-}
for some

a,b\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}
. Arguing as in the proof of
loc.cit.
shows that
a,b\mid T
and that
at least one of
a,b
is a unit. As
e_{\bar{v}}=c\cdot e_{v}=ae_{+}-be_{-}
is a generator of
c\cdot{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
,
it follows that
ae_{+},be_{-}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
and these generate
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
. In particular,

{\mathbb{L}}/{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cong\Lambda_{L}^{v}/a\Lambda_{L}^{v}\oplus\Lambda_{L}^{v}/b\Lambda_{L}^{v}

from which the first claim of part (ii) follows.
Since
ae_{+}
and
be_{-}
are both contained in the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module
generated by
e_{v}=ae_{+}+be_{-}
,
T_{v}e_{+}
, and
T_{v}e_{-}
(which is just

T_{v}{\mathbb{L}}+{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
) the second claim also holds.
∎

Let

{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{T_{v}}{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}.

By Lemma
4.14
,
T_{v}{\mathbb{L}}\subset{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
. Hence
{\mathbb{L}}\subset{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}
.

Lemma 4.15
.

The following hold:

(i)

{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
,

(ii)

{\mathbb{L}}_{v}=T_{v}{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}_{v}
,

(iii)

if
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbb{L}}
, then the inclusion
{\mathbb{L}}\subset{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}
induces an isomorphism

{\mathbb{L}}/{\mathbb{L}}_{v}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}_{v}
and satisfies
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L}^{v}
.

Proof.

Parts (i) and (ii) are immediate from the definition of
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}
.
Suppose
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbb{L}}
. Then by Lemma
4.14
(ii),
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}=T_{v}{\mathbb{L}}+{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}_{v}
. It follows that

{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}={\mathbb{L}}+{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}_{v}
and hence that the map
{\mathbb{L}}/{\mathbb{L}}_{v}\rightarrow{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}_{v}
is a surjection.
Since both
{\mathbb{L}}/{\mathbb{L}}_{v}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}_{v}^{\prime\prime}
are free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank one,
the map is also an injection. This proves the first claim of part (iii). The second follows from
the first in combination with part (ii) and the snake lemma applied to the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}}_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}}/{\mathbb{L}}_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

∎

Lemma 4.16
.

Suppose
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\subsetneq{\mathbb{L}}_{2}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
are
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-submodules of rank two such that
{\mathbb{L}}_{1,v}={\mathbb{L}}_{2,v}
.
Then
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}={\mathbb{L}}_{1,v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{L}}_{1,v}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))
and

{\mathbb{L}}_{2}/{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L}^{v}
. In particular,
the inclusion
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\subset{\mathbb{L}}_{2}
induces an identification of

{\mathbb{L}}_{1}/{\mathbb{L}}_{1,v}
with
T_{v}({\mathbb{L}}_{2}/{\mathbb{L}}_{2,v})
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{L}}_{1,v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{L}}_{1,v}={\mathbb{L}}_{2,v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{L}}_{2,v}
. Then
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\subsetneq{\mathbb{L}}_{2}
.
Let
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{T_{v}}{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
.
It follows from Lemma
4.14
(ii) applied to
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{L}}_{2}
that
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\subsetneq{\mathbb{L}}_{2}\subseteq{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{T_{v}}{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
.
Moreover, if
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\neq{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
, then it would follow from Lemma
4.15
(iii) that
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\simeq{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}_{2}
.
But combined with the surjection
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime\prime}/{\mathbb{L}}_{2}
,
this would then imply that
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}={\mathbb{L}}_{2}
, a contradiction. So it must be that
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}={\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
, as claimed in the lemma.
That
{\mathbb{L}}_{2}/{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}/T_{v}\Lambda_{L}^{v}
then follows from Lemma
4.14
(ii).
∎

4.2.9.
Is
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
induced?

One of the important ancillary results of this paper will show that

\begin{split}\bullet\ &{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}={\mathbb{T}}_{1}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\\
\bullet\ &{\mathbb{T}}_{1}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v}))\\
\bullet\ &\text{the $\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]$-surjection ${\mathbb{T}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}_{1}$ splits}\\
\bullet\ &\text{${\mathbb{T}}_{1}/{\mathbb{T}}^{+}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{T}}_{v}$ is identified with $T_{v}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{H}}_{v})$ in $\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{H}}_{v}=\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{H}}^{+}$.}\end{split}

(
Ind
)

Note that this will provide a positive answer to each of the questions (
Q1
)–(
Q4
).

By combining the results in Section
4.2.8
with Proposition
4.13
, we deduce the following result towards establishing (
Ind
).

Proposition 4.17
.

The statements (
Ind
) hold unless

{\mathbb{H}}^{+}=T_{v}{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}=T_{v}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\neq{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.13
(ii), either
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}_{v}
or
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}=T_{v}{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
. Suppose that the former holds.
Then (
Ind
) follows from combining Proposition
4.13
(i),(iii) with
Lemma
4.16
. The key point is that there are inclusions

{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{T}}^{+}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\subseteq{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subsetneq\widetilde{\mathbb{H}},

so that the equality of the first and third modules (which follows from Lemma
4.16
) implies
equality of the first three.

Suppose then that
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}=T_{v}{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
. Then by Proposition
4.13
(iv),

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}=T_{v}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
. There are two cases to consider: (1)
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
and (2)
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}
. In case (1), that (
Ind
) holds follows immediately from Proposition
4.13
(i), so
{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}={\mathbb{T}}^{+}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\subseteq{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
. These leaves case (2), which is exactly the exception in the proposition.
∎

To help with the eventual exclusion of the exceptional case in Proposition
4.17
,
we record the following application of the results from Section
4.2.8
.

Lemma 4.18
.

There exists an induced lattice

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subset{\mathbb{I}}\subsetneq\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
such that
either (a)
{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{I}}
and (
Ind
) holds, or
(b)
{\mathbb{T}}\neq{\mathbb{I}}
, (
Ind
) does not hold, and
the inclusions

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subsetneq{\mathbb{I}}\subsetneq\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
determine
identifications

(i)

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}_{v}=T_{v}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}=T_{v}{\mathbb{H}}_{v}
,

(ii)

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{-}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{I}}/{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\xrightarrow{\sim}T_{v}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{H}}_{v})
.

Proof.

If (
Ind
) holds, then possibility (a) clearly holds with
{\mathbb{I}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
.
So suppose (
Ind
) does not hold.
Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{T}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
, and let

{\mathbb{I}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{T_{v}}\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
. Part (i) is immediate from the definitions.
Since
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\neq\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
, it follows from Lemma
4.15
(iii) that

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{T}}^{+}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{T}}_{v}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{I}}/{\mathbb{I}}_{v}
.
Similarly,
{\mathbb{I}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{\prime}
but
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\neq\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{\prime}

(else
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
since
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}=T_{v}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
), so
part (ii) follows from Lemma
4.14
(ii).
∎

4.3.
Congruence ideal

Another key ingredient in our later arguments is the congruence ideal of the canonical CM family
{\bf{h}}_{v}
.

4.3.1.
The ideal(s)

The (inverse) congruence ideal
I_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}\subset{\bf{F}}
associated with
{\bf{h}}_{v}
is the fractional

\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-ideal
characterised by the existence of a surjective

\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-morphism

M_{\Lambda_{D}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{H}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}},\varphi}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\twoheadrightarrow I_{{\bf{h}}_{v}},\ \ {\bf{h}}_{v}\mapsto 1.

Let
\widetilde{I}_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}\subset{\bf{F}}
be the reflexive closure of
I_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}
. Then

\widetilde{I}_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}
is a principal fractional
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-ideal of the form
\widetilde{I}_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}=\frac{1}{H_{v}}\Lambda_{L}^{v}
for some
H_{v}\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}
.
Such an
H_{v}
is typically referred to as a congruence power series (well-defined only up to a unit of
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
).
In the following we identify a congruence power series for
{\mathbf{h}}_{v}
.

We similarly define the cuspidal congruence ideal
I^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}\subset{\bf{F}}

to be the fractional
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-ideal such that there exists a surjective
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-morphism

S_{\Lambda_{D}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{T}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}},\varphi}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\twoheadrightarrow I^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{{\bf{h}}_{v}},\ \ {\bf{h}}_{v}\mapsto 1.

Clearly
I^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}\subset I_{{\bf{h}}_{v}}\subset{\bf{F}}
. Let
H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}}\in\Lambda_{L}^{v}
be
such that
1/H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
is a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-generator of the reflexive closure
\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
of
I_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
.
Then
H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}}\mid H_{v}
in
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
. We identify a cuspidal congruence power series
H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
along the way to
identifying
H_{v}
.

4.3.2.
The Katz
p
-adic L-function

By results of Katz (see
[
66
]
), we have the following
p
-adic

L
-function for the imaginary quadratic field
L
.

Theorem 4.19
.

There exists an unique
\mathcal{L}_{v}(L)\in\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}}=\Lambda_{L,W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})}^{v}
such that
for any continuous
W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
-linear homomorphism
\theta:\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}

such that
\theta(\gamma_{v}^{h_{p}})=\epsilon(\gamma^{+})^{m}
for some
m\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ {(p-1)}
,
m\geq 0
,

\frac{\theta(\mathcal{L}_{v}(L))}{\Omega_{p}^{2m}}=(1-p^{m}\psi_{\theta}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-2})\cdot(1-p^{m-1}\psi_{\theta}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-2})\frac{w_{L}}{2}\cdot\frac{\pi^{m-1}\cdot m!}{\sqrt{D_{L}}^{m-1}}\cdot\frac{L(1,\psi_{\theta}/\psi_{\theta}^{c})}{\Omega_{\infty}^{2m}},

where

\psi_{\theta}
is the algebraic Hecke character such that
\sigma_{\psi_{\theta}}
is the (composite)
p
-adic character
G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta}}{{\rightarrow}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
, and

(\Omega_{p},\Omega_{\infty})\in W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})^{\times}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
are CM periods over
L
as in

[
76
, §4.5]
.

4.3.3.
The cuspidal congruence ideal and the Katz
p
-adic L-function

The connection between the (cuspidal) congruence ideal associated to the canonical CM Hida family
{\bf{h}}_{v}
and the Katz
p
-adic L-function for
L
is given by
the following.

Theorem 4.20
.

Let
H^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{v}
be a cuspidal congruence power series for
{\bf{h}}_{v}
.
Then

\big(\frac{h_{L}}{w_{L}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{v}(L)\big)=(H^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{v})\subset\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}}.

Proof.

By
[
67
, Thm. I]
and
[
22
]
,
\frac{h_{L}}{w_{L}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{v}(L)\mid H_{v}\text{ in }\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}}.

Conversely, by
[
68
, Thm. 1.4.7]
and
[
121
]
,

H_{v}\mid\frac{h_{L}}{w_{L}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{v}(L)\text{ in }\Lambda_{L,P}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}}

for
(T_{v})\neq P\in 0pt_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}})
.
It thus suffices to show that

{\mathrm{ord}}_{(T_{v})}(H_{v})\neq 0.

By the definition of the congruence ideal (see §
4.3.1
),
{\mathrm{ord}}_{(T_{v})}(H_{v})=0
if (and only if) there is a unique cuspidal Hida family of tame level
D_{L}
passing through the weight one specialisation of
{\bf{h}}_{v}
.
In view of the
q
-expansion (see §
4.1.6
), such a weight one specialisation is nothing but the
p
-ordinary stabilisation of the theta series corresponding to the quadratic Dirichlet character
\chi_{L}
. The desired uniqueness then follows from
[
16
, Thm. A(i)]
.
∎

Remark 4.21
.

The weight one specialisation of
{\bf{h}}_{v}
as above corresponds to a ‘trivial zero’ in the sense of
[
68
, Def. 1.5.2]
.
Furthermore, the non-vanishing of the congruence power series at the identity does not
follow directly from
[
68
]
(for example,
[
68
, Thm. 1.4.7]
) and
[
121
]
):
in
[
68
]
it is assumed throughout that the underlying CM family satisfies a
p
-distinguished hypothesis that does not hold for
{\mathbf{h}}_{v}
.

4.3.4.
Congruence power series

It is relatively straightforward to identify
a congruence power series
H_{v}
in terms of a cuspidal congruence power series
H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
:

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
H^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{v}
be a cuspidal congruence power series for
{\bf{h}}_{v}
. Then
H_{v}=T_{v}\cdot H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}}

is a congruence power series for
{\mathbf{h}}_{v}
. In particular,

\big(\frac{h_{L}}{w_{L}}\cdot T_{v}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{v}(L)\big)=(H_{v})\subset\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}}.

Proof.

We clearly have
{\mathrm{ord}}_{P}(H_{v})={\mathrm{ord}}_{P}(H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}})
for every height one prime
P\in ht_{1}(\Lambda_{L}^{v})
such that
there are no congruences modulo
P
between
{\mathbf{h}}_{v}
and an Eisenstein family. In particular, this equality holds for all
P\neq(T_{v})

as
(T_{v})
is the only height one prime such that
{\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{L}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Psi_{P})
,
\Psi_{P}=\Psi_{L}^{v}\ \mathrm{mod}\ P
, is not irreducible
(and so the only prime for which such a congruence could possibly exist). That
{\mathrm{ord}}_{(T_{v})}(H_{v})=1
follows
from
[
16
, Prop. 5.4(ii)]
(see also Proposition
4.9
(ii)). This proves that
H_{v}=T_{v}\cdot H_{v}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
is a
congruence power series. The second claim of the theorem then follows from Theorem
4.20
.
∎

In light of Theorem
4.22
we find it convenient to set

\mathcal{H}_{v}=\frac{h_{L}}{w_{L}}\cdot T_{v}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{v}(L)\in\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}},

(4.12)

so that
(H_{v})=(\mathcal{H}_{v})
in
\Lambda_{L}^{v,{\mathrm{ur}}}
.

5.
Zeta elements over imaginary quadratic fields: the ordinary case

In this section we introduce the Beilinson–Flach element associated with an ordinary weight two newform and the canonical
CM Hida family
and recall its associated explicit reciprocity laws and their connection with
p
-adic
L
-functions.
Our Beilinson–Flach element is just a variant of the Rankin–Selberg zeta elements constructed
by Loeffler and Zerbes together with Lei
[
93
]
,
[
94
]
and Kings
[
85
]
.
We use these elements (together with the choice of a suitable auxiliary newform) to finish answering
the questions (
Q1
)-(
Q4
) raised in §
4
. With these answers in hand,
we then define a two-variable zeta element over the CM field for an ordinary newform.
In Section
6
below we define
the analog of these Beilinson–Flach classes and zeta elements for supersingular newforms and record the corresponding
explicit reciprocity laws.

We continue with the notation and conventions introduced in §§
2
–
4
.
Throughout this section we assume
g
to be ordinary at
p
.

5.1.
Some more Iwasawa cohomology

For
M={\mathbb{T}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1},{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
, or
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
as in §
4
, let

H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\hat{\otimes}M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=H^{1}(G_{{\mathbb{Q}},\Sigma},T(1)\hat{\otimes}M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

where
\Sigma
is any finite set of primes containing all
\ell\mid ND_{L}p\infty
.
These cohomology groups are independent of the choice of
\Sigma
and consist of classes unramified
outside
p
, as the notation suggest (cf. Remark
3.1
).

Recall that
M
is a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module and there is a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-filtration

0\rightarrow M^{+}\rightarrow M\rightarrow M^{-}\rightarrow 0
.
Since
g
is ordinary at
p
, there is also a
\mathcal{O}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-filtration
0\rightarrow T^{+}\rightarrow T\rightarrow T^{-}\rightarrow 0
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For
M={\mathbb{T}}_{1}
or
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
,
the natural maps

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

and

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

are injective.

Proof.

The kernel in both instances is the image of
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
Since the
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-actions on
T^{-}(1)
and
M^{-}
are unramified while the action on
\Lambda
is totally ramified,
it is easily seen that
H^{0}(I_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=0
and hence
that
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=0
.
∎

By Lemma
5.1
we can identify
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
with its image
in
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
, and similarly for

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
We then let

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\hat{\otimes}M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=\{\kappa\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\hat{\otimes}M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\kappa)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}M^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\}.

The reason for using the subscripts ‘
\mathrm{rel}
’ and ‘
{\mathrm{ord}}
’ will be made clear later (see Section
5.5
below).

5.2.
The Beilinson–Flach element

Let
\alpha
be the unit root of
x^{2}-a_{p}(g)x+p
. We will say that
g
is
anomalous
if

\alpha^{2}\equiv 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ \lambda\ \ \text{and}\ \ \text{(irr${}_{\mathbb{Q}}$) does not hold.}

(anom)

Let
g_{\alpha}(z)=g(z)-\alpha^{-1}pg(pz)\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(Np))
be the
p
-stabilisation of

g
corresponding to
\alpha
. In the notation of
[
85
]
there is an isomorphism

(Pr^{\alpha})^{*}:M_{F_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*}\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{F_{\lambda}}(g)^{*}=V(1)

that maps
M_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*}\hookrightarrow M_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}(g)^{*}=T(1)
,
and the latter is an isomorphism if
g
is not anomalous
[
85
, Prop. 7.3.1]
.
It follows from the proof of
op. cit.
that the image of
M_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*}
in
T(1)
contains

(1-\alpha^{2})T(1)
in all cases.

The form
g_{\alpha}
is the specialisation of a Hida family
{\bf g}
in the sense of
[
85
, §7]
.
Let
M({\bf g})^{*}
be the
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-module defined in
[
85
, Def. 7.2.5]
. As explained in
[
85
, §7.3]
, there is a specialisation
map
M({\bf g})^{*}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\bf g}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*}
, so composing
with
(Pr^{\alpha})^{*}
yields a specialisation map

M({\bf{g}})^{*}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\bf{g}}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}\rightarrow T(1)

(5.1)

whose image is a sublattice of finite index.
Here
\Lambda_{\bf{g}}
is a localisation of Hida’s ordinary Hecke algebras
{\mathbb{H}}_{Np^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
(see §
4.2.2
)
at a maximal ideal and the tensor product is with respect to the local
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-homomorphism
\Lambda_{\bf{g}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}

that sends
T_{\ell}^{\prime}
to
a_{\ell}(g)
for all
\ell\neq p
and maps
U_{p}^{\prime}
to
\alpha
.
This specialisation map (
5.1
) is surjective if
g
is not anomalous. If
g
is anomalous,
then (
5.1
) might not be surjective, but the image does contain

(1-\alpha^{2})T(1)
.

The canonical CM family
{\mathbf{h}}_{v}
corresponds to a branch (in the sense of
[
85
, §7.5]
) of a Hida family
{\mathbf{h}}
of tame level
D_{L}
,
and it follows from the definition of
{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
and
M(\mathbf{h})^{*}
that there is a specialisation map

M({\bf h})^{*}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\bf h}}\Lambda^{v}_{L}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{H}}_{1}.

(5.2)

More precisely,
\Lambda_{\bf{h}}
and
M({\bf h})^{*}
are the respective localisations of

{\mathbb{H}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\mathrm{ord}}(D_{L}p^{\infty})
at a maximal ideal of
{\mathbb{H}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}

and the tensor product in (
5.2
) is with respect to the map induced by

\varphi:{\mathbb{H}}_{D_{L}p^{\infty}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}\rightarrow\Lambda^{v}_{L}
from §
4.2.4
.
In particular, the left-hand side of (
5.2
) is just
{\mathbb{H}}
and the map is just
the quotient modulo
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-torsion.

Fix an integer
c>1
such that
(c,6ND_{L}p)=1
.
Let

{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}^{{\bf g},{\bf h}}_{1}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],M({{\mathbf{g}}})^{*}\hat{\otimes}M({{\mathbf{h}})}^{*}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

be the class associated in
[
85
, Def. 8.1]
to the Hida families
\bf g
and
\bf h
. Recall our convention
that
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
acts on
\Lambda
via the inverse of the canonical character
\Psi
.
Let

{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

be the image of
{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}^{{{\mathbf{g}}},{{\mathbf{h}}}}_{1}
under the map induced by the specialisations
(
5.1
) and (
5.2
).

5.2.1.
Local properties at
p
.

We record some important local properties
of these Beilinson–Flach classes.

Lemma 5.2
.

The image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g_{/L}))
in
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

is
0
.

Proof.

In the proof of
[
85
, Prop. 8.1.7]
it is shown that the image of
{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}^{{\bf g},{\bf h}}_{1}

in
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\mathscr{F}^{--}M({\bf g}\otimes{\bf h})^{*}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is
0

(notation as in
loc. cit.
). Since

\mathscr{F}^{--}M({\bf g}\otimes{\bf h})^{*}\subset M({\bf g})^{*}\hat{\otimes}M({\bf h})^{*}
projects onto

T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}
under the specialisation maps (
5.1
) and (
5.2
),
the lemma follows.
∎

As an immediate consequence of this lemma and Lemma
5.1
we have:

Corollary 5.3
.

(i)

The image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g_{/L}))

in
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is contained in

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
. In particular,

{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

(ii)

The image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g_{/L}))
in
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is contained in
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

5.3.
Explicit reciprocity laws

In this section we recall the explicit reciprocity laws
for the Beilinson–Flach elements from
[
85
]
, which connect the elements

{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g_{/L})
to two-variable
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Let
{\mathscr{R}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda
.
We will consider two sets
\Xi^{(I)}
and
\Xi^{(II)}
of continuous characters

\chi:\Gamma_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}_{p}
. Each such character determines
a continuous
{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
-homomorphism
\phi_{\chi}:{\mathscr{R}}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, where

{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
is identified with the completion of
\iota_{p}({\mathcal{O}})
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.
Note that any character
\chi:\Gamma_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}_{p}

is determined by the pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=(\chi|_{\Gamma_{L}^{v}},\chi|_{\Gamma})
. Then the first set of homomorphisms is

\Xi^{(I)}:=\{\chi=(1,\chi_{2})\ :\ \text{$\chi_{2}=\psi_{\zeta}$ for some $p^{t}$th-root of unity $\zeta$}\},

(5.3)

and the second set of homomorphisms is

\Xi^{(II)}:=\left\{\chi=(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\ :\ \begin{matrix}\text{$\chi_{1}(\gamma_{v}^{h_{p}})=\epsilon(\gamma_{+})^{m}$ for some $m\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ p-1$, $m>0$}\\
\text{$\chi_{2}(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})=\zeta\epsilon(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})^{n}$ for some $p^{t}$th-root of unity $\zeta$ and some $0\leq n\leq m$}\end{matrix}\right\}.

(5.4)

5.3.1.
The explicit reciprocity law I

As we explain below, from the constructions in
[
85
, §§8,10]
we obtain an injective

{\mathscr{R}}
-morphism

{\mathscr{C}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow{J_{g}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}{\mathscr{R}},

(5.5)

where
J_{g}\subset F_{\lambda}
is a certain fractional ideal
such that

\text{$J_{g}=\frac{1}{\alpha(1-p\alpha^{-2})(1-\alpha^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)c_{g}}{\mathcal{O}}\ $ if \ (irr${}_{\mathbb{Q}}$) holds},

where
c_{g}
is the congruence number of
g
as in Section
2.2.3
and

\lambda_{N}(g)=\pm 1
is the eigenvalue of
g
for the usual Atkin–Lehner involution
w_{N}
of level
N
.
We let

{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}(g/L)={\mathscr{C}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g/L)))\in{J_{g}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}{\mathscr{R}}.

This makes sense in light of Corollary
5.3
(ii).

The map
{\mathscr{C}}
is related to the Coleman map
Col_{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}
as follows.

Proposition 5.4
.

The reduction of the map
{\mathscr{C}}
modulo
\gamma_{v}-1
equals the composition

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ \mathrm{mod}\ \gamma_{v}-1}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\frac{1}{\alpha(1-p\alpha^{-2})(1-\alpha^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)}Col_{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}J_{g}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

The first map in this proposition depends on an identification
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}/(\gamma_{v}-1){\mathbb{H}}^{+}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
determined by part of the data defining
the map
{\mathscr{C}}
(the reduction modulo
\gamma_{v}-1
of the map
\omega_{{\bf h}_{v}}
recalled below).

The element
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}(g/L)
is related to
L
-values by the following explicit reciprocity law.

Theorem 5.5

(Explicit Reciprocity Law I)
.

The element
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}(g/L)
satisfies:
For
\chi\in\Xi^{(I)}
,

\phi_{\chi}({\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}(g/L))=(c^{2}-\langle c\rangle\psi_{\zeta}^{2}(c)\chi_{L}(c))\frac{\mathcal{E}(\zeta)}{(1-p\alpha^{-2})(1-\alpha^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)}\frac{L(1,f\otimes\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})L(1,f\otimes\chi_{L}\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}{\pi^{2}(-i)2^{3}\langle g,g\rangle},

where

\mathcal{E}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\alpha^{-2(t+1)}\frac{p^{2(t+1)}}{\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})^{2}}&\zeta\neq 1\\
(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})^{4}&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Here
\langle c\rangle=(1+p)^{\log_{p}(c)}\in 1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

The proofs of both Proposition
5.4
and Theorem
5.5
are given below.

Remark 5.6
.

As explained below,
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}(g/L)
is essentially the specialization at
g_{\alpha}
of the
p
-adic Rankin-Selberg
L
-function
constructed by Hida for
the branches
{\bf a}
and
{\bf h_{v}}
of the Hida families
{{\mathbf{g}}}
and
{{\mathbf{h}}}
. As such it satisfies an interpolation formula as in
Theorem
5.5
for all characters
\chi:\Gamma_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
of finite order.
However, the general interpolation formula is more complicated to write and not necessary for the purposes of this paper.

Let
{\mathscr{S}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}=\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}^{v}
, so
{\mathscr{R}}={\mathscr{S}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda
.
Let
\nu:{\mathscr{R}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{R}}
be the
{\mathscr{S}}
-algebra automorphism such that

\nu(a\otimes b\otimes[\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}])=a\otimes b[\gamma_{+}]^{2}\otimes[\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]=a\otimes b\epsilon\Psi_{D}(\tilde{\gamma}_{+})\otimes[\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}],

(5.6)

for any lift
\tilde{\gamma}^{+}\in G_{L}
of
\gamma_{L}^{+}\in\Gamma_{L}
. (Here we view
\Psi_{D}
as
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-valued via the map

\varphi_{v}
fixed at the end of Section
4.2.2
.)
If
M
is any profinite
{\mathscr{S}}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-module, then there is an isomorphism
of profinite
{\mathscr{S}}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-modules

M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda=M\hat{\otimes}_{\mathscr{S}}{\mathscr{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle id\otimes\nu}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}M(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathscr{S}}{\mathscr{R}}=M(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda,

(5.7)

where the
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-action on the
\Lambda
-factor is by the inverse of the canonical character
\Psi
.

To define
{\mathscr{C}}
and see that the claims in the preceding proposition and theorem hold, we recall
a construction in
[
85
]
. In particular, for any unramified profinite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-module
M
,

[
85
, Thm. 8.2.3]
provides a homomorphism of
\Lambda
-modules

{\mathcal{L}}_{M}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow D(M)\hat{\otimes}I^{-1}\Lambda

that is functorial in
M
.
Here
D(M)=(M\hat{\otimes}W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}))^{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}}
and
I=(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}-\epsilon^{-1}(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}))
.
More precisely, this is the restriction of the map denoted
{\mathcal{L}}_{M}
in
op. cit.
to the
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{(0)}
-summand.
Taking
M=(T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+})(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})=T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})

and pre-composing with the isomorphism

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

coming from (
5.7
) yields
a homomorphism

{\mathcal{C}}_{g,{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow D(T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\subset D(T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\hat{\otimes}I^{-1}\Lambda.

That the image of
{\mathcal{L}}_{M}
lies in
D(M)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda
in this case follows from the fourth bullet point of

[
85
, Thm. 8.2.3]
and the fact that
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+})=0
. This same vanishing implies that

{\mathcal{L}}_{M}
is injective in this case by the third bullet point of
loc. cit.
, hence so is
{\mathcal{C}}_{g,{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
.
The map
{\mathscr{C}}
is then defined to be the composition of
{\mathcal{C}}_{g,{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
with an isomorphism

\psi_{g,{\bf h_{v}}}:D(T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\sim}{J_{g}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}{\mathscr{R}},

defined as follows.

Following the notation of
[
85
]
, let
{\bf a}
be the (new, cuspidal) branch of the Hida family
{\mathbf{g}}
containing the
p
-stabilization
g_{\alpha}
of
g
,
and let
\eta_{\bf g}:D(\mathscr{F}^{-}M({\mathbf{g}})^{*})\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}}}\Lambda_{\bf a}\rightarrow I_{\bf a}
be the

\Lambda_{\bf a}
homomorphism of
[
85
, Prop. 10.1.1 part 2]
. Here
I_{\bf a}
is the congruence ideal for the branch
\bf a

as in
[
85
, 7.7.1]
. Specializing to
g_{\alpha}
as in
[
85
, Prop. 10.1.1 part 2(b)]
yields a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathscr{F}^{-}M({\mathbf{g}})^{*})\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}}}\Lambda_{\bf a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\bf a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{dR}^{0}(M_{F_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathscr{F}^{-}M_{F_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{dR}^{0}(V^{-}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(V^{-}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\lambda},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\bf a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Pr^{\alpha})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Pr^{\alpha})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\eta}_{g}}

(5.8)

where the top vertical arrows are induced via functoriality from the specialisation map

M({\bf g})^{*}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\bf a}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}\twoheadrightarrow M_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*}
(the corresponding
homomorphism
\Lambda_{\bf a}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
extends to a homomorphism
I_{\bf a}\rightarrow F_{\lambda}
),
the left horizontal arrows are just the identifications as in Remark
2.12
, and the middle right horizontal isomorphism is
such that the composition of the middle horizontal arrows is given by
loc. cit.
.
We note that by functoriality the image of the composition of the middle
two vertical arrows is in
D(T^{-}(1))
.
The map
\tilde{\eta}_{g}
is defined to be
the isomorphism making this diagram commute. From the definition of

\eta_{{\mathbf{g}}}
and the proof of
[
85
, Prop. 10.1.1 part 2(b)]
we find that

\tilde{\eta}_{g}=[\cdot,\frac{1}{\alpha(1-p\alpha^{-2})(1-\alpha^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)}\eta_{\omega_{g}}]:D_{dR}(V^{-}(1))=D(T^{-}(1))\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}F_{\lambda}\rightarrow F_{\lambda},

(5.9)

where
[\cdot,\cdot]
is the pairing as in (
2.8
).
We define
J_{g}
to be the image of
D(T^{-}(1))
under

\tilde{\eta}_{g}
. Recall that the image of

S_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
under the deRham-étale comparison map
S_{F_{\lambda}}=D_{dR}(V^{-}(1))\simeq D(V^{-}(1))
is contained in
D(T^{-}(1))
, with equality if (irr
Q
) holds
(see Remark
2.12
). By Lemma
2.5
an
{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
-generator of
S_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
is of the form
\omega_{g}/c
for some
c\in{\mathcal{O}}
that divides
c_{g}
and we may take
c=c_{g}
if (irr
Q
) holds. It
follows that if (irr
Q
) holds, then
J_{g}
is generated by
\tilde{\eta}_{g}(\omega_{g}/c_{g})=(\alpha(1-p\alpha^{-2})(1-\alpha^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)c_{g})^{-1}
.

Still following the notation of
[
85
]
, the map
\omega_{\bf h}
of
[
85
, Prop. 10.1.1 part 1]

induces a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-homomorphism

\omega_{{\bf h}_{v}}:D({\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{L}^{v}.

(5.10)

Here we have used that
\mathscr{F}^{+}M({\bf h})^{*}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\bf h}}\Lambda_{L}^{v}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}
and that the twist by
\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1}
is
then identified with the
(-1-{\bf k})
-twist of
loc. cit.
(and that
\varepsilon_{\bf h}|_{G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}}=1
since
p
splits
in the quadratic field
L
).

The map
\psi_{g,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{v}}
is defined to be the composition of isomorphisms

\psi_{g,{\bf h_{v}}}:D(T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\hat{\otimes}\Lambda=D(T^{-}(1))\hat{\otimes}D({\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{\eta}_{g}\otimes\omega_{{\bf h}_{v}}\otimes id}}{{\longrightarrow}}{J_{g}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}{\mathscr{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle id\otimes\nu^{-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{J_{g}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}{\mathscr{R}},

and
{\mathscr{C}}
is then defined to be the injective map

{\mathscr{C}}=\psi_{g,{\bf h_{v}}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}_{g,\bf{h}_{v}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow{J_{g}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}{\mathscr{R}}.

This final map is an
{\mathscr{R}}
-homomorphism.

Proof of Proposition
5.4
.

The map
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
factors through a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-isomorphism
D({\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\cong{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})

(see also
[
52
, Prop. 1.7.6]
). Since
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module of rank one,
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
determines a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-basis of

{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})
. In particular,
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
determines isomorphisms

{\mathbb{H}}^{+}/(\gamma_{v}-1){\mathbb{H}}^{+}\simeq{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\Psi_{D}^{-1})/(\gamma_{v}-1){\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\Psi_{D}^{-1})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
It then follows from functoriality that
{\mathscr{C}}\,\ \mathrm{mod}\ \gamma_{v}-1
factors as
the composition

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ \mathrm{mod}\ \gamma_{v}-1}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}}_{T^{-}(1)}}}{{\rightarrow}}D(T^{-1})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{\eta}_{g}\otimes id}}{{\rightarrow}}J_{g}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda,

where the first map is just the projection induced by the isomorphism
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}/(\gamma_{v}-1){\mathbb{H}}^{+}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
from above.
The characterization (
5.9
) of
\tilde{\eta}_{g}
in terms of
\eta_{\omega_{g}}
combined with
the fact that
{\mathcal{L}}_{T^{-}(1)}
is just Perrin-Riou’s ‘big logarithm map’ for
V^{-}(1)
(see the second bullet point of
[
85
, Thm. 8.2.3]
)
shows – by comparing with the definition of the Coleman map
Col_{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}
in terms of this big logarithm map – that the above displayed composition of maps equals

\frac{1}{\alpha(1-p\alpha^{-2})(1-\alpha^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)}Col_{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

It follows from the definition of
{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g/L)
and the commutativity of the diagram (
5.8
)
that
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}(g/L)
is – in the notation of
[
85
, §10]
– just the image of
\langle{\mathcal{L}}(_{c}\mathcal{BF}_{1}^{{\bf g},{\bf h}}),\eta_{\bf a}\otimes\omega_{\bf h}\rangle\in I_{{\bf a}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{\bf h}^{\rm{cusp}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda
under the projection

I_{{\bf a}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{\bf h}^{\rm{cusp}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow J_{g}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda=J_{g}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}{\mathscr{R}},

induced by the specialization map
\Lambda_{\bf a}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
corresponding to
g_{\alpha}

and the projection
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\rm cusp}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}=\Lambda_{L}^{v}
.
The claim in the theorem is then an easy consequence of
[
85
, Thms. 2.7.4, 7.7.2, 10.2.2]

(see also the comment about weight one specialisations following
[
85
, Thm. 7.7.2]
). Here we have
used that the weight one specialisation of
{\mathbf{h}}_{v}
is the ordinary stabilisation of the weight one Eisenstein series
E_{1}(1,\chi_{L})

for the characters
1
and
\chi_{L}
, which means that the Rankin-Selberg
L
-function

L(s,g,E_{1}(1,\chi_{L}),\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})
equals
L(s,g\otimes\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})L(s,g\otimes\chi_{L}\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})
.
∎

5.3.2.
The explicit reciprocity law II

The second explicit reciprocity law essentially arises from exchanging the roles of
g
and
\bf{h}_{v}
in the preceding analysis.
As we explain, there is an injective
{\mathscr{R}}
-homomorphism

{\mathscr{L}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow\tilde{I}_{\bf h_{v}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\mathscr{R}}

defined analogously to
{\mathscr{C}}
. Here
I_{\bf{h}_{v}}
is the congruence ideal for the canonical CM family introduced in Section
4.3
and

\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
is its reflexive closure.
We let

{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}^{Gr}(g/L)={\mathscr{L}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g/L)))\in\tilde{I}_{\bf h_{v}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\mathscr{R}}.

This makes sense by Corollary
5.3
(i). These satisfy:

Theorem 5.7

(Expicit Reciprocity Law II)
.

For
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
,

\phi_{\chi}({\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}^{Gr}(g/L))=\lambda_{D_{L}}(h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0})(c^{2}-\psi_{\zeta}^{2}(c)\chi_{L}(c)\langle c\rangle^{2n-m+1)}){\mathcal{E}}(\chi)\frac{n!(n-1)!}{\pi^{2n+1}(-i)^{m-1}2^{2n+m+1}}\frac{L(1+n,g,h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n})}{\langle h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0}\rangle},

where

{\mathcal{E}}(\chi)=\begin{cases}\frac{(1-\frac{p^{n}}{\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\alpha})(1-\frac{p^{n}}{\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\beta})(1-\frac{p^{m}\alpha}{p^{n+1}\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\alpha})(1-\frac{p^{m}\beta}{p^{n+1}\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\alpha})}{(1-p^{m-1}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))(1-p^{m}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))}&\zeta=1,n\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ p-1\\
\frac{(p^{t+1}/\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n}))^{2}(p^{2n-1}/\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{t+1}}{(1-p^{m-1}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))(1-p^{m}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))}&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Here
h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0}
is the newform of level
D_{L}
and weight
2m+1
whose ordinary
p
-stabilisation is
h_{v,\chi_{1}}
.
The
L
-function
L(s,g,h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},\psi)
is the
\psi
-twist of the usual Rankin–Selberg
L
-functions (cf.
[
85
, §2.7]
).
Also, we identify the Galois character
G_{L}\rightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\chi_{1}}}{{\rightarrow}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}

with an algebraic Hecke character of infinity type
(-2m,0)
as in Section
4.1.2
, which we continue to denote by
\chi_{1}
.
The hypotheses on
\chi_{1}
ensure that this algebraic Hecke character is unramified at each prime above
p
, and we have
denoted by
\varpi_{\bar{v}}
a uniformiser at
\bar{v}
(note that
\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})
is a
p
-adic unit).
Here
t\geq 0
is such that
\zeta
is a primitive
p^{t}
th root of unity. Finally,
\langle c\rangle=(1+p)^{\log_{p}c}\in 1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

Remark 5.8
.

Just as for Theorem
5.5
, the proof of Theorem
5.7

essentially identifies
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}^{Gr}(g/L)
as the specialization at
g_{\alpha}
of a
p
-adic Rankin-Selberg
L
-function
constructed by Hida for
the branches
{\bf h_{v}}
and
{\bf a}
of the Hida families
{\bf h}
and
{\bf g}
. As such it also satisfies an interpolation formula
for a larger collection of characters than just
\Xi^{(II)}
. However – as for
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}
–
the general interpolation formula is more complicated to write and not necessary for the purposes of this paper.

Remark 5.9
.

The superscript ‘
Gr
’ on
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}^{Gr}(g/L)
is intended to reference Greenberg. The use of this notation is motivated
by
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}^{Gr}(g/L)
being essentially a
p
-adic
L
-function for a
p
-adic family of Galois representations
satisfying the Panchishkin condition, much as considered in
[
58
]
.

The definition of
{\mathscr{L}}
is analogous to that of
{\mathscr{C}}
.
Let
\iota_{\epsilon}:\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{\mathcal{G}}
be the isomorphism such that
\gamma\mapsto\epsilon(\gamma)\gamma
for all
\gamma\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
From the isomorphism
T^{+}(1)\otimes\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle id\otimes\iota_{\epsilon}}}{{\longrightarrow}}T^{+}\otimes\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}
we obtain an
injective homomorphism

{\mathcal{L}}_{g,{\bf h}_{v}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{(-1)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}}_{T^{+}\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}D(T^{+}\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{(-1)}\cong D(T^{+}\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda,

just as we did
{\mathcal{C}}_{g,{\bf h}_{v}}
. Then
{\mathscr{L}}
is the composition of
{\mathcal{L}}_{g,{\bf h}_{v}}
with an isomorphism

\xi_{g,{\bf h}_{v}}:D(T^{+}\otimes\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\sim}\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\mathscr{R}}

defined as follows.

We let
\eta_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}:D({\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-})\xrightarrow{\sim}I_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
be the
\Lambda_{L^{v}}
-map as in
[
85
, Prop. 10.1.1 part 2(b)]
.
Here we are using that
\eta_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
from
op. cit.
factors through the quotient

D(\mathscr{F}^{-}M({\mathbf{h}})^{*})\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\Lambda_{L}^{v}=D({\mathbb{H}}^{-})\twoheadrightarrow D({\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{1})
by functoriality.
The map
\eta_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
then induces a map on reflexive closures

\tilde{\eta}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}:\widetilde{D({\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{1}))}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
. Since

\widetilde{D({\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{1})}=D(\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}_{1})=D(\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-})

by functoriality (this is easily seen from the fact that there is a natural identification
D(M)\simeq M
for profinite unramified

{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-modules
M
that is functorial in
M

[
52
, Prop. 1.7.6]
, this yields a

\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-isomorphism
\tilde{\eta}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}:D(\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-})\xrightarrow{\sim}\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}

of free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-modules of rank one
and a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathscr{F}^{-}M({\mathbf{h}})^{*})\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\Lambda_{L}^{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\eta}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}}

(5.11)

where the left vertical arrow is induced by functoriality.

Let
