\omega_{g,\alpha}:D(V^{+})\rightarrow F_{\lambda}
denote the map

[\omega_{g},-]:D(V^{+})=D_{cris}^{0}(V)\xrightarrow{\sim}F_{\lambda}
,
where
[-,-]
is the pairing from (
2.8
). The restriction to
D(T^{+})
is mapped isomorphically onto
{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
, and it follows
from
[
85
, Prop. 10.1.1 part 1]
that
\omega_{g,\alpha}
fits into a commutative
diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathscr{F}^{+}M({\mathbf{h}})^{*}(\Psi_{D}^{-1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\bf a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathscr{F}^{+}M_{F_{\lambda}}(g_{\alpha})^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(V^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\lambda},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{{\mathbf{g}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[(Pr_{\alpha})^{*}(\omega_{g}),-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Pr_{\alpha})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{g,\alpha}}

(5.12)

where the vertical arrows come from the specialisation maps. We note that by functoriality, the image of the composition of the left vertical arrows is
contained in
D(T^{+})
.

The maps
\xi_{g,{\bf h}_{v}}
and
{\mathscr{L}}
are then defined to be

\xi_{g,{\bf h_{v}}}:D(T^{+}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega_{g}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{{\bf h}_{v}}\otimes id}}{{\longrightarrow}}{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle id\otimes id\otimes\iota_{\epsilon}^{-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda=\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\mathscr{R}}

and

{\mathscr{L}}=\xi_{g,{\bf h_{v}}}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{g,\bf{h}_{v}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\mathscr{R}}.

The former is an isomorphism and the latter is an injective
{\mathscr{R}}
-homomorphism.

Proof of Theorem
5.7
.

It follows from the definition of
{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g/L)
and the commutativity of the diagrams (
5.11
) and (
5.12
)
that
{\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}^{Gr}(g/L)
is - in the notation of
[
85
, §10]
– the image of

\langle_{c}{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{BF}_{1}^{{\bf g},{\bf h}}),\omega_{\bf g}\otimes\eta_{\bf h_{v}}\rangle\in\Lambda_{\bf g}^{\rm{cusp}}\hat{\otimes}I_{\bf h}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda

under the projection

\Lambda_{\bf g}^{\rm{cusp}}\hat{\otimes}I_{\bf h}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{I}_{\bf h_{v}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda=\tilde{I}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L}^{v}}{\mathscr{R}}
induced by the map
\Lambda_{\bf g}^{\rm{cusp}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
underlying the specialisation map (
5.1
).
The claim in the theorem is then an easy consequence of
[
85
, Thms. 2.7.4, 7.7.2, 10.2.2]
.
∎

5.3.3.
Integral normalisations

Let
\tilde{c}_{g}\in{\mathcal{O}}
be such that
(\alpha(1-p\alpha^{-2})(1-\alpha^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)\tilde{c}_{g})^{-1}
is a
{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
-generator of
J_{g}
. As noted above, we can, and do, take

\tilde{c}_{g}=c_{g}
if (irr
Q
) holds, where
c_{g}\in{\mathcal{O}}
is a congruence number as in Section
2.2.3
. We then put

{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}=\tilde{c}_{g}\cdot{\mathscr{C}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda={\mathscr{R}}.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}_{L}\in\Lambda_{L}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
be as in (
4.12
). Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
be the completion of the ring of integers of the
maximal unramified extension of
F_{\lambda}
(this is just the compositum of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}
and
W(\bar{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
).
We put

{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}={\mathcal{H}}_{v}\cdot{\mathscr{L}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\otimes_{\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}^{v}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{v}\rightarrow{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}},

where
{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda={\mathscr{R}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}^{v}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{v}
.

We also normalise the elements
{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g/L)
. Let
r_{c}=\log_{p}(c)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. and let

\mathbf{c}=(c^{2}-\chi_{L}(c)\otimes\langle c\rangle\gamma_{v}^{-r_{c}}\otimes\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}^{2r_{c}})\in{\mathscr{R}}.

Modulo the maximal ideal of
{\mathscr{R}}
,
\mathbf{c}
is congruent to
c^{2}-\chi_{L}(c)
.
As
p
is odd, we can therefore choose
c
so that
\mathbf{c}\in{\mathscr{R}}^{\times}
. Henceforth we assume that
c
satisfies this.
We then put

\mathcal{BF}(g/L)=\mathbf{c}^{-1}\cdot{{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}(g/L)}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda).

We also put

{\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L)={\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathcal{BF}(g/L)))\in{\mathscr{R}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)={\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathcal{BF}(g/L)))\in{\mathscr{R}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}.

We record the following versions of our two explicit reciprocity laws (Theorems
5.5
and
5.7
) and their consequences.

Theorem 5.10

(Explicit Reciprocity Law I
′
)
.

The element
{\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L)\in{\mathscr{R}}
satisfies:
For
\chi\in\Xi^{(I)}
with
\chi_{1}=1
,

\phi_{\chi}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L))=\mathcal{E}(\zeta)\frac{L(1,f\otimes\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})L(1,f\otimes\chi_{L}\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}{\pi^{2}(-i)2^{3}\Omega_{g}},

where

\Omega_{g}=\frac{\langle g,g\rangle}{\tilde{c}_{g}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mathcal{E}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\alpha^{-2(t+1)}\frac{p^{2(t+1)}}{\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})^{2}}&\zeta\neq 1\\
(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})^{4}&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Given
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
let

\psi_{\chi}
be the algebraic Hecke character of
L
with infinity type
(n-m,n)
and such that

\sigma_{\psi_{\chi}}
is the composition

G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}\times\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\chi_{1}\times\chi_{2}^{-1}}}{{\rightarrow}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Then the second explicit reciprocity law can be rewritten as follows.

Theorem 5.11

(Explicit Reciprocity Law II
′
)
.

The element
{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)\in{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
satisfies:
For
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
,

\phi_{\chi}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L))=-w_{L}(\chi_{1}(\gamma_{v})-1){\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\chi)\frac{n!(n-1)!\pi^{2m-2n-1}}{2^{2n-2m+2}D_{L}^{m/2}}\cdot\Omega_{p}^{2m}\frac{L(1,g,\psi_{\chi})}{\Omega_{\infty}^{2m}},

where

{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\chi)=\begin{cases}(1-a(p)\psi_{\chi}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}p^{-1}+\psi_{\chi}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-2}p^{-1})^{2}&\zeta=1,n\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ p-1\\
(p^{t+1}/\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n}))^{2}(p^{2n-1}/\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{t+1}&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Here
(\Omega_{p},\Omega_{\infty})
are the CM periods as in Theorem
4.19
,
and

L(s,g,\psi_{\chi})=\sum_{\mathfrak{a},(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{f}_{\psi_{\chi}})=1}a_{g}(N(\mathfrak{a}))\psi_{\chi}(x_{\mathfrak{a}})N(\mathfrak{a})^{-s}=L(s,g,h_{\chi_{1}}^{0},\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n}).

The key to this rewrite of the second explicit reciprocity law is the following formula (essentially due to Shimura – see
[
67
, §7]
), which expresses
{\langle h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0}\rangle}

in terms of
L(1,\chi_{1}/\chi_{1}^{c})
:

{\langle h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0}\rangle}=\frac{h_{L}m!D_{L}^{1/2}}{w_{L}2^{m-1}(2\pi)^{m+1}}L(1,\chi_{1}/\chi_{1}^{c}).

Combined with the interpolation formula for the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathscr{L}}_{v}(L)
from Theorem
4.19
, this easily yields
the formula in Theorem
5.11
.

Remark 5.12
.

A priori
, the class
\mathcal{BF}(g/L)
depends on the choice of an auxiliary integer
c
. However, as
the notation suggest, the class can be shown to be independent of this choice. This in fact follows from
Theorem
5.11
, the injectivity of
{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}
, and the Zariski density of the specialisations
\phi_{\chi}
,
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
.

5.3.4.
Comparisons with other
p
-adic
L
-functions

Our application of the Beilinson–Flach elements to the conjecture of Perrin-Riou and other problems stems in large part from being able to realize various
p
-adic
L
-functions as specialisations of the elements
{\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L)
and
{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)
.

Comparison with cyclotomic
L
-functions.
From Theorem
5.10
together with Proposition
5.4
we conclude the following:

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
. Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F,g}
, and let
\gamma\in V_{F,g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in V_{F,g^{\prime}}

such that
\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0
and
(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}\neq 0
.
There exists a constant
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in F^{\times}
such that

(i)

{\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L)\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{v}-1)=c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g/L)\in\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}={\mathscr{R}}/(\gamma_{v}-1){\mathscr{R}}
,

(ii)

if (irr
Q
) holds,
\omega\in S_{g,{\mathcal{O}}}
is good, and
\gamma\in T_{g,{\mathcal{O}}}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime},{\mathcal{O}}}

are such that
\gamma^{\pm}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}
-basis of
T_{{\mathcal{O}},g}^{\pm}
and
(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}
-basis of
T_{{\mathcal{O}},g^{\prime}}^{\pm}
,
then
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
.

In part (i),
{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g/L)
is the cyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function for
g
over
L
as in
Section
3.4.3
.

Proof.

By comparing Theorems
5.10
and
3.13
we see that

{\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L)\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{v}-1)=c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g){\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha,\omega^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})=c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g/L),

where
\omega^{\prime}
is the image of
\omega
under the isomorphism (
2.23
) and

c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=\frac{\Omega_{\omega,+}\Omega_{\omega^{\prime},+}\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})}{\pi^{2}(-i)2^{3}\Omega_{g}}.

Here
\Omega_{\omega,\pm}
(resp.
\Omega_{\omega^{\prime},\pm}
) are the periods determined by
\omega
and
\gamma^{\pm}

(resp. by
\omega^{\prime}
and
(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}
) as in Section
2.2.7
.
From the definitions of the periods and Lemmas
2.13
and
2.6
we have

\Omega_{\omega,+}\sim_{F^{\times}}\Omega_{g}^{+},\ \ \Omega_{\omega^{\prime},+}\sim_{F^{\times}}\Omega_{g^{\prime}}^{+}\sim_{F^{\times}}\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}\Omega_{g}^{-},\ \ \text{and}\ \ \Omega_{g}\sim_{F^{\times}}(4\pi^{2}i)^{-1}\Omega_{g}^{+}\Omega_{g}^{-}.

So
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\sim_{F^{\times}}1
, proving part (i). If (irr
Q
) holds and
\omega
,
\gamma
, and
\gamma^{\prime}
are as in part (ii), then each
\sim_{F^{\times}}
can be replaced with
\sim_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}}
, yielding part (ii).
∎

Comparison with the
L
-function of Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna.

If the pair
(g,L)
satisfies the Heegner hypothesis:

\ell\mid N\implies\text{$\ell$ splits in $L$},

(Heeg)

then Bertolini, Darmon, and Prasanna
[
11
]
constructed an anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function interpolating the special values

L(1,g,\psi)
for
\psi
a Hecke character with infinity type
(-n,n)
:

Theorem 5.14
.

Suppose (
Heeg
) holds. There exists
{\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)\in\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\mathrm{ac}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}[\![\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}]\!]

such that for
\chi:\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
with
\chi(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}^{h_{p}})=\epsilon(\gamma)^{n}
for some positive integer

n>0
,
n\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ p-1
,

\phi_{\chi^{-1}}({\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L))=(1-a(p)\psi_{\chi}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}p^{-1}+\psi_{\chi}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-2}p^{-1})^{2}\cdot w_{L}\frac{n!(n-1)!(2\pi)^{2n-1}}{4D_{L}^{n}}\Omega_{p}^{4n}\frac{L(1,g,\psi_{\chi})}{\Omega_{\infty}^{4n}}.

Here again
(\Omega_{p},\Omega_{\infty})
are the CM periods as in Theorem
4.19

and

\psi_{\chi}
is the algebraic Hecke character of
L
with infinity type
(-n,n)
such that

\sigma_{\psi_{\chi}}
is the composition

G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\chi}}{{\rightarrow}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
.
In fact,
{\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)
is only constructed as a continuous function in
[
11
]
. That it is in fact a measure
(and in particular an element of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}[\![\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}]\!]
) is shown in
[
32
]
.

Remark 5.15
.

The newform
g
is not required to be ordinary in
[
11
]
: Theorem
5.14
holds
in the both the ordinary and supersingular cases.

Of crucial importance to us is the following remarkable formula of Bertolini, Darmon, and Prasanna
[
11
, Thm. 5.13]
, which
generalizes and extends a theorem of Rubin
[
122
]
for the CM case:

Theorem 5.16
.

Suppose (
Heeg
) holds.
The image of
{\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)
under the specialisation corresponding
to the trivial character
{\bf 1}:\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
(which is
the augmentation map
\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{v}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
) is

\phi_{\bf 1}({\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L))=(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})^{2}(\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L}))^{2},

where
\log_{\omega_{g}}
is the
p
-adic logarithm on
J_{0}(N)
for the differential
\omega_{g}
and
y_{L}\in J_{0}(N)(L)
is a Heegner point.

Comparing Theorems
5.14
and
5.11
yields:

Proposition 5.17
.

Suppose (
Heeg
) holds.
The image of
{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)
under the map

\phi_{\mathrm{ac}}:{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{ac}
induced from the homomorphism

\Gamma_{L}^{v}\times\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}
,
\gamma_{v}\mapsto\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}^{2}
and

\gamma\mapsto\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}^{h_{p}}
, equals
-T_{\mathrm{ac}}^{2}
times the image of
{\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)
under the involution

\iota_{\mathrm{ac}}
of
\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}
induced by
\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}\mapsto\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}^{-1}
:

\phi_{\mathrm{ac}}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L))=-((1+T_{\mathrm{ac}})^{2}-1)\cdot\iota_{\mathrm{ac}}({\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)).

Recall that if the Heegner hypothesis (
Heeg
) holds, then the root number

\epsilon(g/L)
equals
-1
. When this root number is
+1
we have:

Proposition 5.18
.

Suppose
\epsilon(g/L)=+1
. Let
\phi_{\mathrm{ac}}
be
as in Proposition
5.17
. Then

\phi_{\mathrm{ac}}({\mathscr{L}}^{Gr}(g/L))=0.

Proof.

Let
\chi:\Gamma_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
be a character such that

\chi(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}^{h_{p}})=\epsilon(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})^{n}
for some integer
n>0
,
n\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ (p-1)
.
Then it follows from Theorem
5.11
that
\phi_{\chi}(\phi_{\mathrm{ac}}({\mathscr{L}}^{Gr}(g/L)))
is a multiple
of
L(1,g,\chi)
. The hypothesis that
\epsilon(g/L)=+1
together with
(p,ND_{L})=1
implies that the root number
\varepsilon(1,g,\chi)=-1
(see
[
42
, Lem. 3.1(2)]
).
In particular,
L(1,g,\chi)=0
.
Since the (kernels of) specialisations at such characters
\chi
are Zariski dense in
{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}]\!]
, this implies that
\phi_{\mathrm{ac}}({\mathscr{L}}^{Gr}(g/L))=0
.
∎

5.4.
First applications:
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
satisfies (
Ind
) and
{\mathcal{BF}}(g/L)
arises from
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}

In this section we prove the following theorems:

Theorem 5.19
.

The
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L}]
-module
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
satisfies (
Ind
).

Theorem 5.20
.

The Beilinson–Flach element
{\mathcal{BF}}(g/L)
belongs to the submodule

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
of

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

Our proofs of Theorems
5.19
and
5.20
are intertwined.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subset{\mathbb{I}}\subsetneq\widetilde{\mathbb{I}}
be an induced lattice as in Lemma
4.18
.
By Proposition
4.13
(iii),(iv), the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\![T_{v}]\!]
-module
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
is
annihilated by
T_{v}
. It follows that as a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-modules
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
,
{\mathbb{I}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
, and

\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{I}}
are all quotients of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(\chi_{L})
.
Consequently, as
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=0=H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda(\chi_{L}))
,
the inclusions
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subset{\mathbb{I}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
induce inclusions

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda),

by which we view the first two as submodules of the third.

Let
{\mathcal{BF}}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
be the image of
{\mathcal{BF}}(g/L)
under the map induced by the inclusion

{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
.
The image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}})
in
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)^{-}\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

is the image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}}(g/L))
under the induced map
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)^{-}\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)^{-}\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
So it follows from Lemma
5.2
that

{\mathcal{BF}}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=\{c\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(c)=0\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)^{-}\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\}.

As a step toward proving the above theorems, we show:

Proposition 5.21
.

The Beilinson–Flach element
{\mathcal{BF}}
satisfies

{\mathcal{BF}}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=\{c\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(c)=0\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)^{-}\hat{\otimes}({\mathbb{I}}/{\mathbb{I}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\}.

Moreover, if (
Ind
) does not hold, then
{\mathcal{BF}}\in T_{v}H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Since
T_{v}
annihilates
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{I}}
, we certainly have
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}=T_{v}{\mathcal{BF}}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
We also have that
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
acts trivially on
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/{\mathbb{I}}
, from which it follows easily that

H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/({\mathbb{I}}+\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=0
and hence that

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}({\mathbb{I}}/{\mathbb{I}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
Consequently,
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}
belongs to the submodule
H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

Since
{\mathbb{I}}={\mathbb{I}}_{v}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{I}}_{v}

we have
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=H^{1}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
by Shapiro’s Lemma, which yields
an identification
H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
, where

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=\{c\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(c)=0\in H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
This induces
an injection

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)/T_{v}H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

(5.13)

after choosing an isomormophism
{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}
(and hence an isomorphism
{\mathbb{I}}_{v}/T_{v}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
).
Let
\overline{{\mathcal{BF}}}^{\prime}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
denote the image of
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}
under (
5.13
).

We now consider the image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(\overline{{\mathcal{BF}}}^{\prime})
under the Coleman map
Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}
.
Since either
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{I}}_{v}
or
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}=T_{v}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}_{v}
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}^{+}
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(L_{v},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}.}
(
5.13
)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/T_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/T_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Col_{\eta_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(**)}

(5.14)

Here we have fixed the isomorphism
{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}
to restrict to the isomorphism
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}
determined
by
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
as in the proof of Proposition
5.4
. This in turn induces an isomorphism
{\mathbb{I}}_{v}/T_{v}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
which we take as the isomorphism for (
5.13
). The lower veritical arrows in (
5.14
) are induced from the inclusion

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{H}}^{+}\subset{\mathbb{I}}_{v}
. In particular,
(*)
and
(**)
are both the identity map if
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}_{v}
(so if (
Ind
) holds) and are both the zero
map if
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}=T_{v}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}
(so if (
Ind
) does not hold).
Since
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime})\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is the image of

T_{v}{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\cdot{\mathcal{BF}}(g/L))\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
, it follows from the commutativity of (
5.14
) that

Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}({\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(\overline{{\mathcal{BF}}}^{\prime}))=0
.

By Theorem
3.11
,
H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is a torsion-free
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
-module and

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is a free
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-module of rank one.
As
Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}\circ{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}
is non-zero on

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
(see the proof of Theorem
3.12
), we have an injection

Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}\circ{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Since
Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}({\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(\overline{{\mathcal{BF}}}^{\prime}))=0
, it then follows that
\overline{{\mathcal{BF}}}^{\prime}=0
and hence that

{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}\in T_{v}H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
by (
5.13
).
As
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
has no
T_{v}
-torsion (since
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(\chi_{L}))\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=0
),
it follows that
{\mathcal{BF}}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
, as claimed.

If furthermore (
Ind
) does not hold, then both (*) and (**) are the zero map, and the same arguments now applied to
{\mathcal{BF}}
in place of
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}
show
that
{\mathcal{BF}}\in T_{v}H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
∎

We can now complete the proof of Theorems
5.19
and
5.20
.
If (
Ind
) holds, then there is nothing more to prove: Theorem
5.20
follows from Proposition
5.21
.
So suppose (
Ind
) does not hold. Then by the same proposition,
{\mathcal{BF}}\in T_{v}H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
We will use the Explicit Reciprocity Law II to deduce a contradiction.

If
{\mathcal{BF}}\in T_{v}H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{I}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
, then

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}})\in T_{v}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}({\mathbb{I}}/{\mathbb{I}}_{v})\otimes\Lambda)
. On the other hand, since
{\mathbb{I}}
is as in Lemma
4.18
,
the inclusion
{\mathbb{I}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
induces an isomorphism
{\mathbb{I}}/{\mathbb{I}}_{v}\simeq T_{v}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{+})
. Consequently,

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}})\in T_{v}^{2}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}\otimes\Lambda)
. It follows that we must have

{\mathscr{L}}^{Gr}_{p}(g/L)={\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}}))\in T_{v}^{2}{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}.

(5.15)

Since this must hold for
any
ordinary newform
g
if (
Ind
) does not hold, the following proposition yields the desired contradiction and hence completes the proofs
of Theorems
5.19
and
5.20
.

Proposition 5.22
.

There exists a positive integer
N
and a newform
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
such that

(i)

(N,p)=1
,

(ii)

g
is
p
-ordinary, that is, there exists a prime
\lambda\mid p
of the Hecke field
F_{g}
such that
a_{p}(g)
is a
\lambda
-adic unit,

(iii)

{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)\not\in T_{v}^{2}{\mathscr{R}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
.

The proof of this proposition is contained in the next section.

5.4.1.
An auxiliary newform

To prove Proposition
5.22

we first prove:

Proposition 5.23
.

There exists a positive integer
N
and a newform
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
such that

(i)

p\nmid N
,

(ii)

every prime
\ell\mid N
splits in
L
,

(iii)

g
is
p
-ordinary, that is, there exists a prime
\lambda\mid p
of the Hecke field
F_{g}
such that
a_{p}(g)
is a
\lambda
-adic unit,

(iv)

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g_{/L})=1
.

Note that condition (ii) is just the usual Heegner condition for the pair
(g,L)
.

Proof.

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field with discriminant
D_{K}
coprime to
pD_{L}
and such that
p
splits in
K
and every prime

\ell\mid D_{K}
splits in
L
.
Let
\lambda
be a Hecke character over
K
with infinity type
(-1,0)
such that

•

\lambda
is conjugate-dual:
\lambda^{\tau}=\lambda^{-1}|\cdot|_{K}
, where the superscript
\tau
denotes composition with the action of complex conjugation

\tau
on
K
,

•

p\nmid\mathrm{cond^{r}}(\lambda)
, and

•

the conductor of
\lambda
is only divisible by primes whose residue characteristic is split in
L
.

In particular, the CM modular modular form corresponding to the Hecke character
\lambda
satisfies the classical Heegner hypothesis with respect to
L
,
and

\epsilon(1/2,\lambda)\cdot\epsilon(1/2,\lambda\chi_{L})=-1
.

Let
\ell\nmid 2p\cdot D_{K}D_{L}N(\mathrm{cond^{r}}(\lambda))
be any prime that splits in both
K
and
L
.
Let
\mathfrak{X}_{K,\ell}^{ac}
denote the set of finite order anticyclotomic Hecke characters over
K
having

\ell
-power conductor. If
\epsilon(\frac{1}{2},\lambda)=+1
, then by the main result of
[
119
]
(also see
[
57
, §1]
)

L(1,\lambda\cdot\chi)\cdot L^{\prime}(1,\lambda\chi_{L}\cdot\chi)\neq 0

for all but finitely many
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K,\ell}^{ac}
.
Otherwise,

L^{\prime}(1,\lambda\cdot\chi)\cdot L(1,\lambda\chi_{L}\cdot\chi)\neq 0

for all but finitely many
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K,\ell}^{ac}
.
In either case, let
\chi_{0}\in\mathfrak{X}_{K,\ell}^{ac}
be a Hecke character for which the non-vanishing holds.
We may then take
g
to be the CM modular form corresponding to the Hecke character
\lambda\chi_{0}
.
∎

Remark 5.24
.

It is natural to ask: Does there exist a non-CM elliptic newform
g
satisfying Proposition
5.23
(iv)?

We now explain how Proposition
5.22
follows from Proposition
5.23
.

Proof of Proposition
5.22
.

Let
g
be as in Proposition
5.23
. Since the pair
(g,L)
satisfies the Heegner hypothesis by
Proposition
5.23
(ii), it folllows from Proposition
5.23
(iv) and the Gross–Zagier formula for the pair
(g,L)
that
the image of the Heegner point
y_{L}\in J_{0}(N)(L)
has infinite order in the abelian variety quotient associated to
g
.
In particular, the
p
-adic logarithm
\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L})
is non-zero. Combined with the formula
of Theorem
5.16
, this implies that
T_{\mathrm{ac}}\nmid{\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)
. On the other hand, Proposition
5.17

implies that if
T_{v}^{2}\mid{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)
then
T_{\mathrm{ac}}\mid{\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)
. Together, this shows

T_{v}^{2}\nmid{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)
, proving Proposition
5.22
.
∎

5.5.
A two-variable zeta element

In light of Theorems
5.19
and
5.20
, we can use the Beilinson–Flach element
\mathcal{BF}(g/L)
to define a
two-variable zeta element for
g
, as explained in the following.

5.5.1.
The zeta element
{\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)

By Theorem
5.19
, (
Ind
) holds. In particular
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
is identified with the induction from
G_{L}
to
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
of the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L}]
-module

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
. Recall that
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module of rank one on which
G_{L}
acts via the canonical character
\Psi_{L}^{v}
and, furthermore,
we have a preferred
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-isomorphism
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})
arising from the map
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
. The latter determines via
Shapiro’s Lemma identifications

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

of
{\mathscr{R}}
-modules.
Similarly,
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is identified with
H^{1}(L_{v},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

and
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}^{+})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is identified with
H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
,
and also with
T(1)
replaced by
T^{\pm}(1)
.
It follows that we have an identification

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

where the subscripts ‘
{\mathrm{rel}}
’ and ‘
{\mathrm{ord}}
’ on the right-hand side denote the submodule of classes

c
such that no condition is imposed on
{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(c)
but we require
{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(c)\in H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{v}^{L}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
. We may thus view

{\mathcal{BF}}(g/L)
as an element of
H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{v}^{L}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

We now consider the composition of isomorphism

\Gamma_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{L}^{v}\times\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma_{v}\mapsto\gamma_{v}^{-1}}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}\Gamma_{L}^{v}\times\Gamma,

(5.16)

this determines an isomorphism
\Lambda_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda

and hence also isomorphism
\theta:\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{R}}
and

\theta^{\mathrm{ur}}:\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.
The isomorphism (
5.16
) also induces

T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}(\Psi_{L}^{-1})\xrightarrow{\sim}T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda(\Psi^{-1}),

which is compatible with
\theta
. We thus obtain an identification

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\simeq H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda),

that is compatible with
\theta
, where
G_{L}
acts on
\Lambda_{L}
in the left-hand side via the inverse of the canonical character, and
the subscripts ‘
{\mathrm{rel}}
’ and ‘
{\mathrm{ord}}
’ on the left-hand side denote the submodule of classes
c\in H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})
such that
{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(c)\in H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})
. We let

{\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)\in H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})

be identified with
{\mathcal{BF}}(g/L)
under the preceding isomorphism.
This is the two-variable zeta element associated with
g
and
L
in the ordinary case.

We let
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}:H^{1}(L_{v},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}

be the composition

{\mathcal{C}}_{v}:H^{1}(L_{v},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\simeq H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}{\mathscr{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta^{-1}}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

This is a
\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
-injection. Let

{\mathcal{L}}_{v}(g/L)={\mathcal{C}}_{v}({\mathrm{loc}}_{v}({\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)))\in\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

Note that
{\mathcal{L}}_{v}(g/L)=\theta^{-1}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L))
.

We similarly let
{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}:H^{1}(K_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}
be the composition

{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}:H^{1}(K_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\simeq H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\frac{1}{T_{v}}{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},-1}}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}.

Note that the inclusion
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
induces
an isomorphism
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}=T_{v}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}
, and so
{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}
maps

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
into
T_{v}{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
. In particular,
the middle arrow of the composition defining
{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}
is well-defined.
We also let

{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)={\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}({\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)))\in\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}.

So
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)=\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},-1}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L))
.

5.5.2.
Connections with cyclotomic
L
-functions and cyclotomic zeta elements

From Propositions
5.4
and
5.13
we immediately conclude:

Proposition 5.25
.

(i)

The reduction of
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
modulo
\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1
equals the composition

H^{1}(L_{v},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{v}-1)}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}H^{1}(L_{v},T^{-}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

(ii)

For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
,
\gamma\in V_{F,g}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in V_{F,g^{\prime}}
,
and
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in F^{\times}
as in
Proposition
5.13
,

{\mathcal{L}}_{v}(g/L)\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)=c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g/L)\in\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}=\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}/(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

We exploit this proposition to prove that the Beilinson–Kato element
\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})

is essentially the cyclotomic specialisation of the two-variable zeta element
{\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)
.

Theorem 5.26
.

Let
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
. Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F,g}
, and let
\gamma\in V_{F,g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in V_{F,g^{\prime}}

such that
\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0
and
(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}\neq 0
, and let
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in F^{\times}
be as in Proposition
5.13
.

(i)

The image of
{\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)
under the map

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes\Lambda_{L})\rightarrow H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

induced by the projection
\Lambda_{L}/(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)\Lambda_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda

equals
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}
.

(ii)

If (Van
Q
) holds,
\omega\in S_{g,{\mathcal{O}}}
is good, and
\gamma\in T_{g,{\mathcal{O}}}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime},{\mathcal{O}}}

are such that
\gamma^{\pm}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}
-basis of
T_{{\mathcal{O}},g}
and
(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}
-basis of
T_{{\mathcal{O}},g^{\prime}}^{\pm}

(so
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
),
then the equality in
(i) holds in
H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[\frac{1}{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
5.25
(i) that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes\Lambda_{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(L_{v},T^{-}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(L_{v},T^{-}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)}

commutes.
By Theorem
3.11
(a),
H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

is a free
(\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-module of rank one. Since

Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}(\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}})\neq 0
(cf. the proof of Theorem
3.12
(a)),
it follows that the composition of maps on the right of the diagram is an injection. So to prove part (i) of the theorem it suffices
to prove that
{\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)
and
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}\mathbf{z}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}
have the same image in the

If (Van

From Propositions

.

(i)

is

.

This proposition allows us to relate the image of

under Perrin-Riou’s regulator map (or ‘big logarithm’) to
Heegner points, providing a key link in our subsequent proof of the Perrin-Riou Conjecture.

Recall that Perrin-Riou’s regulator map for
H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}})
is the composition

\begin{array}[]{ccl}{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}:H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Psi_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}(g)\mapsto\varepsilon(g)^{-1}\Psi_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}(g)}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle res}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{ur}},T^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda^{\mathrm{ac}}_{L})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}}_{T^{+}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&D(T^{+})\hat{\otimes}W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}},\end{array}

where for the third map we have used that as a
G_{L_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{ur}}}
-module
\Lambda^{\mathrm{ac}}_{L}
is naturally isomorphic to the cyclotomic
algebra
\Lambda
.
Let

{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{g}=([\omega_{g},-]\otimes id\otimes id)\circ{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}:H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}})\rightarrow\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\mathrm{ac}}.

From the specialisation properties of
{\mathcal{L}}_{T^{+}}
(see
[
97
, Thm. B.5]
) it follows that

\phi_{\bf 1}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{g}^{PR}=(1-\alpha/p)(1-1/\alpha)^{-1}\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}},

(5.17)

where

denotes the Bloch–Kato logarithm for
T^{+}
and
\eta_{g}=\eta_{\omega_{g}}
(so

\log_{BK}(-)=\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}(-)\cdot\eta_{g}\in D_{\mathrm{cris}}(V^{+})
).

Lemma 5.29
.

There exists a unit
U_{L}\in(\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\times}
depending only on
L
such that

U_{L}\cdot{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{+}-1)={\mathcal{L}}_{g}^{PR}.

In particular,
u_{L}\cdot\phi_{\bf 1}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}=(1-\alpha/p)(1-1/\alpha)^{-1}\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}
,
for
u_{L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}},\times}
the image of
U_{L}
under the specialization map

\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}/(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)\Lambda^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.19
,
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\mathbb{T}}^{+}\oplus c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
. In particular,
c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
projects isomorphically onto

. Let
\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\in{\mathbb{T}}^{+}_{1}
be the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-basis determined by
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
. Then

c\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
is a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-basis of
c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}
. Let

{\mathcal{U}}_{L}=\frac{1}{T_{v}}{\mathcal{H}}_{v}\cdot\eta_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{+})\in(\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}
and let

U_{L}={\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{-1}\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{+}-1)\in(\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\times}.

The lemma then follows directly from the identification
H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})=H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T^{+}(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
determined by the basis
\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
and comparing the definitions of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{g}^{PR}
;
the conclusion for
\phi_{\bf 1}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}
the follows from (
6.11
).
∎

From Lemma

and Proposition

.

in

Remark 5.31
.

It is natural to ask whether the units
U_{L}
and especially
u_{L}
can be explicitly identified. This might be
possible by an explicit comparision of
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
with a familiy of CM motives, but we do not pursue this here as it is not needed for our purposes
or applications. However, in the course of our proof of the Perrin-Riou’s Conjecture (see section
7
below), we do show that
u_{L}
is
algebraic and, for suitably chosen
L
, belongs to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}^{\times}
. The proof of this involves
auxiliary input from the arithmetic of CM forms, in the form of a proof of a version of Perrin-Riou’s conjecture for certain ordinary CM forms
(see Appendix
A
).

6.
Zeta elements over imaginary quadratic fields: the supersingular case

In this section we construct the plus/minus
p
-adic zeta element for a weight two newform for supersingular primes
p

and describe its explicit reciprocity laws.

The construction relies on the Beilinson–Flach element associated to
p
-stabilisations of the newform and the canonical CM Hida family constructed
by Loeffler and Zerbes
[
98
]
, as initiated in their work with Lei
[
93
]
,
[
94
]
, as well as its variants by Buyukboduk and Lei
[
28
]
. Our approach is similar to the ordinary case treated in the previous section. By definition, the Beilinson–Flach element arises from the Tate lattice of étale cohomology of a tower of open modular curves.
The principal result of this section is that it lives in the Tate lattice of closed modular curves (cf. Theorem
6.22
).
Based on (
Ind
), established in the previous section,
we then define the sought after zeta element.

We continue with the notation and conventions introduced in §§
2
–
4
.
Throughout this section we assume the newform
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
to be supersingular at
p
.

6.1.
The Beilinson–Flach element

For
M={\mathbb{T}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1},{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
, or
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}_{1}
as in §
4
, let

H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\hat{\otimes}M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=H^{1}(G_{{\mathbb{Q}},\Sigma},T(1)\hat{\otimes}M\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

where
\Sigma
is any finite set of primes containing all
\ell\mid ND_{L}p\infty
.

Let
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
be a root of the Hecke polynomial

.
Let

.

Since
a_{p}(g)=0
, the Hecke eigenform
g_{\gamma}
is specialisation of a Coleman family
{\bf g}_{\gamma}
.

Recall that the canonical CM family

corresponds to a branch of a Hida family

of tame level

and it follows from the definition of

(6.1)

More precisely, the left-hand side of (

) is just
{\mathbb{H}}
and the map the quotient modulo
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-torsion.

Fix an integer

such that
(c,6ND_{L}p)=1
.
Let

{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}^{\gamma}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)})

be the base layer of the Beilinson–Flach Euler system

associated to the pair

associated to the pair

Remark 6.1
.

In

]

[

,
[

on
p

6.1.2.
Some more Iwasawa cohomology

Recall that

Proof of Lemma
6.6
.

In view of the definition of the
\circ
-Coleman maps it suffices to prove analogous compatibility for the Coleman maps
{\mathscr{C}}_{\gamma}
.

The map
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
factors through a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-isomorphism
D({\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1}))\cong{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})

(see also
[
52
, Prop. 1.7.6]
). Since

is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module of rank one,
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
determines a
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-basis of

{\mathbb{H}}^{+}(\epsilon^{-1}\Psi_{D}^{-1})

\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}

It then follows from functoriality that
{\mathscr{C}}_{\gamma}\,\ \mathrm{mod}\ \gamma_{v}-1
factors as
the composition

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\mathcal{F}^{-+}(T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1})\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)})\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\mathcal{F}^{-}T(1)\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathcal{F}^{-}T(1)}}}{{\rightarrow}}D(\mathcal{F}^{-}{\bf D}(T(1))\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{\eta}_{g}\otimes id}}{{\rightarrow}}J_{g}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)},

where the first map is just the projection induced by the isomorphism
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}/(\gamma_{v}-1){\mathbb{H}}^{+}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
from above.
The characterization (
6.3
) of
\tilde{\eta}_{g}
in terms of
\eta_{\omega_{g}}
combined with
the fact that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathcal{F}^{-}T(1)}
is just Perrin-Riou’s ‘big logarithm map’ for
V(1)

shows – by comparing with the definition of the Coleman map
Col_{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}
in terms of this big logarithm map – that the above displayed composition of maps equals

\frac{1}{\gamma(1-p\gamma^{-2})(1-\gamma^{-2})\lambda_{N}(g)}Col_{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}
.
∎

6.3.
The explicit reciprocity law II

The second explicit reciprocity law essentially arises from exchanging the roles of

and

in the preceding section.

Result

As we explain below, there is an injective
{\mathscr{R}}
-homomorphism

Theorem 6.10

(Expicit Reciprocity Law II)
.

For
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
, we have

\phi_{\chi}({\mathscr{L}}_{p,c}^{Gr}(g/L))=\lambda_{D_{L}}(h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0})(c^{2}-\psi_{\zeta}^{2}(c)\chi_{L}(c)\langle c\rangle^{2n-m+1)}){\mathcal{E}}(\chi)\frac{n!(n-1)!}{\pi^{2n+1}(-i)^{m-1}2^{2n+m+1}}\frac{L(1+n,g,h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n})}{\langle h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0}\rangle},

where

{\mathcal{E}}(\chi)=\begin{cases}\frac{(1-\frac{p^{n}}{\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\alpha})(1-\frac{p^{n}}{\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\beta})(1-\frac{p^{m}\alpha}{p^{n+1}\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\alpha})(1-\frac{p^{m}\beta}{p^{n+1}\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\alpha})}{(1-p^{m-1}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))(1-p^{m}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))}&\zeta=1,n\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ p-1\\
\frac{(p^{t+1}/\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n}))^{2}(p^{2n-1}/\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{t+1}}{(1-p^{m-1}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))(1-p^{m}\chi_{1}^{-2}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))}&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Here

is the newform of level

and weight

Here

6.4.
Integral normalisations

We introduce certain normalisations of the Beilinson–Flach elements, and regulator maps. These will be subsequently used in regards to zeta elements, and reciprocity laws.

Put

{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}=c_{g}\cdot{\mathscr{C}}_{\circ}:H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda={\mathscr{R}}.

for

).
We put

{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}={\mathcal{H}}_{v}\cdot{\mathscr{L}}_{\circ}:H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\otimes_{\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}^{v}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{v}\rightarrow{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}},

where
{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda={\mathscr{R}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}^{v}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}^{v}
.

We also normalise the elements
{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}^{\circ}(g/L)
. Put
r_{c}=\log_{p}(c)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
and

\mathbf{c}=(c^{2}-\chi_{L}(c)\otimes\langle c\rangle\gamma_{v}^{-r_{c}}\otimes\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}^{2r_{c}})\in{\mathscr{R}}.

Modulo the maximal ideal of
{\mathscr{R}}
,
\mathbf{c}
is congruent to
c^{2}-\chi_{L}(c)
.
As
p
is odd, we can therefore choose
c
so that
\mathbf{c}\in{\mathscr{R}}^{\times}
. Henceforth we assume that
c
satisfies this.
Define

\mathcal{BF}^{\circ}(g/L)=\mathbf{c}^{-1}\cdot{{}_{c}\mathcal{BF}^{\circ}(g/L)}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda),

and

{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{\circ}(g/L)={\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathcal{BF}^{\circ}(g/L)))\in{\mathscr{R}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)={\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathcal{BF}^{\circ}(g/L)))\in{\mathscr{R}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}.

We record the following versions of the earlier explicit reciprocity laws (Theorems
6.4
and
6.10
).

Theorem 6.16

(Explicit Reciprocity Law I
′
)
.

The element
{\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L)\in{\mathscr{R}}
satisfies:
For
\chi\in\Xi^{(I)}
with
\chi_{1}=1
,

\phi_{\chi}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}(g/L))=e_{p}^{\circ}(\zeta)^{2}\frac{L(1,g\otimes\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})L(1,g\otimes\chi_{L}\psi_{\zeta}^{-1})}{\pi^{2}(-i)2^{3}\Omega_{g}},

where

\Omega_{g}=\frac{\langle g,g\rangle}{\tilde{c}_{g}}

and
e_{p}^{\circ}(\zeta)
is as in Theorem
3.16
.

Given
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
let

\psi_{\chi}
be the algebraic Hecke character of
L
with infinity type
(n-m,n)
and such that

\sigma_{\psi_{\chi}}
is the composition

G_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{L}^{v}\times\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\chi_{1}\times\chi_{2}^{-1}}}{{\rightarrow}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Then the second explicit reciprocity law can be rewritten as follows.

Theorem 6.17

(Explicit Reciprocity Law II
′
)
.

The element
{\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)\in{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
satisfies:
For
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
,

\phi_{\chi}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L))=-w_{L}(\chi_{1}(\gamma_{v})-1){\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\chi)\frac{n!(n-1)!\pi^{2m-2n-1}}{2^{2n-2m+2}D_{L}^{m/2}}\cdot\Omega_{p}^{2m}\frac{L(1,g,\psi_{\chi})}{\Omega_{\infty}^{2m}},

where

{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\chi)=\begin{cases}(1-a(p)\psi_{\chi}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}p^{-1}+\psi_{\chi}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-2}p^{-1})^{2}&\zeta=1,n\equiv 0\ \mathrm{mod}\ p-1\\
(p^{t+1}/\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n}))^{2}(p^{2n-1}/\chi_{1}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{t+1}&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Here
(\Omega_{p},\Omega_{\infty})
are the CM periods as in Theorem
4.19
,
and

L(s,g,\psi_{\chi})=\sum_{\mathfrak{a},(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{f}_{\psi_{\chi}})=1}a_{g}(N(\mathfrak{a}))\psi_{\chi}(x_{\mathfrak{a}})N(\mathfrak{a})^{-s}=L(s,g,h_{\chi_{1}}^{0},\psi_{\zeta}^{-1}\omega^{n}).

The key to this rewrite of the second explicit reciprocity law is the following formula (essentially due to Shimura – see
[
67
, §7]
), which expresses
{\langle h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0}\rangle}

in terms of
L(1,\chi_{1}/\chi_{1}^{c})
:

{\langle h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0},h_{v,\chi_{1}}^{0}\rangle}=\frac{h_{L}m!D_{L}^{1/2}}{w_{L}2^{m-1}(2\pi)^{m+1}}L(1,\chi_{1}/\chi_{1}^{c}).

Combined with the interpolation formula for the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathscr{L}}_{v}(L)
from Theorem
4.19
, this easily yields
the formula in Theorem
6.17
.

Remark 6.18
.

The class
\mathcal{BF}^{\circ}(g/L)
does not depend on the choice of an auxiliary integer
c
.
This follows from
the second explicit reciprocity law, the injectivity of
{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}
, and the Zariski density of the specialisations
\phi_{\chi}
,
\chi\in\Xi^{(II)}
.

6.4.1.

Our application of the Beilinson–Flach elements to the conjecture of Perrin-Riou and other problems stems in large part from being able to realize various

-functions.
From Theorem

together with Proposition

we conclude the following:

Proposition 6.19
.

Let
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
. Let

, and let

and

\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0

.

In part (i),

over

.

Comparison with the

-function of Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna.

Comparing Theorems

and

yields:

Proposition 6.20
.

Suppose (

) holds.
The image of

Recall that if the Heegner hypothesis (

equals

.

6.5.
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}(g/L)
arises from
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}

Theorem 6.22
.

The Beilinson–Flach element
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}(g/L)
belongs to the submodule

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
of

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.13
(iii),(iv), the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\![T_{v}]\!]
-module
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
is
annihilated by
T_{v}
. It follows that as a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-modules
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}

is a quotient of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(\chi_{L})
.
Consequently, as
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=0=H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda(\chi_{L})),

the inclusion
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
induce an inclusion

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda),

by which we view the former as a submodule of the latter.

Let
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
be the image of
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}(g/L)
under the map induced by the inclusion

{\mathbb{H}}_{1}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
.
Put

H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=\frac{H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}{H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)},

where we utilise the fact that
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

injects into
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
Then the image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ})
in
H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

is the image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}({\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}(g/L))
under the induced map
H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{H}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\rightarrow H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda).

So it follows from Lemma
6.3
that

{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=\{c\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(c)=0\in H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\}.

Since
T_{v}
annihilates
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
, we have

{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}:=T_{v}\cdot{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda).

As
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
acts trivially on
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
, note that

H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}/{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T\hat{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.
Consequently,
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}
belongs to the submodule
H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

We have
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=H^{1}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
by (
Ind
) (cf. Theorem
5.19
).
This induces
an identification

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

where

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)=\{c\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\ :\ {\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(c)=0\in H^{1}_{/\circ}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\}
.
In turn, we have
an injection

H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)/T_{v}H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

(6.8)

after choosing an isomormophism
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\simeq\Lambda_{L}^{v}
(and hence an isomorphism
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}/T_{v}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
).
Let
\overline{{\mathcal{BF}}}^{\prime}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
denote the image of
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\prime}
under (

).

We now consider the image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(\overline{{\mathcal{BF}}}^{\prime})
under the Coleman map
Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}^{\circ}
.
Since
{\mathbb{H}}^{+}={\mathbb{T}}^{+}
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{\mathrm{rel},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)}

(

)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/T_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/T_{v}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*)}

(6.9)

Here we have fixed the isomorphism

to restrict to the isomorphism

. In particular,

and

are both the identity map.
Since

is the image of

, it follows from the commutativity of (

) that

.

By Theorem

,

is a free

-module of rank one.
As

-torsion

Remark 6.23
.

The proof does not rely on the second reciprocity law.

.

.

6.6.
Two-variable zeta element

In light of Theorem
6.22
and (
Ind
), the Beilinson–Flach element
\mathcal{BF}^{\circ}(g/L)
leads to a
two-variable zeta element for
g
, as explained below.

6.6.1.
The zeta element
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\circ}(g/L)

By (
Ind
),
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
is identified with the induction from
G_{L}
to
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
of the
\Lambda_{L}^{v}[G_{L}]
-module

{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
. Recall that
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}
is a free
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-module of rank one on which
G_{L}
acts via the canonical character
\Psi_{L}^{v}
and, furthermore,
we have a preferred
\Lambda_{L}^{v}
-isomorphism
{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})
arising from the map
\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
. The latter determines via
Shapiro’s Lemma identifications

H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

of
{\mathscr{R}}
-modules.
Similarly,
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is identified with
H^{1}(L_{v},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

and
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}(c\cdot{\mathbb{T}}^{+})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
is identified with
H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
,
and also with
H^{1}
replaced by
H^{1}_{\circ}
.
It follows that we have an identification

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},\circ}({\mathbb{Q}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)

where the subscripts ‘
{\mathrm{rel}}
’ and ‘
\circ
’ on the right-hand side denote the submodule of classes

c
such that no condition is imposed on
{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}(c)
but we require
{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(c)\in H^{1}_{\circ}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
. We may thus view

{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}(g/L)
as an element of
H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
.

We now consider the composition of isomorphism

\Gamma_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{L}^{v}\times\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma_{v}\mapsto\gamma_{v}^{-1}}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}\Gamma_{L}^{v}\times\Gamma,

(6.10)

this determines an isomorphism
\Lambda_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{L}^{v}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda

and hence also isomorphism
\theta:\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{R}}
and

\theta^{\mathrm{ur}}:\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.
The isomorphism (
6.10
) also induces

T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}(\Psi_{L}^{-1})\xrightarrow{\sim}T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda(\Psi^{-1}),

which is compatible with
\theta
. We thus obtain an identification

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\simeq H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{v}(\Psi_{L}^{v})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda),

that is compatible with
\theta
, where
G_{L}
acts on
\Lambda_{L}
in the left-hand side via the inverse of the canonical character, and
the subscripts ‘
{\mathrm{rel}}
’ and ‘
\circ
’ on the left-hand side denote the submodule of classes
c\in H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})
such that
{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(c)\in H^{1}_{\circ}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})
.

We let

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\circ}(g/L)\in H^{1}_{{\mathrm{rel}},\circ}(L,T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})

be identified with
{\mathcal{BF}}^{\circ}(g/L)
under the preceding isomorphism.
This is the two-variable zeta element associated with
g
and
L
in the supersingular case.

We let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}_{v}:H^{1}_{/\circ}(L_{v},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}

be the composition

{\mathcal{C}}_{v}^{\circ}:H^{1}_{/\circ}(L_{v},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\simeq H^{1}_{/\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}{\mathscr{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta^{-1}}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

This is a
\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
-injection. Let

{\mathcal{L}}_{v}^{\circ}(g/L)={\mathcal{C}}_{v}^{\circ}({\mathrm{loc}}_{v}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\circ}(g/L)))\in\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

Note that
{\mathcal{L}}_{v}^{\circ}(g/L)=\theta^{-1}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{\circ}(g/L))
.

We similarly let
{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}:H^{1}_{\circ}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}
be the composition

{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}:H^{1}_{\circ}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\simeq H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\frac{1}{T_{v}}{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},-1}}}{{\xrightarrow{\sim}}}\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}.

Note that the inclusion
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}
induces
an isomorphism
{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}=T_{v}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-}
, and so
{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\circ}
maps

H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
into
T_{v}{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
. In particular,
the middle arrow of the composition defining
{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}
is well-defined.
We also let

{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)={\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\circ}(g/L)))\in\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}.

So
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)=\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},-1}({\mathscr{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L))
.

6.6.2.
Connections with cyclotomic
L
-functions and cyclotomic zeta elements

From Propositions

and
6.19
we immediately conclude:

Proposition 6.25
.

(i)

The reduction of
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}^{\circ}
modulo
\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1

H^{1}_{/\circ}(L_{v},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{v}-1)}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}H^{1}_{/\circ}(L_{v},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Col_{\eta_{\omega},v}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

For
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
,

{\mathcal{L}}_{v}^{\circ}(g/L)\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)=c^{\circ}(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}(g/L)\in\Lambda_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}=\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}/(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}.

.

. Let

.

.

If (irr
Q
) holds,

.

One may proceed just as in the proof of Theorem

.
∎

6.6.3.
Connections with anti-cyclotomic
L
-functions and Heegner points

From Propositions
6.20
and
6.21
and Theorem
5.16
we conclude:

Proposition 6.27
.

(i)

Suppose (
Heeg
) holds.
The image of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)
modulo
\gamma_{+}-1
equals
-{\mathscr{L}}_{v}^{BDP}(g/L)
. In particular,
the image of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)
under
\phi_{\bf 1}:\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}/(\gamma_{+}-1,\gamma_{-}-1)\Lambda_{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{ur}}
is

\phi_{\bf 1}({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L))=-(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})^{2}(\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L}))^{2}.

(ii)

Suppose
