. The image of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{Gr}(g/L)
modulo
\gamma_{+}-1

is
0
.

This proposition allows us to relate the image of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\circ}(g/L)
under Perrin-Riou’s regulator map (or ‘big logarithm’) to
Heegner points, providing a key link in our subsequent proof of the Perrin-Riou Conjecture.

Recall that Perrin-Riou’s regulator map for
H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}})
is the composition

\begin{array}[]{ccl}{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{\gamma}:H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{ac}})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Psi_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}(g)\mapsto\varepsilon(g)^{-1}\Psi_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}}(g)}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{ac}})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle res}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{ur}},{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{ac}})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&D({\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1)))\hat{\otimes}W(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{ac}},\end{array}

where
\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{ac}}
is the anticyclotomic counterpart of
\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}
and for the third map we have used that as a
G_{L_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{ur}}}
-module
\Lambda^{\mathrm{ac}}_{L}
is naturally isomorphic to the cyclotomic
algebra
\Lambda
.
Let

{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{g_{\gamma}}=([\omega_{g},-]\otimes id\otimes id)\circ{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{\gamma}:H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{ac}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{K}_{1,F_{\lambda}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}.

As in Proposition
6.7
,
there exist
{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathrm{ac}}
-module homomorphisms

{\mathscr{L}}_{\pm}^{PR}:H^{1}_{\pm}(L_{\overline{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac}})\rightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}.

such that

\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{L}}_{g_{\alpha}}^{PR}\\
{\mathcal{L}}_{g_{\beta}}^{PR}\\
\end{pmatrix}=M\cdot\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{-}\\
{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{+}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Lemma 6.28
.

There exists a unit
U_{L}\in(\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\times}
depending only on
L
such that

U_{L}\cdot{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{+}-1)={\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{\circ}.

In particular, we have

u_{L}\cdot\phi_{\bf 1}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}=\begin{cases}\frac{p(p-1)}{p+1}\cdot\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}&\text{ if $\circ=-$}\\
\frac{-2p}{p+1}\cdot\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}&\text{if $\circ=+$},\end{cases}

where
u_{L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}},\times}
is the image of
U_{L}
under the specialization map

\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}/(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)\Lambda^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}
.

Proof.

This is analogous to the proof of Lemma
5.29
.

As therein, put

{\mathcal{U}}_{L}=\frac{1}{T_{v}}{\mathcal{H}}_{v}\cdot\eta_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{+})\in(\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}
and

U_{L}={\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{-1}\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{+}-1)\in(\Lambda_{L}^{\mathrm{ac},{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\times}.

The lemma then follows directly from the identification

H^{1}_{\circ}(L_{\bar{v}},T(1)\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{L})=H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\hat{\otimes}{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda)
determined by the basis
\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}_{v}}
and comparing the definitions of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{PR}_{\circ}
.

From the specialisation properties of
{\mathcal{L}}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))}
(see
[
97
, Thm. B.5]
) it follows that

\phi_{\bf 1}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{g_{\gamma}}^{PR}=(1-\gamma/p)(1-1/\gamma)^{-1}\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}},

(6.11)

where
\log_{BK}
denotes the Bloch–Kato logarithm for
{\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1))
and
\eta_{g}=\eta_{\omega_{g}}
(so

\log_{BK}(-)=\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}(-)\cdot\eta_{g}\in D_{\mathrm{cris}}({\mathcal{F}}^{+}_{\gamma}{\bf D}(T(1)))
).
This implies the conclusion for
\phi_{\bf 1}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{v}}^{\circ}
.
∎

From Lemma
6.28
and Proposition
5.28
we conclude:

Lemma 6.29
.

(i)

Suppose (Heeg) holds. Then

-u_{L}(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})^{2}(\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L}))^{2}=\begin{cases}\frac{p(p-1)}{p+1}\cdot\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\phi_{\bf 1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{-}(g/L)))&\\
\frac{-2p}{p+1}\cdot\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\phi_{\bf 1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{+}(g/L))).&\end{cases}

(ii)

If
\varepsilon(g/L)=+1
, then
{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\phi_{\bf 1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\circ}(g/L)))=0
.

7.
The Perrin-Riou Conjecture

In this section we complete our proofs of results towards the Perrin-Riou conjecture. In particular, we prove the conjecture for elliptic curves at primes

p\geq 5
of good reduction.

The eponymous conjecture predicts a link between Beilinson–Kato element associated to a weight two newform and rational points on its
associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety. It is a
p
-adic Beilinson conjecture for the
p
-adic Beilinson–Kato elements (see
[
112
]
for more on this perspective). Its weak version (stated below) can be regarded as a
p
-adic Leopoldt-style conjecture.

7.1.
The Perrin-Riou Conjecture

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a newform and
p
a prime. We keep to the notation of the preceding sections,
especially Section
2
. In particular,
T=T_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda}}
is a lattice in the
p
-adic Galois representation

V=V_{F_{\lambda}}
associated with
g
and a prime
\lambda\mid p
of the Hecke field
F
.

Let
A_{/{\mathbb{Q}}}
be a
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety in the isogeny class of such varieties associated
with
g
(see §
2.3.4
).

7.1.1.
The conjecture

Let
\gamma\in V_{F}
with
\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0
and let
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],V(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)

be the Beilinson–Kato element as in Section
3.2.1
. Let

z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},V(1))

be the image of
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}(g)
under the map induced by the specialisation map
\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\mapsto 1
.
Kato’s explicit reciprocity law
[
79
, Thm. 12.5]

implies:

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))\in H^{1}_{f}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1))\iff L(1,g_{/{\mathbb{Q}}})=0.

The following conjecture, connecting
z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g)
to the arithmetic of
A_{g}
, especially when
0\neq{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))\in H^{1}_{f}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1))
,
is essentially due to Perrin-Riou
[
112
, §3.3-3.4]
.

Conjecture 7.1

(The Perrin-Riou Conjecture)
.

Suppose that
L(1,g)=0

(
equivalently,
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))\in H^{1}_{f}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1))

)
.
Let
\lambda\mid p
be a prime of the Hecke field
F
of
g
.
There exists
P\in A({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
such that

(1)

\log_{BK}^{\eta_{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\rm Kato}(g)))\doteq\log_{\omega_{A}}(P)^{2}
, where

–

0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
and
0\neq\omega_{A}\in\Omega^{1}(A/{\mathbb{Q}})
are
F
-bases,

–

\log_{BK}^{\eta_{\omega}}:H^{1}_{f}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V_{F_{\lambda}}(1))\rightarrow F_{\lambda}
is the Bloch–Kato logarithm
arising from
\omega
, and

–

\log_{\omega_{A}}:A({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\rightarrow F_{\lambda}
is the logarithm associated with
\omega_{A}
and
\lambda
,

–

‘
\doteq
’ denotes an equality up to an element of
F^{\times}
;

(2)

P\neq 0\iff{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,f_{/{\mathbb{Q}}})=1
.

In particular,

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{Kato}(g))\neq 0\iff{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1.

We refer to the ‘In particular’ part as the
weak version
of the Perrin-Riou Conjecture.

Remark 7.2
.

The conjecture in
[
112
]
is stated for elliptic curves over the rationals and for odd primes
p
of good reduction.
For
p\geq 5
this is proved below as a special case of the main results of this section. The generalisation of the statement of the conjecture
to newforms is straightforward. However, as stated, the point
P\in A({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
could depend on the prime
\lambda\mid p
. A slightly
stronger version of the conjecture would be that there exists
P
such that (1) holds for all
\lambda\mid p
(as well as (2)).
This is equivalent to the conjecture as stated holding for
all

\lambda\mid p
.

Remark 7.3
.

The
p
-adic logarithm of a non-torsion point in
A({\mathbb{Q}})
is expected to be transcendental
17
17

17

We are grateful to G. Wustholz for reminding us of this
.
Conjecture
7.1
thus implies a transcendence result for the Beilinson–Kato elements with non-zero localisation at
p
.

7.2.
Main result

The main result of this section is a proof of the Perrin-Riou conjecture for all newforms
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
, all primes
p\nmid 2N
,
and all
\lambda\mid p
such that
g
is ordinary with respect to
\lambda
(so
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
) and all
\lambda\mid p
if
a_{p}=0
.
Along the way we prove a more precise (integral) version of the conjecture, which has strong arithmetic consequences.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a newform. Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime and let
\lambda\mid p
be a prime of the
Hecke field of
g
. Suppose that either
a_{p}(g)=0
or
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
(that is,
g
is ordinary with respect to
\lambda
).
Then Conjecture
7.1
holds.

As every elliptic curve
E_{/{\mathbb{Q}}}
is modular, we conclude:

Corollary 7.5
.

Let
E_{/{\mathbb{Q}}}
be an elliptic curve with conductor
N
. Let
p\nmid 6N
be a prime. Then Conjecture
1.12
holds.

Since for a CM form
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
and
p\nmid 2N
, either
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
for all
\lambda\mid p
(when
p
splits in the CM field) or
a_{p}(g)=0
(when
p
is inert in the CM field), this theorem also implies:

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a CM newform. Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime.
Then Conjecture
7.1
holds.

However, a proof of a version of Conjecture
7.1
in the case that
g
is a CM form that is ordinary at
\lambda\mid p

(see Theorem
A.1
), based on results in
[
95
]
,
[
10
]
, and
[
112
]
,
plays a role in our proof of the general case of Theorem
7.4
.

Remark 7.7
.

For CM elliptic curves of analytic rank one and
p>2
an ordinary prime, a result towards Conjecture
7.1
is due to Rubin
[
122
]
.
Rubin’s result preceded Conjecture
7.1
, and the formulation of Conjecture
7.1
was in fact inspired by Rubin’s theorem.

7.2.1.
Proof of Theorem
7.4
: ordinary case

We first give the proof in the ordinary case, that is, in the case
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
.

Suppose
L(1,g)=0
.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{L}<0
such that

•

(D_{L},N)=1
,

•

the prime
p
splits in
L
:
p=v\bar{v}
,

•

\varepsilon(g^{\prime})=+1
and
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g^{\prime})=0
,
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
.

The existence of
L
follows from
[
18
]
.
Note that we do not (yet) require that
L
also satisfies (Heeg). In fact, if
\epsilon(g)=+1
then this would not be possible.
Let
(\alpha
,
\omega
,
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})
be a quadruple as in §
3.2.3
.
If (irr
Q
) holds, then we choose
\omega\in S_{g,{\mathcal{O}}}
to be good, and
\gamma\in T_{g,{\mathcal{O}}}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in T_{g^{\prime},{\mathcal{O}}}

such that
\gamma^{\pm}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}
-basis of
T_{{\mathcal{O}},g}^{\pm}
and
(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}
-basis of
T_{{\mathcal{O}},g^{\prime}}^{\pm}
.

Let
z(g/L)\in H^{1}(L,V(1))
be the image of
{\mathcal{Z}}(g/L)
under the map induced by specializing
\Lambda_{L}
at the trivial character.
Then it follows from Theorem
5.26
and the choice of
L
that we have

z(g/L)=c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})(1-1/\alpha)^{2}\frac{L(1,g^{\prime})}{\Omega^{-}}z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g)\in H^{1}(L,V(1)),

(7.1)

where
c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in F^{\times}
is as in Proposition
5.13
.
Since
z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},V(1))=H^{1}(L,V(1))^{+}
, both

H^{1}(L_{v},V(1))
and
H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},V(1))
are canonically identified with
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1))
.
In particular, we see that

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))\neq 0\iff{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(z(g/L))\neq 0.

(7.2)

Proof of the weak version
:

Suppose first that
\varepsilon(g)=+1
, so
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)
is even (and
\neq 1
, in particular).
We then need to show that
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=0
.
By the choice of
L
,
\varepsilon(g/L)=\varepsilon(g)\varepsilon(g^{\prime})=+1
. But then

{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(z(g/L))=0
by Lemma
5.30
(ii), which implies
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=0
by (
7.2
).

Suppose next that
\varepsilon(g)=-1
. In this case, we can - and do - require that
L
also satisfies the Heegner hypothesis (
Heeg
).
It then follows from Lemma
5.30
(i) that

(1-\alpha/p)(1-1/\alpha)^{-1}\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(z(g/L)))=-u_{L}(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})^{2}(\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L}))^{2}.

Here
y_{L}\in J_{0}(N)(L)
is the Heegner point associated with
L
and
\log_{\omega_{g}}
is the
p
-adic logarithm
on
J_{0}(N)(L_{\bar{v}})
associated with the differential
\omega_{g}
(which corresponds to the differential
on
X_{0}(N)
that pulls back to
2\pi ig(z)dz
under the usual complex uniformization).
Combining this with (
7.1
) yields

\log^{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}_{BK}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=-u_{L}\frac{(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})}{c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})L(1,g^{\prime})/\Omega^{-}}(\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L}))^{2}.

(7.3)

It follows immediately that
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))\neq 0
if and only if
y_{L}
is non-torsion, and by the Gross–Zagier formula this happens if and only if

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1
, which completes the proof of the weak form of the Perrin-Riou conjecture.

Proof of the stronger form
:

To prove the stronger form of the conjecture, we can and do assume that
L\neq{\mathbb{Z}}(\sqrt{-1}),{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-2})
.
We fix a quotient map

\phi_{A}:J_{0}(N)\rightarrow A,

which also determines a quotient map
J_{1}(N)\rightarrow A

by composition with the natural map
J_{1}(N)\rightarrow J_{0}(N)
. We then let
P_{L}=\phi_{A}(y_{L})\in A(L)
.
Then
\phi_{A}^{*}(\omega_{A})=c_{g}\cdot\omega_{g}
for some
0\neq c_{g}\in F^{\times}
, and so

\log_{\omega_{A}}(P_{L})=c_{g}\log_{\omega_{A}}(y_{L})
.
Without loss of generality we may assume that
\omega=\omega_{g}
and
\omega
and
\omega_{A}
are identified by
the quotient map
J_{1}(N)\rightarrow A
.

We can then rewrite (
7.7
) as

\log^{\eta_{\omega}}_{BK}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=-u_{L}c_{g}^{-2}\frac{(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})}{c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})L(1,g^{\prime})/\Omega^{-}}(\log_{\omega_{A}}(P_{L}))^{2}.

Let
P_{L}^{\pm}=\frac{1\pm c}{2}P_{L}
.
Then
P_{L}^{-}\in(A(L)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}F)^{-}=A^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}F
, where
A^{\prime}
is the
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety associated with
g^{\prime}
that is just the
L
-twist of
A
.
Since
L(1,g^{\prime})\neq 0
by the choice of
L
, it follows (say from Kato
[
79
, Thm. 14.2]
) that
A^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}F=0
, hence
P_{L}^{-}=0

and so
\log_{\omega_{A}}P_{L}=\log_{\omega}P_{L}^{+}
. Let
P=P_{L}^{+}\in(A(L)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}F)^{+}=A({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}}F
. We then have

\log_{BK}^{\eta_{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=c\cdot(\log_{\omega_{A}}(P))^{2},

(7.4)

with

c=-u_{L}\frac{(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})}{c_{g}^{2}c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})L(1,g^{\prime})/\Omega^{-}}.

Since
\frac{(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})}{c_{g}^{2}c(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})L(1,g^{\prime})/\Omega^{-}}\in F^{\times}
,
to prove part (1) of the conjecture, it remains to note that
u_{L}\in F^{\times}
. We in fact show that
u_{L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}^{\times}
; this is explained below.
Part (2) of the conjecture is an immediate consequence of
P\neq 0\iff y_{L}\neq 0
and the Gross–Zagier formula for
y_{L}
.

Proof that
u_{L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}^{\times}
:

To determine
u_{L}
and complete the proof of the stronger form of the Perrin-Riou Conjecture, we exploit
a version of the conjecture proved for CM forms in Theorem
A.1
. Theorem
A.1

applies to the CM forms associated with the characters
\psi\in\mathfrak{X}
, for
\mathfrak{X}
as in §
B.2
for
the chosen field
L
. It then follows from comparing Theorem
A.1
with (
7.4
) for
g=g_{\psi}
that

u_{L}\in F_{\psi}^{\times}
for all
\psi\in\mathfrak{X}
. In particular,

u_{L}\in\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}}F_{\psi}.

The latter equals

{\mathbb{Q}}
by Lemma
B.2
. This shows that
u_{L}\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}},\times}={\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}^{\times}
.

This completes the proof of Theorem
7.4
in the ordinary case.

Remark 7.8
.

The two-variable zeta element underlies the above proof.

7.2.2.
Proof of Theorem
7.4
: supersingular case

We now consider the supersingular case, that is, assume
a_{p}(g)=0
. The proof is analogous to the ordinary case.

Suppose
L(1,g)=0
. Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field as in the second paragraph of subsection
7.2.1
.
Let
z^{+}(g/L)\in H^{1}(L,V(1))
be the image of the zeta element
{\mathcal{Z}}^{+}(g/L)
under the map induced by specializing
\Lambda_{L}
at the trivial character. (Alternatively, one may consider
z^{-}(g/L)
.)

In view of Theorem
6.26
and the choice of
L
we have

z^{+}(g/L)=c^{+}(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})\frac{L(1,g^{\prime})}{\Omega^{-}}z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g)\in H^{1}(L,V(1)),

(7.5)

where
c^{+}(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in F^{\times}
is as in Proposition
6.19
.
Since
z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}},V(1))=H^{1}(L,V(1))^{+}
, both

H^{1}(L_{v},V(1))
and
H^{1}(L_{\bar{v}},V(1))
are canonically identified with
H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(1))
.
In particular, we see that

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))\neq 0\iff{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(z^{+}(g/L))\neq 0.

(7.6)

Proof of the weak version
:

Suppose first that
\varepsilon(g)=+1
, so
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)
is even (and
\neq 1
, in particular).
We then need to show that
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=0
.
By the choice of
L
,
\varepsilon(g/L)=\varepsilon(g)\varepsilon(g^{\prime})=+1
. But then

{\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(z^{+}(g/L))=0
by Lemma
6.29
(ii), which implies
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=0
by (
7.2
).

Suppose next that
\varepsilon(g)=-1
. In this case, we further suppose that
L
also satisfies the Heegner hypothesis (
Heeg
).
It then follows from Lemma
6.29
(i) that

\frac{-2p}{p+1}\cdot\log_{BK}^{\eta_{g}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\bar{v}}(\phi_{\bf 1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{+}(g/L)))=-u_{L}(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})^{2}(\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L}))^{2}.

Here
y_{L}\in J_{0}(N)(L)
is the Heegner point associated with
L
and
\log_{\omega_{g}}
is the
p
-adic logarithm
on
J_{0}(N)(L_{\bar{v}})
associated with the differential
\omega_{g}
.
Combining this with (
7.5
) yields

\log^{\eta_{\omega_{g}}}_{BK}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))=\frac{u_{L}}{2p/p+1}\cdot\frac{(1-a(p)p^{-1}+p^{-1})^{2}}{c^{+}(\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}(\chi_{L})L(1,g^{\prime})/\Omega^{-}}(\log_{\omega_{g}}(y_{L}))^{2}.

(7.7)

It follows immediately that
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\mathrm{Kato}}(g))\neq 0
if and only if
y_{L}
is non-torsion, and by the Gross–Zagier formula this happens if and only if

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1
, which completes the proof of the weak form of the Perrin-Riou conjecture.

Proof of the stronger form
:
Given the preceding analysis, one may proceed exactly as in the ordinary case (cf. subsection
7.2.1
).

Remark 7.9
.

The supersingular case can also be approached via Kobayashi’s
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formula
[
88
]
(cf.
[
23
, App. B]
). This approach does not rely on the two-variable zeta element.

Appendix A
The ordinary CM case

In this appendix we present a proof of a version of the Perrin-Riou Conjecture for certain CM modular forms at good ordinary primes
p>2
. Our proof essentially pieces together results from
[
95
]
,
[
10
]
, and
[
112
]
. In particular, it does not rely on the two-variable zeta elements for auxiliary imaginary quadratic fields that are the focus of Part I of this paper or on the
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formula, which undergirds Rubin’s earlier work
[
122
]
. The truth of the Perrin-Riou Conjecture for the cases considered in this appendix is used in the proof of the general case given in Part I of this paper (it is used to show that the constants
u_{L}
associated with the auxiliary imaginary quadratic fields
L
belong to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}^{\times}
).

A.1.
The set-up

Let
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
be a separable algebraic closure of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and let
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}

be an embedding. Let
K\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{K}<0

and
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
its ring of integers. The embedding
\iota_{\infty}
identifies
K\otimes{\mathbb{R}}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
\tau\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the involution induced via
\iota_{\infty}
by complex conjugation on
{\mathbb{C}}
(so
\tau
restricts to the non-trivial automorphism of
K
).

Let
\psi:K^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a Hecke character with infinity type
(-1,0)
(meaning that
\psi(z)=z^{-1}
for
z\in K\otimes{\mathbb{R}}={\mathbb{C}}
). The values
\psi({\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times})
of
\psi
on the finite ideles generate a finite extension
F_{\psi}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
of
K
, which we view as a subfield
of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
via
\iota_{\infty}
. Let
\mathfrak{f}_{\psi}
be the conductor of
\psi
, and let
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathfrak{f}_{\psi}
be an ideal as in
[
95
, Thm. 2.2]
. Then there is a unique CM pair
(E,\alpha)
over the ray class field
K(\mathfrak{f})
:
{\mathrm{End}}_{K(\mathfrak{f})}(E)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
such that the induced action on
\mathrm{coLie}(E/K(\mathfrak{f}))
is the natural action of
K
, the annihilator of
\alpha\in E(K(\mathfrak{f}))_{{\mathrm{tor}}}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
is

\mathfrak{f}
, and there is an isomorphism
E({\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{C}}/\mathfrak{f}
that maps
\alpha
to
1
. Following

[
79
, §15.8]
we let
V(\psi)=H^{1}(E({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{K}F_{\psi}
and
S(\psi)=H^{0}({\mathrm{Gal}}(K(\mathfrak{f})/K),\mathrm{coLie}(E/K(\mathfrak{f}))\otimes_{K}F_{\psi})
, where the action of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K(\mathfrak{f})/K)
is defined in
op. cit.
These are one-dimensional
F_{\psi}
-spaces.
For any
F_{\psi}
-algebra
A
, let
V(\psi)_{A}=V(\psi)\otimes_{F_{\psi}}A
and
S(\psi)_{A}=S(\psi)\otimes_{F_{\psi}}A
. We let

\mathrm{per}_{\psi}:S(\psi)\hookrightarrow V(\psi)_{\mathbb{C}}
be the period map induced from the usual
period map
\mathrm{coLie}(E/K(\mathfrak{f}))\rightarrow H^{1}(E({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{C}})
.

Let
p
be a prime and
\lambda\mid p
a prime of
F_{\psi}
. We fix an embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
that induces

\lambda
. We let
G_{K}
act on
V(\psi)_{\lambda}=V(\psi)_{F_{\psi,\lambda}}
as described in
[
79
, §15.8]
and denote the corresponding
character
G_{K}\rightarrow F_{\psi,\lambda}^{\times}
by
\psi_{\lambda}
(this action is the inverse of that defined in §
4
). Let

\widetilde{V(\psi)}_{\lambda}={\mathrm{Ind}}_{K}^{{\mathbb{Q}}}V(\psi)_{\lambda}.

This is an irreducible two-dimensional
F_{\psi,\lambda}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Concretely,

\widetilde{V(\psi)}_{\lambda}=V(\psi)_{\lambda}\oplus V(\psi)_{\lambda}^{\tau}
, where
V(\psi)_{\lambda}^{\tau}
has the same underlying
space as
V(\psi)_{\lambda}
but the action of
\sigma\in G_{K}
is multiplication by
\psi_{\lambda}(\tau^{-1}\psi\tau)
; the action
of
\tau
just swaps the two summands. We similarly let

\widetilde{V(\psi)}={\mathrm{Ind}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{{\mathbb{R}}}V(\psi)
, which is a two-dimensional
F_{\psi}
-space with an action of
G_{\mathbb{R}}={\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathbb{C}}/{\mathbb{R}})
.

Let
g=g_{\psi}
be the weight
2
newform associated with
\psi
. For our purposes we will assume that

\psi
is conjugate self-dual, that is,

\psi|_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}=\chi_{K}|\cdot|^{-1},

(A.1)

where
\chi_{K}
is the quadratic character corresponding to the quadratic extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}}
. This implies that

g
has trivial Nebentypus. In particular,
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
is a newform of level
N=D_{K}N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{f}_{\psi})
.
We therefore freely use the notations of §
2
.

Note that the Hecke field
F
of
g
is contained in
F_{\psi}
, and by a mild abuse of notation we also denote by
\lambda
the prime
of
F
under the chosen prime
\lambda\mid p
of
F_{\psi}
. We let
V(g)_{\lambda}=V_{F_{\psi},\lambda}=V_{\lambda}\otimes_{F_{\lambda}}F_{\psi,\lambda}
.
Then

V(g)_{\lambda}\simeq\widetilde{V(\psi)}_{\lambda}={\mathrm{Ind}}_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}V(\psi)_{\lambda}.

Such an isomorphism can be normalized as in
[
79
, Lem. 15.11]
: Fixing an isomorphism

s:S(\psi)\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{F_{\psi}}
of one-dimensional
F_{\psi}
-spaces, there is a unique
F_{\psi,\lambda}
-isomorphism
\widetilde{V(\psi)}_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}V(g)_{\lambda}
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-representations such that the isomorphism
S(\psi)_{F_{\psi,\lambda}}\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{F_{\psi,\lambda}}
induced by the functoriality of
D_{dR}
is just that induced from
s
. There also exists an unique
F_{\psi}
-isomorphism
\widetilde{V(\psi)}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{F_{\psi}}
of

G_{\mathbb{R}}
-representations inducing via
s
an identification of the period maps
per_{\psi}:S(\psi)\rightarrow V(\psi)_{\mathbb{C}}\subset\widetilde{V(\psi)}\otimes_{F_{\psi}}{\mathbb{C}}
and
per:S_{F_{\psi}}\rightarrow V_{\mathbb{C}}
. From now on we assume that we have fixed an isomorphism
s
as above and freely appeal to these resulting isomorphisms and identifications.

A.2.
The main result

Let
0\neq\gamma\in V(\psi)
and let
0\neq\gamma^{\prime}\in V_{F_{\psi}}
be the corresponding element. Note that
(\gamma^{\prime})^{\pm}\neq 0
.
Let
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],V(g)_{\lambda}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
be the Beilinson–Kato element as in
§
3.2
(with the field of coefficients extended from
F_{\lambda}
to
F_{\psi,\lambda}
).
Let
z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)\in H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],V(g)_{\lambda}(1))
be the image of this element via the specialisation
map
\Lambda\mapsto{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}\mapsto 1
.

Suppose now that

p
splits in
K

(A.2)

and

(p,\mathfrak{f}_{\psi})=1.

(A.3)

This means in particular that
p\nmid N
and
g
is ordinary with respect to
\lambda
.
One of the key results of
[
95
]
identifies the image of
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)
under
Perrin-Riou’s
p
-adic regulator in terms of cyclotomic specialisations of Katz’s two-variable
p
-adic
L
-functions for
K
,
as we now recall.

Let
p=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
be the factorisation of
p
in
K
with
\mathfrak{p}
the prime determined by the fixed
embedding
\iota_{p}
.
Let
K_{\infty}=K(\mathfrak{f}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})
be the union of the ray class extensions of
K

of conductor
\mathfrak{f}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}
,
n>0
. Let
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\infty}}
be the completion of the ring of integers of
K_{\infty}

at the prime above
\mathfrak{p}
determined by
\iota_{p}
. From the measure
\mu(\mathfrak{f}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})

of
[
46
, Thm. II.4.14]
(equivalently, the element
{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}
of
[
95
, Def. 2.2.1]
) we obtain two specialisations

{\mathbb{L}}_{1},{\mathbb{L}}_{2}\in\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\infty}}}
that are characterised by

{\mathbb{L}}_{1}(\chi)={\mathbb{L}}_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}(\chi\psi_{\lambda}^{-1})=\phi_{\chi\psi_{\lambda}^{-1}}(\mu_{\mathfrak{f}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}})\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathbb{L}}_{2}(\chi)={\mathbb{L}}_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}(\chi(\psi_{\lambda}^{\tau})^{-1})=\phi_{\chi(\psi_{\lambda}^{\tau})^{-1}}(\mu_{\mathfrak{f}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}})

for all finite order characters
\chi:\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
. Recall that
\phi_{?}
is the continuous homomorphism of the Iwasawa algebra
defined to the linear extension of the character
\chi
. The values
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}(\chi)
are multiples of
L(1,g\otimes\chi^{-1})
.
More precisely,
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}
is the specialisation of the two-variable Katz
p
-adic
L
-function of
K
(and the prime
\mathfrak{p}
) along the cyclotomic line passing through
\psi_{\lambda}
, and so is a multiple - by a
p
-adic period - of the
p
-adic
L
-function of
g
.
However, the values
{\mathbb{L}}_{2}(\chi)
are not interpolating critical values:
{\mathbb{L}}_{2}
is the specialisation of
the two-variable Katz
p
-adic
L
-function at the cyclotomic line passing through
\psi_{\lambda}^{\tau}
, which is outside the region of interpolation.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}:H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(g)_{\lambda}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\rightarrow{\mathcal{H}}(\Gamma)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}D_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(g)_{\lambda}(1))

be Perrin-Riou’s regulator (cf.
[
97
, App. B]
). By
[
95
, Thm. 3.2]
,
the image of
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))
under this regulator is

{\mathcal{L}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)))=L_{p,1}+L_{p,2},

where

\bullet

L_{p,1}={\mathbb{L}}_{1}\cdot t\otimes\gamma^{\prime}

and
L_{p,2}=\ell_{0}\cdot{\mathbb{L}}_{2}\otimes\tau\gamma^{\prime}
in
\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}B_{{\mathrm{cris}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}V(1)

with
\ell_{0}=\log(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})/\log\varepsilon(\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}})\in\Lambda
and
t\in B_{{\mathrm{cris}}}
as usual;

•

under the identification
D_{{\mathrm{cris}}}(V(g)_{\lambda}(1))=D_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(\psi)_{\lambda}(1))\oplus D_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(\psi)_{\lambda}^{\tau}(1))

induced from the fixed isomorphism
\widetilde{V(\psi)}_{\lambda}\simeq V(g)_{\lambda}
,

L_{p,1}\in\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}D_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(\psi)_{\lambda}(1))
and
L_{p,2}\in\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}D_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(\psi)_{\lambda}^{\tau}(1))
;

•

via the de Rham-crystalline comparison isomorphism,
L_{p,1}\in\Lambda\otimes\mathrm{Fil}^{0}D_{dR}(V(g)_{\lambda}(1))
.

Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
and let
\omega_{\psi}=s^{-1}(\omega)\in S(\psi)
.
Let
(\Omega_{\infty},\Omega_{p})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\times\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\infty}}^{\times}
be the pair of CM periods such that

per_{\psi}(\omega_{\psi})=\Omega_{\infty}\gamma
in
V(\psi)_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\omega_{\psi}=\Omega_{p}\gamma
in
D_{dR}(V(\psi)_{\lambda})
.
Then
per(\omega)=\Omega_{\infty}\gamma
in
V_{\mathbb{C}}
,
\omega=\Omega_{p}\gamma^{\prime}
in
D_{dR}(V(g)_{\lambda}(1))
, and
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}=\Omega_{p}Col_{\eta_{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)))\in\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{K}_{\infty}
. Note that
Col_{\eta_{\omega}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)))\in\Lambda\otimes F_{\psi}
is just the usual
p
-adic
L
-function of
g
for the period
\Omega_{\infty}
.

Let
\eta\in D_{dR}(V(g)_{\lambda}(1))
be such that
[\eta,\omega]=1
, where
[-,-]
is the pairing induced via the de Rham-crystalline isomorphism
from the Weil-pairing on
V(g)_{\lambda}(1)
(which is just the pairing induced by the Poincare pairing
(,)
on
V_{F}
).
Let
c_{\gamma}=(\tau\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})^{-1}\in F_{\psi}^{\times}
. Then it follows that
\eta=c_{\gamma}\Omega_{p}^{-1}\tau\gamma^{\prime}
.

Suppose now that
L(1,g)=L(1,\psi)=0
. This means that
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}
vanishes on the identity character and that

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))\in H^{1}_{f}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},V(g)_{\lambda}(1))
. It then follows from

[
112
, Prop. 2.2.2]
and the listed properties of
{\mathcal{L}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(\mathbf{z}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)))
that

(1-1/\alpha)(1-1/\beta)^{-1}{\mathbb{L}}_{2}(1)c_{\gamma}\Omega_{p}^{-1}=\log_{BK}^{\eta}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))),

(A.4)

where
\alpha=\psi(\varpi_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}})
, with
\varpi_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}
a uniformiser,
is the unit root of
x^{2}-a_{p}(g)x+p
with respect to
\iota_{p}
and
\beta
is the other root, and
\log_{BK}^{\eta}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)))\cdot\eta=\log_{BK}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))\in D_{dR}(V(g)_{\lambda}(1))
is the Bloch–Kato logarithm.

Suppose the root number
\epsilon(\psi)
, which is just
\epsilon(g)
, is
+1
.
Then

{\mathbb{L}}_{2}(1)=0.

This is because
{\mathbb{L}}_{2}(1)
is the
p
-adic limit of the values

{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathfrak{f}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}}((\psi_{\lambda}/\psi_{\lambda}^{\tau})^{-(p-1)j}(\psi_{\lambda}^{\tau})^{-1})
for integers
j>0

tending to
0

p
-adically, and because
{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathfrak{f}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}}((\psi_{\lambda}/\psi_{\lambda}^{\tau})^{-(p-1)j}(\psi_{\lambda}^{\tau})^{-1})

is a multiple of
L(1,\psi^{\tau}(\psi/\psi^{\tau})^{(p-1)j})
, which is readily seen to vanish as the corresponding root number is then
-1
.
Combined with (
A.4
), this shows that if
L(1,g)=0
and
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)
is even, then
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))=0
.

Suppose that
\epsilon(\psi)=\epsilon(g)=-1
. Suppose additionally that

D_{K}
is odd and
\mathfrak{d}_{K}
exactly divides
\mathfrak{f}_{\psi}
.

(A.5)

That is, we assume hypotheses (1-7), (1-8), and (1-9) of
[
10
]
hold for the character
\psi
.
That (1-6) also holds is a consequence of
g
having trivial Nebentypus (see (
A.1
)).
Then we have the important formula
[
10
, Thm. 2]

{\mathbb{L}}_{2}(1)=_{F_{\psi}^{\times}}\Omega_{p}^{-1}\log_{\omega}(P_{\psi})^{2},

(A.6)

where the subscript ‘
F_{\psi}^{\times}
’ denotes equality up to an
F_{\psi}^{\times}
-multiple,

A_{g}
is an abelian variety in the isogeny class of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian varieties associated with
g
such that
{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\psi}}\subset{\mathrm{End}}_{K}(A_{g})
,
and
P_{\psi}\in A_{g}(K)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\psi}}}F_{\psi}
is a rational point. Here we are using that there is a natural
F_{\psi}
-linear
isomorphism
\Omega^{1}(A_{g}/K)\simeq S_{F_{\psi}}
, so
\omega
is identified with a differential on
A_{g}
.
We also have

\text{$P_{\psi}$ has infinite order if and only if ${\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1$}.

(A.7)

To deduce (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) from
op. cit.
we have used:

•

{\mathbb{L}}_{2}(1)={\mathcal{L}}_{p}(\psi^{*})
, where the right-hand side is as in
op. cit.
(this follows immediately from
{\mathbb{L}}_{2}(1)
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}(\psi^{*})
being the
p
-adic limit of
L
-values as described above in the case
\epsilon(\psi)=+1
);

•

A_{g}
can be taken to be the abelian variety
B_{\psi}
associated with
\psi
as in
[
10
, Thm. 2.5]
;

•

by their definitions, both
\Omega_{p}
and the
p
-adic period
\Omega_{p}(\psi^{*})
of
[
10
, Def. 2.13]
are
F_{\psi}^{\times}
-multiples of a
p
-adic period

\Omega_{p}(E)
associated with the CM pair
(E,\alpha)
.

Combined with (
A.4
) this shows, at least under the hypotheses (
A.5
) that if
\epsilon(g)=-1
, then

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))\neq 0
if and only if
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1
.

In fact, we have proved

Theorem A.1
.

Suppose that (
A.1
), (
A.2
), (
A.3
) and (
A.5
) hold.
Let
g=g_{\psi}
and suppose
L(1,g)=0
.
Let
0\neq\omega\in\Omega^{1}(A_{g}/K)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\psi}}}F_{\psi}
.
Then
there exists a rational point
P_{\psi}\in A_{g}(K)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\psi}}}F_{\psi}
such that

\log_{BK}^{\eta}({\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g)))=_{F_{\psi}^{\times}}\Omega_{p}^{-1}\log_{\omega}(P_{\psi})^{2},

and
P_{\psi}\neq 0
if and only if
{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))\neq 0
. In particular,

{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g))\neq 0\ \iff\ {\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1.

Remark A.2
.

It should be possible to dispense with the hypotheses (
A.5
), as noted in the introduction to
[
10
]
. Much of the work needed to do this has been carried out in
[
20
]
and
[
101
]
. However, we have not pursued this here. In part because, Theorem
A.1
suffices for our purposes: the proof of Theorem
7.4
, which also covers the more general CM case.

Appendix B
Values of Hecke characters

In the proof of the Perrin-Riou Conjecture given in §
7
it is necessary to show that a certain constant –
u_{L}
in the notation of the proof – belongs to
{\mathbb{Q}}
. This is achieved by appealing to Theorem
A.1
and by knowing that is possible to choose suitable Hecke characters
\psi
whose values generate disjoint fields. The result on disjoint fields is provided by this appendix.

Let
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
be a separable algebraic closure of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and let
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}

be an embedding. Let
K\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{K}

and
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
its ring of integers. The embedding
\iota_{\infty}
identifies
K\otimes{\mathbb{R}}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
\tau\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the involution induced via
\iota_{\infty}
by complex conjugation on
{\mathbb{C}}
(so
\tau
restricts to the non-trivial automorphism of
K
).

Recall that a Hecke chararacter
\psi:K^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
has infinity type
(-1,0)
if
\psi(z)=z^{-1}
for all
z\in(K\otimes{\mathbb{R}})^{\times}={\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. For such a character, the values
\psi({\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times})
generate a finite extension
F_{\psi}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We consider
F_{\psi}
as a subfield of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
via
\iota_{\infty}
.

B.1.
Values of canonical characters

Suppose
-D_{K}<-4
.
Let
\mathfrak{d}_{K}
be the different of
K
. The canonical characters of
K
are the Hecke characters
\psi
of infinity type
(-1,0)
and
conductor
\mathfrak{d}_{K}
such that
\psi(\tau(x))=\tau(\psi(x))
for all
x\in{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}.
Such characters were first considered by Rohrlich in
[
118
]
.
They exist precisely when

D_{K}\equiv 3\ \mathrm{mod}\ 4\ \ \text{or}\ \ 8\mid D_{K}.

(B.1)

Moreover, it is clear that a canonical Hecke character
\psi
must satisfy

\psi|_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}=\chi_{K}|\cdot|^{-1},

(B.2)

where
\chi_{K}
is the quadratic character associated with the extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Using this, it is easy to describe all the canonical characters as follows.

Let
U=K^{\times}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}
. The quotient
{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}/U
is isomorphic to the class group of
K
and, in particular, is finite. Via the isomorphisms

({\mathbb{Z}}/D_{K}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\xrightarrow{\sim}({\mathcal{O}}_{K}/\mathfrak{d}_{K})^{\times}\xrightarrow{\sim}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}/(1+\hat{\mathfrak{d}}_{K})^{\times},

we can view
\chi_{K}
as a character of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
of conductor
\mathfrak{d}_{K}
.
Let
\psi_{U}:U\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be the character given by
\psi_{U}(\alpha x)=\alpha\chi_{K}(x)
for
\alpha\in K^{\times}
and
x\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
.
This is well-defined as
K^{\times}\cap\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}=\{\pm 1\}
since
-D_{K}<-4
by hypothesis and since
\chi_{K}(-1)=-1
.
Let
\psi_{f}:{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be any character extending
\psi_{U}
. Then
\psi(x)=x_{\infty}^{-1}\psi_{f}(x_{f})
is a
canonical Hecke character, and clearly all canonical characters are obtained in this way. In particular, there are
h_{K}
(the class number of
K
) canonical characters and the ratio of any two is a character of the class group.

Let
\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}
be the set of canonical Hecke characters of
K
. The fields
F_{\psi}
,
\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}
, admit a simple description.
Since
\psi_{U}
is the identity on
K^{\times}
, we certainly have
K\subset F_{\psi}
.
Let
x_{1},...,x_{r}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}
be such that their images
\bar{x}_{1},...,\bar{x}_{r}
in the quotient
{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}/U

satisfy

{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}/U=\langle\bar{x}_{1}\rangle\oplus\cdots\oplus\langle\bar{x}_{r}\rangle

and (for convenience later)
the order
h_{i}
of each
\bar{x}_{i}
is a power of a prime
\ell_{i}
.
Since
{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\infty,\times}/U
is isomorphic to the class group,
h_{i}\mid h_{K}
.
Suppose
x_{i}^{h_{i}}=\alpha_{i}u_{i}
with
\alpha_{i}\in K^{\times}
and
u_{i}\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
. Then
\psi(x_{i})^{h_{i}}=\psi(\alpha_{i}u_{i})=\psi_{U}(\alpha_{i}u_{i})=\alpha_{i}\chi_{K}(u_{i})=\pm\alpha_{i}
. Replacing
\alpha_{i}
with
-\alpha_{i}
and
u_{i}
with
-u_{i}
if necessary, we may then assume that
\psi(x_{i})^{h_{i}}=\alpha_{i}
.
That is,
\beta_{i}=\psi(x_{i})
is an
h_{i}
th root of
\alpha_{i}
. Let
\beta_{\psi}=(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{r})
. Then
F_{\psi}
is just the radical extension

F_{\psi}=K(\beta_{\psi}):=K(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{r}).

Furthermore, the fields
F_{\psi}
,
\psi
running over all characters in
\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}
,
are exactly those fields
K(\beta)
,
\beta=(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{r})
with the
\beta_{i}
running over all possible
h_{i}
th roots of the
\alpha_{i}
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
be the ideal corresponding to
x_{i}
. Then by the choice of the
x_{i}
:

\mathfrak{a_{i}}^{h_{i}}=(\alpha_{i})
,
h_{i}
is the order of the image
\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}
of
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
in the
class group
Cl(K)
of
K
, and
Cl(K)=\langle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\rangle\oplus\cdots\oplus\langle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{r}\rangle
.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field with discriminant
-D_{K}<-4
satisfying (
B.1
) and
suppose the class number
h_{K}
of
K
is odd (so
D_{K}
is a prime).
Then
\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}}F_{\psi}=K
.

Proof.

Suppose
h_{K}\neq 1
(else there is nothing to prove).
Let
\mathfrak{B}=\{\beta=(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{r}):\beta_{i}^{h_{i}}=\alpha_{i}\}
. Then
\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}}F_{\psi}=\cap_{\beta\in\mathfrak{B}}K(\beta)
, so we want to show that
F=\cap_{\beta\in\mathfrak{B}}K(\beta)
equals
K
.

The collection of fields
K(\beta)
,
\beta\in\mathfrak{B}
, is stable under
G_{K}
: for
\sigma\in G_{K}
,

\sigma(K(\beta)))=K(\sigma(\beta))
, with
\sigma(\beta)=(\sigma(\beta_{1}),...,\sigma(\beta_{r}))
.
To prove that
F=K
it then suffices to show that
K(\beta)\neq K(\beta^{\prime})
for
\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{B}
:
For then it will follow that the conjugates
\sigma(K(\beta))
,
\sigma\in G_{K}
, over
K
of each
K(\beta)
are distinct. This can only happen if the intersection
of these conjugates is
K
. So
F
, which is contained in this intersection, must also equal
K
.

Suppose
\beta=(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{r}),\beta^{\prime}=(\beta_{1}^{\prime},....,\beta_{r}^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{B}
are such that
\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}
but
K(\beta)=K(\beta^{\prime})
.
Since
\beta_{i}/\beta_{i}^{\prime}
is an
h_{i}
th root of unity, it follows that
K(\beta)
must contain some nontrivial
h_{j}
th root of unity for some
j
.
Since each
h_{j}
is odd and
K\neq{\mathbb{Q}}(\mu_{3})
, this non-trivial root of unity is not contained in
K
.
In particular,
K(\beta)\cap K(\mu_{h})\neq K
for
h=[h_{1},...,h_{r}]
the least common multiple
of the
h_{i}
.

Let
W=\langle\alpha_{1}^{h/h_{1}},...,\alpha_{r}^{h/h_{r}}\rangle\subset K^{\times}
and let
\overline{W}
be the image of
W
in
K^{\times}/(K^{\times})^{h}
.
We claim that if
\prod_{i}\alpha_{i}^{m_{i}h/h_{i}}=\alpha^{h}
then
h_{i}\mid m_{i}
.
For then
\prod_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{m_{i}h}=\prod_{i}(\alpha_{i}^{m_{i}h/h_{i}})=(\alpha)^{h}
, and so
\prod_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{m_{i}}=(\alpha)
.
From the description of the class group
Cl(K)
preceding the statement of the proposition, it then follows that
we must have
h_{i}\mid m_{i}
. In particular,
\overline{W}
is isomorphic to the class group of
K
.

Suppose
K(\beta)\cap K(\mu_{h})\neq K
. Then
[K(\beta,\mu_{h}):K(\mu_{h})]<[K(\beta):K]\leq h_{K}=\#\overline{W}
.
However,
K(\beta,\mu_{h})
corresponds via Kummer theory with the image of
\overline{W}
in
K(\mu_{h})^{\times}/(K(\mu_{h})^{\times})^{h}

and so this image, whose order equals
[K(\beta,\mu_{h}):K(\mu_{h})]
, must be smaller than
\overline{W}
. Hence

\overline{W}
must have non-trivial intersection with the kernel of the natural map
K^{\times}/(K^{\times})^{h}\rightarrow K(\mu_{h})^{\times}/(K(\mu_{h})^{\times})^{h}
.
Let
w\in W
be an element with image
\bar{w}
in
\overline{W}
in this kernel and having order a prime, say
\ell
(necessarily odd).
Let
I_{\ell}=\{1\leq i\leq r\ ;\ \ell_{i}=\ell\}
. Then

w=\prod_{i\in I_{\ell}}\alpha_{i}^{m_{i}h/h_{i}}
with each
h_{i}/m_{i}=\ell
or
1
. Let
I_{w}\subset I_{\ell}
be the subset of those

i
such that
h_{i}/m_{i}=\ell
. Then
w=\prod_{i\in I_{w}}\alpha_{i}^{h/\ell}
. Let
u=\prod_{i\in I_{w}}\alpha_{i}
.
Then
\omega=\prod_{i\in I_{w}}\beta_{i}^{h_{i}/\ell}
is an
h
th-root of
w
and an
\ell
th-root of
u
.

Since
\overline{w}
is in the kernel of
K^{\times}/(K^{\times})^{h}\rightarrow K(\mu_{h})^{\times}/(K(\mu_{h})^{\times})^{h}
,

K(\omega)\subset K(\mu_{h})
. In particular,
K(\omega)
equals each of its
G_{K}
-conjugates.
As
u\not\in(K^{\times})^{\ell}
(else
w=u^{h/\ell}\in(K^{\times})^{h})
it follows that
K(\omega)
contains
a primitive
\ell
th root of unity. As
\ell
is odd and
K\neq{\mathbb{Q}}(\mu_{3})
, this means
K(\omega)\cap K(\mu_{\ell})\neq K
and so, by Kummer theory again,
u\in(K(\mu_{\ell})^{\times})^{\ell}
. It follows that

w=u^{h/\ell}\in(K(\mu_{\ell})^{\times})^{h}
. That is,
\prod_{i\in I_{w}}\alpha_{i}^{h/\ell}=\gamma^{h}
for
some
\gamma\in K(\mu_{\ell})
. But in term of ideals this becomes

\prod_{i\in I_{w}}\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{hh_{i}/\ell}{\mathcal{O}}_{K(\mu_{\ell})}=(\gamma)^{h}
, so

\prod_{i\in I_{w}}\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{h_{i}/\ell}{\mathcal{O}}_{K(\mu_{\ell})}=(\gamma)
. This last equality means that the ideal

\mathfrak{a}=\prod_{i=I_{w}}\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{h_{i}/\ell}
capitulates in the extension
K(\mu_{\ell})
,
which in turn implies
\mathfrak{a}^{[K(\mu_{\ell}):K]}
is principal.
As
[K(\mu_{\ell}):K]\mid(\ell-1)
but
\mathfrak{a}
has order
\ell
in the class group of
K
, this is impossible.
This contradiction completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

B.2.
An auxiliary result

Suppose
p>2
is a fixed prime and
L
is a fixed imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{L}<0

Consider the set
\mathfrak{X}
of Hecke characters
\psi:K^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
of
varying imaginary quadratic fields such that

(i)

the prime
p
splits in
K
;

(ii)

the discriminant
-D_{K}
of
K
is odd;

(iii)

the infinity type of
\psi
is
(-1,0)
;

(iv)

\psi|_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}=\chi_{K}|\cdot|^{-1}
;

(v)

\mathfrak{f}_{\psi}=\mathfrak{d}_{K}\mathfrak{f}
with
(\mathfrak{d_{K}},\mathfrak{f})=1
;

(vi)

the root number
w(\psi)
of
\psi
is
-1
;

(vii)

every prime dividing
D_{K}N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{f}_{\psi})
splits in
L
;

(viii)

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,\psi)=1
.

Note that conditions (i)-(vi) imply that Theorem
A.1
holds for
\psi

Lemma B.2
.

If
L\neq{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-1}),{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-2})
, then
\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}}F_{\psi}={\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proof.

We will define a fairly explicit subset
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{X}
such that

\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}}F_{\psi}={\mathbb{Q}}
.

Let
\mathfrak{Q}
be the set of primes
q
such that

•

q\equiv 3\ \mathrm{mod}\ 8
;

•

-q
is a square modulo
p
;

•

-D_{L}
is a square modulo
q
.

The second and third conditions are equivalent to
q
belonging to an index two subgroup of
({\mathbb{Z}}/p{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}

and
({\mathbb{Z}}/D_{L}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
, respectively. In the case of the third condition, this subgroup –
the kernel of the character
\chi_{L}
– is not induced from any index two subgroup of any

({\mathbb{Z}}/D{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
with
D
a proper divisor of
D_{L}
.
Since
p
is odd and
D_{L}
has a prime factor not equal to
2
(this is where we use
the hypothesis that
L\neq{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-1}),{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-2})
), it is then easy to see that these conditions are satisfied
by infinitely many primes. That is,
\mathfrak{Q}
is an infinite set.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be the set of imaginary quadratic fields
K={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-q})
for

q\in\mathfrak{Q}
. This is then an infinite set of imaginary quadratic fields. The first condition on
q
implies
that the discriminant of
K
is
-D_{K}=-q
, which means that the class number of
K
is odd. The second
condition on
q
is then just the condition that
p
splits in
K
, while the third condition is that every
prime dividing
D_{K}
(which is just
q
!) splits in
L
.

For
K\in\mathfrak{K}
let
\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}
be the set of canonical Hecke characters of
K
of infinity type

(-1,0)
. The condition that
-D_{K}=-q\equiv 3\ \mathrm{mod}\ 8
means that the root number of each

\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}
is
-1
.
If
\ell
is a prime that splits in both
K
and
L
(which is an infinite set), then
for every anticyclotomic Hecke characters
\chi
of
K
of finite
\ell
-power
order and
\ell
-power conductor the root number of
\psi\chi
is also
-1
.
Moreover, by a theorem
of Rohrlich
[
119
]
for all but finitely many such
\chi
,

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,\psi\chi)=1
for all
\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}
;
let
\mathfrak{X}(K,\ell)
be the set of such
\chi
for which this holds.

Let
\mathfrak{S}(K)
be the set of primes that split in both
K
and
L
.
We then have

\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}:=\{\psi\chi\ :\ \psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}},\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(K,\ell),K\in\mathfrak{K},\ell\in\mathfrak{S}(K)\}\subset\mathfrak{X}.

We will next show that
\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}}F_{\psi}={\mathbb{Q}}
, which implies the lemma.

If
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(K,\ell)
then
F_{\psi\chi}\subset F_{\psi}(\mu_{\ell^{\infty}})
.
As
\mathfrak{S}(K)
is infinite it follows that

\cap_{\ell\in\mathfrak{S}(K)}\cap_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(K,\ell)}F_{\psi\chi}\subset\cap_{\ell\in\mathfrak{S}(K)}F_{\psi}(\mu_{\ell^{\infty}})\subset F_{\psi}.

So

\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}}\cap_{\ell\in\mathfrak{S}(K)}\cap_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(K,\ell)}F_{\psi\chi}\subset\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}}F_{\psi}=K,

the final equality being by Proposition
B.1
.
Hence

\cap_{K\in\mathfrak{K}}\cap_{\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{K}^{\mathrm{can}}}\cap_{\ell\in\mathfrak{S}(K)}\cap_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(K,\ell)}F_{\psi\chi}\subset\cap_{K\in\mathfrak{K}}K={\mathbb{Q}}.

∎

Part II
Iwasawa main conjectures and applications

Appendix I
Main conjectures: backdrop

This section presents main conjectures underlying Iwasawa theory of a weight two elliptic newform over the rationals and imaginary quadratic fields, and interrelations among them. We also recall the prior results towards the main conjectures.

I.1.
Selmer groups

The subsection introduces Selmer groups with varying local conditions (cf.
[
130
, §3]
).

I.1.1.
Over the rationals

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring.
Let
A=A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety
18
18

18

In fact there is an abelian variety
A_{0}
over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
{\mathbb{Z}}_{g}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{0})
such that
A_{g}=A_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{g}}\mathcal{O}
, where
{\mathbb{Z}}_{g}\subset\mathcal{O}
is the Hecke order.

over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
as in §
2.3.4
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime and
\Sigma
the set of primes dividing
Np
.
For a prime
\lambda
of the Hecke field
F
above
p
,
we may choose
A_{g}
and

\pi:X_{0}(N)\rightarrow A_{g}

to be an
(\mathcal{O},\lambda)
-optimal paramterisation in the sense of
[
149
, §3.7]
.
Let
T
be a lattice in the attached
p
-adic Galois representation
V=V_{F_{\lambda}}
.
Often, we consider the lattice arising from the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
A=A_{g}
.
Define

W=V/T,

which is a discrete
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}
-divisible
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module.
In the Tate lattice case it is isomorphic to the
p
-divisible group

A[\lambda^{\infty}]
.
Let
{\mathrm{Sel}}(g)
be the Bloch–Kato Selmer group associated to
W(1)
,
{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A)
the Selmer group associated to
A[\lambda^{\infty}]
and
\hbox{\cyr X}(A)[\lambda^{\infty}]
the Tate–Shafarevich group.

Let

M=T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda^{\vee}

be a discrete
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module with the
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-action on
\Lambda^{\vee}
via the inverse of the canonical character
\Psi
(cf. (
2.1
)).

Ordinary and signed Selmer groups.

For an ordinary prime
p\nmid N
, there is an
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-filtration

0\subset T^{+}\subset T

with
{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}T^{+}=1.

Define

S(g)=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{\Sigma},M)\rightarrow\prod_{v\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v},M)\times H^{1}(I_{p},T/T^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda^{\vee})\big\},

(I.1)

a discrete
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module.
Its Pontryagin dual

X(g)={\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathrm{cont}}}(S(g),{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

is a finitely generated compact
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-module.

Remark I.1
.

In the above definition
\prod_{v\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v},M)
may be replaced with

\prod_{v\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}(I_{v},M)
.

We now consider the supersingular case. Let
p\nmid 2N
be a non-ordinary prime so that

a_{g}(p)=0
, which we refer to as a supersingular prime.
For
\circ\in\{+,-\}
,
let

H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},M)\subset H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},M)

be the annihilator of Kobayashi’s signed submodule

H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)\subset H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)
under the Pontryagin duality pairing (cf. §
3.1
).
Define

S_{\circ}(g)=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{\Sigma},M)\rightarrow\prod_{v\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v},M)\times\frac{H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},M)}{H^{1}_{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},M)}\big\}

(I.2)

and let
X_{\circ}(g)
denote its Pontryagin dual.

Strict Selmer groups.

For a newform
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
and a prime
p
,
define

S_{{\mathrm{st}}}(g)=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{\Sigma},M)\rightarrow\prod_{v\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v},M)\times H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},M)\big\}

(I.3)

and let
X_{{\mathrm{st}}}(g)
denote its Pontryagin dual.

I.1.2.
Over imaginary quadratic fields

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the conditions (
2.15
) and (
I.9
),
and so
(p)=v\overline{v}
with
v
determined via the embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.

Let
{\mathrm{Sel}}(g_{/L})
denote the Bloch–Kato Selmer group associated to
W(1)
when viewed as a
G_{L}
-module and

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{/L})
the

\lambda^{\infty}
-Selmer group over
L
.
For
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,{\mathrm{cyc}},\mathrm{ac}\}
,
let

\mathcal{M}^{\cdot}=T(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot,\vee}

be a discrete
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}^{\cdot}
-module
with the
G_{L}
-action on
\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot,\vee}
via the inverse of the canonical character
\Psi_{L}^{\cdot}
(cf. §
4.1.5
).

Ordinary and signed Selmer groups.
For
p\nmid 2N
an ordinary prime, define
a discrete
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-module

S^{\cdot}(g_{/L})=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},\mathcal{M}^{\cdot})\rightarrow\prod_{v\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}(L_{v},\mathcal{M}^{\cdot})\times\prod_{w|p}H^{1}(I_{w},T(1)/T^{+}(1)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot,\vee})\big\},

(I.4)

where we let
\Sigma=\Sigma_{L}
also denote the set of places of
L
over
\Sigma
.
Let

X^{\cdot}(g_{/L})

be the Pontryagin dual.

For
p\nmid 2N
a supersingular prime, define

S_{\circ}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},\mathcal{M}^{\cdot})\rightarrow\prod_{v\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}(L_{v},\mathcal{M}^{\cdot})\times\prod_{w|p}\frac{H^{1}(L_{w},{\mathcal{M}}^{\cdot})}{H^{1}_{\circ}(L_{w},{\mathcal{M}}^{\cdot})}\big\}

(I.5)

and let
X_{\circ}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})
denote its Pontryagin dual.

Strict Selmer groups.

For a newform
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
and an ordinary or a supersingular prime
p\nmid 2N
, let

S_{{\mathrm{st}},\bullet}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})\subset S_{\bullet}^{\cdot}(g_{/L}),\ \ \bullet=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{ord}}&\text{$g$ is ordinary}\\
\circ&\text{$g$ is supersingular}\end{cases}

be the submodule consisting of
\kappa\in S^{\cdot}_{\bullet}(g_{/L})
such that

{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}\kappa=0.

Let
X^{\cdot}_{{\mathrm{st}},\bullet}(g_{/L})
denote its Pontryagin dual.

Greenberg Selmer groups.
Define

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},W(1))\rightarrow\prod_{w\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}(L_{w},W(1))\times H^{1}(L_{v},W(1))\times\frac{H^{1}(L_{\overline{v}},W(1))}{H^{1}(L_{\overline{v}},W(1))_{{\mathrm{div}}}}\big\}

(I.6)

and

S_{\rm{Gr}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},\mathcal{M}^{\cdot})\rightarrow\prod_{w\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}(L_{w},\mathcal{M}^{\cdot})\times H^{1}(L_{v},\mathcal{M}^{\cdot})\big\},

(I.7)

the latter a discrete
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-module.
Let

X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})
denote the Pontryagin dual.

Remark I.2
.

(i)

In the cyclotomic definition
\prod_{w\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}(L_{w},\mathcal{M}^{\rm cyc})
may be replaced with

\prod_{w\in\Sigma,v\nmid p}H^{1}(I_{w},\mathcal{M}^{\rm cyc})
.

(ii)

The Greenberg Selmer groups arise from interpolation of Bloch–Kato Selmer groups associated to twists of
T(1)
by certain infinite order Hecke characters over
L
.

I.2.
The main conjectures

I.2.1.
Over the rationals

Cyclotomic main conjectures with
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Conjecture I.3
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
p\nmid N
a prime of ordinary reduction and
\alpha
the
p
-unit root of the Hecke polynomial at
p
.
Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
,
\gamma\in V_{F}
with
\gamma^{\pm}\neq 0
and
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g)
be the associated
p
-adic
L
-function.

(a)

X(g)
is
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-torsion.

(b)

For
\omega
good (cf. §
2.2.6
) and
\gamma=\gamma_{g}
as in Lemma
2.5
, we have an equality of ideals

(\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma}(g))=\xi(X(g)),

in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and even in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
if (irr
Q
) holds for
p\neq 2
.

In the supersingular case
Kobayashi
[
87
, §1]
proposed the following:

Conjecture I.4
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
p\nmid 2N
a prime of supersingular reduction and
\circ\in\{+,-\}
.

(a)

X_{\circ}(g)
is
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-torsion.

(b)

For
\gamma=\gamma_{g}
as in Lemma
2.5
, we have an equality of ideals

(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}^{\circ}(g))=\xi(X_{\circ}(g))

in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
.

Remark I.5
.

Kobayashi’s original formulation concerns supersingular elliptic curves. The above generalisation to weight two newforms is due to Lei
[
92
, Conj. 1.2]
.

For the choice of
\gamma
as in Conjecture
I.4
(b), we let
({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ}(g))
denote
({\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma}^{\circ}(g))
.

Kato’s main conjecture.
The following
links Kato’s zeta element with the strict Selmer group
(cf.
[
79
, Conj. 12.10]
).

Conjecture I.6
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p
a prime.

(a)

X_{{\mathrm{st}}}(g)
is
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-torsion.

(b)

For

\gamma=\gamma_{g}
as in Lemma
2.5
, we have an equality of ideals

\xi\big(H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)/\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\cdot{{\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(g)}\big)=\xi(X_{{\mathrm{st}}}(g))

in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and even in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
if (irr
Q
) holds for
p\neq 2
.

Remark I.7
.

(i)

The conjecture is uniformly formulated for all primes
p
.

(ii)

The lower bound for
X_{{\mathrm{st}}}(g)
as predicted by Conjecture
I.6
(b) has an application to Conjecture
7.1
: In combination with
[
91
]
(or §
K.2.2
), the lower bound leads to the implication

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1\implies{\mathrm{loc}}_{p}(z_{\rm{Kato}}(g))\neq 0

for any prime
p
.

I.2.2.
Over imaginary quadratic fields

Let
(g,p,L)
be as above
and
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
denote the quadratic twist.

For an ordinary or a supersingular prime
p\nmid N
and
\circ\in\{+,-\}
, put

\Box=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\text{$g$ is ordinary}\\
\circ&\text{$g$ is supersingular},\end{cases}\ \bullet=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{ord}}&\text{$g$ is ordinary}\\
\circ&\text{$g$ is supersingular.}\end{cases}

(I.8)

Cyclotomic main conjectures with standard
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Conjecture I.8
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
p\nmid N
a prime of ordinary reduction and
\alpha
the
p
-unit root of the Hecke polynomial at
p
.
Let
0\neq\omega\in S_{F}
,
\gamma\in V_{F}
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}_{F}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L})
be the associated
p
-adic
L
-function.

(a)

X^{\rm cyc}(g_{/L})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
-torsion.

(b)

For
\omega
good (cf. §
2.2.6
),
\gamma=\gamma_{g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma_{g^{\prime}}
as in Lemma
2.5
, we have an equality of ideals

(\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\omega,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(g_{/L}))=\xi(X(g_{/L}))

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and even in

\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
if (irr
L
) holds for
p\neq 2
.

Conjecture I.9
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
p\nmid 2N
a prime of supersingular reduction and

\circ\in\{+,-\}
.

(a)

X_{\circ}(g_{/L})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
-torsion.

(b)

For
\gamma=\gamma_{g}
and
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma_{g^{\prime}}
as in Lemma
2.5
, we have an equality of ideals

(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}(g_{/L}))=\xi(X_{\circ}(g_{/L}))

in

\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
.

Main conjectures with standard
p
-adic
L
-functions, bis.

Conjecture I.10
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
p\nmid 2N

an ordinary or a supersingular prime, and
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,{\mathrm{cyc}},\mathrm{ac}\}
. In the case
\cdot=\mathrm{ac}
suppose that the root number of
E
over
L
equals
+1
.

(a)

X^{\cdot}_{\bullet}(g_{/L})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-torsion.

(b)

We have an equality of ideals

(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\Box,\cdot}(g_{/L}))=\xi(X_{\bullet}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})),

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and even in
\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot}
if
({\rm irr}_{L})
holds.

Remark I.11
.

(i)

Note that the cyclotomic case of the above conjecture is nothing but Conjecture
I.8
(resp. Conjecture
I.9
) in the ordinary (resp. supersingular) case.

(ii)

A related two-variable main conjecture for supersingular elliptic curves has been proposed by Kim
[
81
]
. Its anticyclotomic counterpart goes back to Darmon–Iovita
[
45
]
.

Greenberg main conjecture.

Conjecture I.12
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
p\nmid N
a prime and
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,{\mathrm{cyc}},\mathrm{ac}\}
. In the case
\cdot=\mathrm{ac}
suppose that the root number of
E
over
L
equals
-1
.

(a)

X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-torsion.

(b)

We have an equality of ideals

(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\cdot}(g_{/L}))=\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})),

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{ur}},\cdot}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and even in
\Lambda_{L}^{{\mathrm{ur}},\cdot}
for
p\neq 2
.

Remark I.13
.

(i)

The conjecture is uniformly formulated for all primes
p
of good reduction.
It is also independent of the choice of lattice (cf.
[
90
, Prop. 2.9]
).

(ii)

The cyclotomic projection
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
does not interpolate classical
L
-values. Yet the cyclotomic Greenberg main conjecture is elemental in our approach to the
p
-part of the BSD formula (cf. §
K.3
) and
p
-converse to the Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin theorem (cf. §
L
).

(iii)

The non-vanishing of

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})

is an open problem.
It can be verified in certain rank one situations (cf. (
K.3
)).

Zeta element main conjecture.

Conjecture I.14
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p\nmid 2N
an ordinary or a supersingular prime.
For
\bullet,\Box
as in (
I.8
) and

\cdot\in\{\emptyset,{\mathrm{cyc}},\mathrm{ac}\}
,
let

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\Box,\cdot}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}_{\rm{rel},\bullet}(L,T\otimes\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot})

be the
corresponding projection of the two-variable zeta element
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\Box}(g_{/L})
.

(a)

X_{{\mathrm{st}},\bullet}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-torsion.

(b)

We have an equality of ideals

\xi\big(H^{1}_{\rm{rel},\bullet}(L,T\otimes\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot})/\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\cdot{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\Box,\cdot}(g_{/L})}\big)=\xi(X_{{\mathrm{st}},\bullet}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})),

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and even in

\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
if
({\rm van}_{L})
holds.

Heegner main conjecture
.
Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p
an odd prime of good ordinary reduction. Suppose that
({\mathrm{van}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
holds.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the conditions (
Heeg
) and
({\mathrm{van}}_{L})
, and so that
p\nmid D_{L}
.

In view of the Heegner hypothesis (
Heeg
) and the ordinarity assumption, the Kummer image of CM points on
X_{0}(N)
over ring class fields of
p
-power conductor of
L
under an
(\mathcal{O},\lambda)
-optimal modular parametrisation
\pi:X_{0}(N)\rightarrow A_{g}
give rise to an Iwasawa-theoretic Heegner class

\kappa_{g}\in H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}})

(cf.
[
7
, (10)]
). The Iwasawa theory of varying Heegner points is encaptured by the following conjecture of Perrin-Riou
[
111
]
.

Conjecture I.15
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p
an odd prime of good ordinary reduction.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
Heeg
) and
({\mathrm{van}}_{L})
, and so that
p\nmid D_{L}
.

(a)

The Heegner class
\kappa_{g}\in H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}
-non-torsion. Moreover,

{\mathrm{rank}}_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}})={\mathrm{rank}}_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}}^{\mathrm{ac}}X^{\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L})=1.

(b)

We have

\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}\bigg(\frac{H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}{)}}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}\cdot\kappa_{g}}\bigg)\cdot\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}\bigg(\frac{H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}\cdot\kappa_{g}^{\iota}}\bigg)=\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}(X(g)_{{\mathrm{tor}}}).

I.3.
Main conjectures and zeta elements

We describe connections between some of the main conjectures in section
I.2
based on zeta elements.

I.3.1.
Via Kato’s zeta element

In light of Theorem
3.14
we have the following (cf.
[
87
, Thm. 7.4]
).

Lemma I.16
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform.

(i)

For a prime
p\nmid N
of ordinary reduction, a one-sided divisibility in Conjecture
I.3
implies the analogous divisibility in Conjecture

I.6
and conversely. In particular, Conjectures
I.3
amd
I.6
are equivalent.

(ii)

For a prime
p\nmid 2N
of supersingular reduction, the assertions as in part (i) hold for
Conjectures
I.4
and

I.6
.

A relation between cyclotomic main conjectures over the rationals and imaginary quadratic fields:

Lemma I.17
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p
a prime.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
) and

(D_{L},N)=1.

(I.9)

(i)

For a prime
p\nmid N
of ordinary reduction, a one-sided divisibility in
Conjecture
I.3
for
g
and the quadratic twist
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
imply the analogous divisibility in Conjecture
I.8
for
g
. In particular, Conjecture
I.3
for
g
and
g^{\prime}
imply Conjecture
I.8
for
g
.

(ii)

For a prime
p\nmid 2N
of supersingular reduction, the assertions as in part (i) hold for
Conjectures
I.4
and
I.9
.

Proof.

We only consider the ordinary case, an analogous argument applies in the supersingular case.

Note that

\xi(X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))=\xi(X(g))\cdot\xi(X(g^{\prime}))

(I.10)

(cf.
[
131
, Lem. 3.1.5 & Prop. 3.2.3]
).
In view of the definition of the
p
-adic
L
-functions over
L
as in §
3.4.3
the assertion follows.
∎

I.3.2.
Via two-variable zeta element

Proposition I.18
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p\nmid 2N
an ordinary or a supersingular prime.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the conditions (
2.15
), (
I.9
) and
({\rm van}_{L})
so that
(p)=v\overline{v}
with
v
determined via the embedding
\iota_{p}
.
For
\bullet,\Box
as in (
I.8
) and

\cdot\in\{\emptyset,{\mathrm{cyc}},\mathrm{ac}\}
,
let

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\Box,\cdot}(g_{/L})\in H^{1}_{\rm{rel},\bullet}(L,T\otimes\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot})

be the projection of the two-variable zeta element
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\Box}(g_{/L})
.
In the case
\cdot={\mathrm{cyc}}
suppose that

{\mathrm{loc}}_{v}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\Box,{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))
and
{\mathrm{loc}}_{\overline{v}}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\Box,{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))
are
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-non-torsion.

(nv)

Then for
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,{\mathrm{cyc}}\}
a one-sided divisibility in one of the Conjectures
I.10
,
I.12
and
I.14

implies the analogous divisibility in the other conjectures.
Moreover, in the case
\cdot=\mathrm{ac}
and
\epsilon(E_{/L})=+1
(resp.
\epsilon(E_{/L})=-1
) a one sided-divisibility in Conjecture
I.14
implies the analogous divisibility in Conjecture
I.10
(resp. Conjecture
I.12
).
In particular, the corresponding subset of Conjectures
I.10
,
I.12
and
I.14
are equivalent.
Without the condition
({\rm van}_{L})
, these assertions still hold in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

In particular, Kato’s main Conjecture
I.6
for
g
and its quadratic twist
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
imply the cyclotomic Greenberg main Conjecture
I.12
for
g
over
L
.

Proof.

We present the ordinary case
assuming the hypothesis
(\rm van_{L})
, and leave the supersingular case to the interested reader.

To begin, the Poitou–Tate global duality (cf.
[
76
, Thm. 2.3.2]
and
[
103
, Thm. 2.3.4]
) yields an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}_{\rm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot})}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\cdot{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})}}\rightarrow\frac{{\mathrm{Im}}(H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L_{\overline{v}},T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot}))}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\cdot{\mathrm{loc}}_{\overline{v}}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L}))}\rightarrow X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})\rightarrow X_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})\rightarrow 0

(I.11)

of
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-modules,
where the second (resp. third) map arises from the localisation at
v
(resp. the dual of the localisation at
\overline{v}
).
Note that the exactness on the left is a consequence of

-

The non-vanishing (
nv
),

-

The fact that

H^{1}_{\rm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T\otimes\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
torsion-free with rank one,

(rk)

-

Moreover, in the case
\cdot=\mathrm{ac}
, the hypothesis that
\epsilon(E_{/L})=-1
.

In regards to the fact (
rk
), it is simply the content of Theorem
3.9
(b) for
\cdot={\mathrm{cyc}}
, which in turn implies the case
\cdot=\emptyset
.
By the second explicit reciprocity law (cf. §
5.5
), we have an analogous exact sequence

0\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}_{\rm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot,{\mathrm{ur}}})}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot,{\mathrm{ur}}}\cdot{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})}}\rightarrow\frac{\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot,{\mathrm{ur}}}}{\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot,{\mathrm{ur}}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\cdot}(g_{/L})}\rightarrow X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\cdot,{\mathrm{ur}}}(g_{/L})\rightarrow X_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}^{\cdot,{\mathrm{ur}}}(g_{/L})\rightarrow 0

(I.12)

of
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-modules.

On the other hand, switching the role of
v
and
\overline{v}
in the preceding analysis and utilising the first explicit reciprocity law, we obtain the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}_{\rm{rel},{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L}^{\cdot})}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})}\rightarrow\frac{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})}\rightarrow X^{\cdot}(g_{/L})\rightarrow X_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})\rightarrow 0

(I.13)

of
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-modules.
Here the exactness on the left is a consequence of

-

Proposition
5.25
,

-

the fact (
rk
),

-

Moreover, in the case
\cdot={\mathrm{cyc}}
, we utilise Remark
3.21
, and in the case
\cdot=\mathrm{ac}
, the hypothesis that
\epsilon(E_{/L})=+1
.

By the exact sequence (
I.13
),
X_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})
is

\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-torsion.
Hence, in light of (
I.12
) a divisibility in the Greenberg main conjecture for
g
is equivalent to the analogous divisibility in Conjecture
I.14
.
The latter is also equivalent to the analogous divisibility in Conjecture
I.10

by
(
I.13
).

Without the condition (van
L
), the above analysis still applies by considering exact sequences of
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-modules.
Note that the ‘In particular’ part just follows by Lemma
I.17
.

∎

Remark I.19
.

(i)

As seen in the proof,
X_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}^{\cdot}(g_{/L})
is
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\cdot}
-torsion.

(ii)

Anticyclotomic variants of the above main conjectures are also equivalent. In this setting (
rk
) is a consequence of the existence of an Euler system.

I.3.3.
Via Heegner points

Proposition I.20
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p
an odd prime of good ordinary reduction.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
spl
), (
Heeg
) and
({\mathrm{van}}_{L})
.
Then a one-sided divisibility in the anticyclotomic case of Conjecture
I.12
implies an analogous divisibility in Conjecture
L.2
, and conversely.
In particular, the anticyclotomic counterpart of Conjectures
I.12
and
L.2
are equivalent.

The proposition is a consequence of the
p
-adic Waldspurger formula
[
11
]
, interpreted as an explicit reciprocity law for the Heegner class
\kappa_{g}
(cf.
[
7
, Thm. 5.2]
).
