Appendix J
Kobayashi’s main conjecture

The aim of this section is to prove Conjecture
I.4
in the semistable case.

J.1.
Main results

J.1.1.

Theorem J.1
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform with
N
square-free, and
p\nmid 2N
a prime of supersingular reduction. Then Kobayashi’s main Conjecture
I.4
is true, that is,

(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\circ}(g))=\xi(X_{\circ}(g))

for
\circ\in\{+,-\}
.
Moreover, the same holds for
g_{K}:=g\otimes\chi_{K}
for any quadratic field extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}}
with discriminant coprime to
Np
.

Theorem
J.1
is proved is subsection
J.3
.

J.1.2.
p
-part of the BSD formula

As first observed by Kobayshi
[
87
,
88
]
, his main conjecture has the following
application to the
p
-part of the conjectural BSD formula:

Corollary J.2
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform with
N
square-free, and
F
the Hecke field with degree
d
.
Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime so that
a_{p}(g)=0
.
If
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=r\leq 1
, then the
p
-part of the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture for
A_{g}
is true, that is,
{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{g}({\mathbb{Q}})=rd
,
\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})[p^{\infty}]
is finite and

\bigg|\frac{L^{(rd)}(1,A_{g})}{d!\cdot\Omega_{A_{g}}R(A_{g})}\bigg|^{-1}_{p}=\big|\#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})[p^{\infty}]\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(A_{g})\big|^{-1}_{p}.

Moreover, the same holds for
g\otimes\chi_{K}
for quadratic field extensions
K/{\mathbb{Q}}
with discriminant coprime to
Np
.

Proof.

Note that

L(s,A_{g})=\prod_{\sigma:F\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}}L(s,g^{\sigma}).

Since the BSD conjecture is isogeny invariant, we may assume that
\mathcal{O}_{F}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}{A_{g}}
.
It suffices to consider the
\lambda
-part of the BSD formula for any prime
\lambda
of
F
above
p
. Choose an embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
so that it induces the place
\lambda
.

In view of Theorem
J.1

Corollary
I.16
, Kato’s main Conjecture
I.6
is true.
In the
r=0
case
it implies the
\lambda
-part of the BSD conjecture for
A_{g}
over
{\mathbb{Q}}
, as observed by Kato
(cf.
[
79
, §14.20]
, see also the proof of
[
131
, Thm. 3.6.13]
and
[
76
, §7.2]
).
In the
r=1
case
{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{g}({\mathbb{Q}})=rd
and
\hbox{\cyr X}(A)[\lambda^{\infty}]
is finite by the Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin theorem (alternatively see Theorems
K.6
and
K.8
).
In the
r=1
non-ordinary case,
Kato’s main conjecture in combination with the
\lambda
-adic Gross–Zagier formula implies the
\lambda
-part of the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer formula,
as observed by Kobayashi
(cf.
[
88
]
,
[
89
]
,
[
23
, Cor. A.5]
).
∎

Remark J.3
.

(i)

The rank zero BSD formula also follows by descent of Kobayashi’s main Conjecture
I.4
.

(ii)

As for the rank one case, Jetchev–Skinner–Wan
[
76
]
have proposed a different approach.
It is based on the
p
-adic Waldspurger formula
[
11
]
, yet
relies on the
r=0
case of Corollary
J.2
. Indeed their approach first proves the rank one BSD formula over an imaginary quadratic field, and relies on the
r=0
case of Corollary
J.2
to isolate the desired rank one formula over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

(iii)

If
g
has CM and
r=0
, then the full Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture is recently proved
[
17
]
, building on the work of Rubin
[
121
]
.

J.2.
Elements of the proof

J.2.1.
An Euler system divisibility

Theorem J.4
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform, and
p\nmid 2N
a prime of supersingular reduction.

(a)

X_{\circ}(g)
is
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
-torsion for
\circ\in\{+,-\}
.

(b)

We have a divisibility of ideals

\xi(X_{\circ}(g))|({\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}_{p}(g))

in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and even in
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
under the condition (
im
).

This theorem is due to Kobayashi
[
87
, Theorems 1.2 and 1.3]
for elliptic curves, and Lei
[
92
, Prop. 6.4 and Rem. 6.10]
. It is a consequence of Kato’s Theorem
I.21
.

J.2.2.
An Eisenstein congruence divisibility

Theorem J.5
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform with
N
square-free and
p\nmid 2N
a prime. Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying
(D_{L},2N)=1
and (
2.15
). In the ordinary case suppose that
({\rm irr}_{L})
holds.
Write
N=N^{+}N^{-}
for
N^{+}
precisely divisible by split primes in
L
. Suppose that either

Each prime dividing
N^{-}
satisfies (
ram
) and
\nu(N^{-})
is odd,

(def)

or

Each prime dividing
N^{-}\neq 1
satisfies (
ram
) and
\nu(N^{-})
is even.

(indef)

Then for
\circ\in\{+,-,\emptyset\}
, one has

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\circ}(g_{/L})\big|\xi(X_{\circ}(g_{/L}))

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}
. Moreover, the same holds for
g_{K}:=g\otimes\chi_{K}
for any quadratic field extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}}
with discriminant coprime to
Np
.

Proof.

In the supersingular case
\bar{T}
is an irreducible
k_{\lambda}[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}]
-module, and the hypothesis
({\rm irr}_{L})
holds by (
spl
). So the hypotheses of Theorem
I.24
(a) are satisfied, and we have the divisibility

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\big|\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\rm ur}(g_{/L}))

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}},{\mathrm{ur}}}}{\mathrm{Frac}}(\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}},{\mathrm{ur}}})
.
In turn, by the proof of Proposition
I.18
, we have the divisibility

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\circ}(g_{/L})\big|\xi(X_{\circ}(g_{/L}))

(J.1)

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}}{\mathrm{Frac}}(\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})
.

Suppose that (
def
) holds. Then
\epsilon(g_{/L})=+1
, and

\mu({\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}_{\mathscr{W}}(g_{/L}))=0

by
[
116
, Thm. 1.2]
.
Here
{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}_{\mathscr{W}}(g_{/L})
is the
\circ
-anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function whose construction is based on the Waldspurger formula on the definite quaternion algebra ramified at the primes dividing
N^{-}{}\infty
(see also
[
137
]
).
In view of the interpolation formulas for the underlying
p
-adic
L
-functions, it follows that

({\mathcal{L}}^{\circ,\mathrm{ac}}_{p}(g_{/L}))=(\prod_{q|N^{-}}c_{q}(g)\cdot{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}_{\mathscr{W}}(g_{/L})).

Note that the Tamagawa numbers
c_{q}(g)
are
p
-indivisible under the hypothesis (
ram
),
and so

\mu({\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}_{p}(g_{/L}))=0.

Hence
{\mathcal{L}}^{\circ}_{p}(g_{/L})
is coprime to height one prime ideals of

\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
, and the divisibility (
J.1
) holds in
\Lambda_{L}
.

In the same vein, the
\mu
-invariant of the BDP
p
-adic
L
-function vanishes
[
21
]
under (
indef
), and the argument in the preceding paragraph applies.
∎

Remark J.6
.

The hypothesis (
indef
) may be generalised as in
[
39
, Thm. 8.2.3(2)]
.

J.2.3.
A control theorem

Proposition J.7
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p\nmid 2N

a supersingular prime. Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field such that (
2.15
) holds.
Then we have

X_{\circ}(g_{/L})/(\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)\simeq X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}_{\circ}(g_{/L}).

In particular

\xi(X_{\circ}(g_{/L}))\ \mathrm{mod}\ (\gamma_{\mathrm{ac}}-1)\big|\xi(X_{\circ}(g))\cdot\xi(X_{\circ}(g\otimes\chi_{L})).

Moreover, an analogous divisibility holds along the anticyclotomic tower.

Proof.

One may proceed as in the proof of
[
131
, Prop. 3.9]
(see also
[
87
, §9]
). Note that ‘In particular’ part follows from supersingular analogue of the factorsiation (
I.10
) (cf.
[
131
, Cor. 3.8(ii)]
).
∎

J.3.
Proof of Theorem
J.1

Proof.

Since
g
is semistable, there exists a prime
q|N
satisfying the condition (
ram
) by Ribet’s level raising.
Pick an imaginary quadratic field
L
such that (
2.15
) holds and

(D_{L},2N)=1
. Suppose also that either
q
is inert in
L
and the primes dividing
N/q
split, or that (
def
) holds.

Recall that

{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ,{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})={\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma}(g)\cdot{\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})

for
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
, and

\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
as in Lemma
2.5
.
So Theorem
J.5
in combination with Proposition
J.7
implies that

{\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma}(g)\cdot{\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\big|\xi(X_{\circ}(g))\cdot\xi(X_{\circ}(g^{\prime})).

On the other hand, we have

\xi(X_{\circ}(g))|{\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma}^{\circ}(g)
and
\xi(X_{\circ}(g^{\prime}))|{\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}(g^{\prime})

by Theorem
J.4
(b) (cf. Remark
I.22
).
Therefore,

\xi(X_{\circ}(g))=({\mathcal{L}}_{\gamma}^{\circ}(g)).

The same argument applies for the quadratic twist
g_{K}
.
∎

J.4.
Complements

J.4.1.
Supersingular main conjecture, bis

Theorem J.8
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform with
N
square-free and
p\nmid 2N
a supersingular prime. Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field such that
(D_{L},2N)=1
, and (
2.15
) holds.
Suppose also that either the condition (
def
) or (
indef
) holds.
Then Conjecture
I.10
is true, that is,
for
\circ\in\{+,-\}
and
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,{\mathrm{cyc}}\}
, we have

\xi(X_{\circ}^{\cdot}(g_{/L}))=(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\circ,\cdot}(g_{/L})).

Moreover, the same holds for
\cdot=\mathrm{ac}
under the condition (
def
).
In particular Conjecture
I.12
is true for
\cdot=\emptyset

Moreover, the same holds for
g_{K}:=g\otimes\chi_{K}
for any quadratic field extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}}
with discriminant coprime to
Np
.

Proof.

Since Theorem
J.1
applies to
g
as well as
g\otimes\chi_{L}
, we have

({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ,{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))=\xi(X_{\circ}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))

by Lemma
I.17
.
On the other hand,
Theorem
J.5
gives the two-variable divisibility
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ}(g_{/L})|\xi(X_{\circ}(g_{/L}))
.
Hence, noting the non-vanishing of

{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ,{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
,
the two-variable main conjecture

({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ}(g_{/L}))=\xi(X_{\circ}(g_{/L}))

follows (cf.
[
131
, Lem. 3.2]
).
In turn, noting the non-vanishing of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ,\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L})
under (
def
),
the anticyclotomic main conjecture follows by descent (cf. Proposition
J.7
).

Note that the ‘In particular’ part is a consequence of Proposition
I.18
.
∎

Remark J.9
.

If the condition (
indef
) holds, then the anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ,\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L})
vanishes by the interpolation property.

J.4.2.
Ordinary main conjecture

.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))

an ordinary prime such that
(\rm irr_{{\mathbb{Q}}})

) and

. Suppose that either the condition (

and

are true.

Moreover, the same holds for

.

Proof.

.
∎

Remark J.11
.

Part (a) gives a different proof of a special case of Theorem
I.21
(c). In the (
def
) case part (b) is also a special case of a result
[

, Thm. 3.30]
of Skinner–Urban towards Conjecture

. In the remaining cases Theorem

presents new evidence towards Conjectures

and

.

J.4.3.
The Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer formula: a rank zero quadratic twist family

Theorem J.12
.

Let
E
be a semistable elliptic curve defined over
\mathbb{Q}
with conductor
N
, and
M>1
a square-free integer with
(M,N)=1
. Let
E^{(M)}
denote the quadratic twist of
E
by the character associated to the quadratic extension
\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{M})/{\mathbb{Q}}
. Suppose that the following conditions hold.

(i)

We have

L(1,E^{(M)})\not=0,

(ii)

The
2
-part of the BSD formula holds for
E^{(M)}
,

(iii)

a_{3}(E)=0
,

(iv)

For all odd primes
p
,
E[p]
is an absolutely irreducible
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-representation,

(v)

For any prime
p|N
, there exists a multiplicative prime
q\neq p
at which
E[p]
is ramified,

(vi)

E
has ordinary reduction at prime divisors of
M
.

Then the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture is true for
E^{(M)}
, that is,
E^{(M)}({\mathbb{Q}})
and
\hbox{\cyr X}(E^{(M)})
are finite, and

\frac{L(1,E^{(M)})}{\Omega_{E^{(M)}}}=\frac{\#\hbox{\cyr X}(E^{(M)})\cdot\prod_{\ell\nmid\infty}c_{\ell}(E^{(M)})}{\#E^{(M)}({\mathbb{Q}})_{{\mathrm{tor}}}^{2}}.

Proof.

In view of the condition (i), note that

E^{(M)}({\mathbb{Q}})
and
\hbox{\cyr X}(E^{(M)})
are finite by the Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin theorem.

As for the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer formula, the archimedian part holds by the non-negativity of the central
L
-value
L(1,E^{(M)})
due to Kohnen–Zagier
[
80
]
.
In view of (ii), it suffices to consider the
p
-part of the BSD formula for odd primes
p
.
For such primes of good ordinary, multiplicative or supersingular reduction, the formula is the content of

[
131
, Thm. 2]
,
[
128
, Thm. C]
and Corollary
J.2
, respectively.
Lastly, for odd primes
p
dividing
M
it is a consequence of Theorem
I.21
(c) (cf. the proof of Corollary
J.2
).
∎

Remark J.13
.

(i)

As for the condition (vi), recall that
E
has ordinary reduction at primes of density one since
E
is non-CM.
In practice the condition may be verified by considering Tamagawa numbers of the quadratic twist.
It holds for square-free products of an explicit set of primes of positive density (cf.
[
41
]
).

(ii)

The
2
-part of the BSD formula as in (ii) is known to hold for example under the conditions of
[
41
, Thm. 1.5]
.

Example 1
.

For semistable elliptic curves
E
up to conductor 150,
the conditions of Theorem
J.12
are satisfied by
22
22

22

We are grateful to Shuai Zhai for his assistance in finding these examples.
the curves denoted in the LMFDB database by

•

46a1, 69a1, 77c1, 94a1, 114b1, 141b1 and 142c1,

•

62a1, 66b1, 105a1, 106d1, 115a1, 118c1, 118d1, 141c1 and 141e1

for infinitely many
M
.
Here for the elliptic curves in the first bullet point we rely on
[
41
, Thm. 1.5]
for the conditions (i)-(ii), and for the curves in the second bullet point on
[
148
, Thm. 1]
.
This gives the first infinite families of non-CM elliptic curves satisfying the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture.

Appendix K
Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin theorem revisited

This section presents a new approach to the Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin theorem based on the Euler system of Beilinson–Kato elements and the two-variable zeta element. It leads to an optimal upper bound for the Tate–Shafarevich group in terms of index of a Heegner point as predicted by the BSD conjecture. Moreover, in combination with cyclotomic Greenberg main conjecture it leads to rank one cases of the
p
-part of the BSD formula.

K.1.
Cyclotomic Greenberg Selmer groups

We begin with some preliminaries.

K.1.1.
A control theorem

Proposition K.1
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform and
p\nmid 2N
a prime.
For
\lambda
a prime of the Hecke field above
p
,
let
(\rho,V)
be the associated Galois representation,
T\subset V
a Galois stable lattice and

W=V/T.

Suppose that
({\rm van}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
holds.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the conditions (
2.15
), (
I.9
) and
({\rm van}_{L})
.
Suppose that the Selmer group
{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})
is finite.

(a)

X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
is a torsion
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
-module.

(b)

We have an exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\rightarrow S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})[\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}-1]\rightarrow\prod_{w|N}H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ur}}}(I_{w},W)\times H_{v}\times H_{{\overline{v}}}\rightarrow 0,

where

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ur}}}(I_{w},W)=\ker\big\{H^{1}(L_{w},W)\rightarrow H^{1}(I_{w},W)\big\}

and

H_{v}=\ker\big\{H^{1}(L_{v},W)\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{v},W\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}[\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}-1])\big\}\simeq\frac{(W\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})^{G_{L_{v}}}}{[\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}-1](W\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})^{G_{L_{v}}}},

H_{{\overline{v}}}=\frac{H^{1}(L_{{\overline{v}}},W)}{H^{1}(L_{{\overline{v}}},W)_{{\mathrm{div}}}}\simeq H^{0}(L_{{\overline{v}}},W)^{\vee}.

Proof.

The following is based on
[
76
, Thm. 3.3.1]
and
[
130
, Prop. 12]
(see also
[
59
, §4]
).

Put

\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}=\prod_{w\in\Sigma}\mathcal{P}_{w}

for

\mathcal{P}_{w}=\begin{cases}H^{1}(I_{w},\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})^{G_{L_{w}}}&\text{$w\nmid p$}\\
H^{1}(L_{v},\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})&\text{$w=v$}\\
0&\text{$w=\overline{v}$}.\\
\end{cases}

By definition,

S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})=\ker\big\{H^{1}(L,\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}\big\}.

Put

P_{\Sigma}=\prod_{w\in\Sigma}P_{w}

for

P_{w}=\begin{cases}H^{1}(L_{w},W)&\text{$w\nmid p$}\\
H^{1}(L_{v},W)_{{\mathrm{div}}}&\text{$w=v$}\\
\frac{H^{1}(L_{{\overline{v}}},W)}{H^{1}(L_{{\overline{v}}},W)_{{\mathrm{div}}}}&\text{$w=\overline{v}$}.\\
\end{cases}

We then have an exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\rightarrow S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})[\gamma-1]\rightarrow{\mathrm{Im}}\big\{H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},W)\rightarrow P_{\Sigma}\big\}\cap\ker\big\{P_{\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}[\gamma-1]\big\}.

(K.1)

(a)

In view of the finiteness of
{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})
the Poitou–Tate global duality implies that the
localisation

H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},W)\rightarrow P_{\Sigma}

is a surjection.
Hence

S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
is a torsion

\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
-module
by (
K.1
) and Nakayama lemma.

(b)

The Poitou–Tate duality yields a surjection

H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}

and so

S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})[\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}-1]=\ker\big\{H^{1}(L,W)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}[\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}-1]\big\}.

Now in light of (
K.1
) there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\rightarrow S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})[\gamma-1]\rightarrow\ker\big\{P_{\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}[\gamma-1]\big\}\rightarrow 0,

(K.2)

and the proof concludes.

∎

In conjunction with Remark
I.2
we note the following.

Corollary K.2
.

Let the notation and conditions be as in Proposition
K.1
.
Then

\#S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})[\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}-1]=\big|\#{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(g)^{2}\cdot\delta_{p}^{2}\big|_{\lambda}^{-1}

for

\delta_{p}=\begin{cases}1+p-a_{g}(p)&\text{if $\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)$}\\
1&\text{else.}\\
\end{cases}

K.1.2.
No pseudo-null submodules

Proposition K.3
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform
and
p\nmid 2N
a prime.
For
\lambda
a prime of the Hecke field of
g
above
p
,
suppose that
({\rm van}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
holds for the associated Galois representation and that either
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
or
a_{p}(g)=0
.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
) and
({\rm van}_{L})
.
If the condition (
nv
) holds, then
X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
has no non-zero pseudo-null submodule.

Proof.

We consider the ordinary case, and an analogous argument applies for the supersingular case (see also Remark
K.4
).

As seen in the proof of Proposition
I.18
,

X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})

is a torsion
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
-module under the condition (
nv
).
Suppose that
X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
has a non-zero pseudo-null submodule.
Then so does
X_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
by
23
23

23

Note that
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}},{\mathrm{ur}}}/(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})

does not have a non-zero pseudo-null submodule.
the exact sequence (
I.12
),
and in turn
X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
also does by the exact sequence (
I.13
).
The latter contradicts
[
131
, Prop. 3.3.19]
.
∎

Remark K.4
.

A special case as in
[
76
, Prop. 3.3.12]
suffices for applications in this paper.
More generally, if
X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})

is a torsion
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
-module, then
an argument of Greenberg
[
60
]
shows that it does not have a non-zero pseudo-null submodules.

A consequence of Corollary
K.2
and Proposition
K.3
:

Corollary K.5
.

Let the notation and conditions be as in Proposition
K.3
.
Then for a generator
h
of
\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))
, we have

|h(0)|_{p}^{-1}=\#S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})[\gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}-1]=\big|\#{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(g)^{2}\cdot\delta_{p}^{2}\big|_{\lambda}^{-1}.

K.2.
Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin theorem

K.2.1.
Mordell–Weil groups

Theorem K.6
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
A_{g}
an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Then

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1\implies{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{g}({\mathbb{Q}})=[F:{\mathbb{Q}}].

Proof.

Let
p\nmid 2N
be an ordinary
24
24

24

The existence of positive density of such primes is well-known (cf.
[
69
, §7]
).
prime.
Let
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
be embeddings, and
\lambda
the prime of the Hecke field
F
above
p
arising from
\iota_{p}
. Let
\rho:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(F_{\lambda})
be the attached Galois representation,
V
the underlying vector space and
T
a Galois stable lattice.

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
) and (
I.9
), in particular
(p)=v\overline{v}
with
v
determined via the embedding
\iota_{p}
.
Suppose that
L
also satisfies:

•

The classical Heegner hypothesis (
Heeg
),

•

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g_{/L})=1
.

The existence of such an
L
is a special case of the main result of
[
51
]
.

By the Gross–Zagier formula
[
61
]
, the Heegner point

P_{L}\in A_{g}(L)

arising from
y_{L}\in J_{0}(N)(L)

is non-torsion. In conjunction with the
p
-adic Waldspurger formula the non-triviality implies that

\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\rm Gr}(g_{/L}))\neq 0

(cf. Proposition
5.28
).
Hence

\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}({\mathrm{loc}}_{\overline{v}}({\mathcal{Z}}(g_{/L})^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}))\neq 0,

(K.3)

and the condition (
nv
) holds.
Then
\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}(\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})))\neq 0

by Corollary
I.23
,
and so

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})

is finite (cf. (
K.1
)).
In particular,

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{st}}}(L,V)=0

for

H^{1}_{{\mathrm{st}}}(L,V):=\ker\big\{H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},V)\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{v},V)\times H^{1}(L_{\overline{v}},V)\big\}.

In view of
[
129
, Lem. 2.3.1]
it then follows that

\dim_{F_{\lambda}}H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},V)=2.

(K.4)

Since
L(1,g^{\prime})\neq 0
,
Theorem
3.14

implies that

0\neq z_{\rm{Kato}}(g^{\prime})\in H^{1}(G_{L,\Sigma},V)
.
Note that

z_{\rm{Kato}}(g^{\prime})\notin H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(G_{L,\Sigma},V)

by the explicit reciprocity law
[
79
, Thm. 12.5]
.
In view of (
K.4
) it thus follows that

\dim_{F_{\lambda}}H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L,V)\leq 1.

On the other hand,

\dim_{F_{\lambda}}H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L,V)\geq 1

since the Heegner point
P_{L}
is non-torsion.
∎

Remark K.7
.

The above rank one approach is akin to that of Kato in the analytic rank zero case
[
79
, §13]
.
It is independent from the anticyclotomic Kolyvagin system of Heegner points
[
87
]
. The Heegner point
presents itself only in the form of reciprocity laws:
p
-adic Waldspurger and Gross–Zagier formulas.

K.2.2.
Tate–Shafarevich groups

Theorem K.8
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring. Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime and
\lambda
a prime of the
Hecke field
F
above
p
.
Suppose that either
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
or
a_{p}(g)=0
.

(a)

We have

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1\implies\#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})[\lambda^{\infty}]<\infty.

(b)

Suppose that
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)\leq 1
. Suppose also that
({\rm van}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and (
im
) hold for the associated
\lambda
-adic Galois representation.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
), (
Heeg
) and
({\rm irr}_{L})
so that
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g_{/L})=1.

Let
P_{L}\in A_{g}(L)
be the Heegner point arising from an
(\mathcal{O},\lambda)
-optimal modular parametrisation of
A_{g}
.
Then

\big[A_{g}(L)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}\cdot P_{L}\big]^{2}\geq\big|\#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g/L})[\lambda^{\infty}]\cdot\prod_{q|N}c_{q}(A_{g})^{2}\big|_{\lambda}^{-1}.

Proof.

Let
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
be embeddings, the latter so that it induces the prime
\lambda
of the Hecke field.

(a)

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field
as in the proof of Theorem
K.6
.
As seen in the proof, the Selmer group

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})
is finite and then so is

\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})[\lambda^{\infty}]

(see also
[
76
, §3.2, Rem. 3.2.2]
).

(b)

Note that
L
satisfies the hypotheses appearing in the proof of Theorem
K.6
.
So the Heegner point
P_{L}\in A_{g}(L)
is non-torsion and

[A_{g}(L):\mathcal{O}\cdot P_{L}]<\infty.

(K.5)

Moreover,

\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L}))\neq 0

and

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})

is finite.
Thus, Corollary
I.23
(a) gives rise to the divisibility

\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}},{\mathrm{ur}}}(g_{/L}))\big|(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))

(K.6)

in
\Lambda_{L}^{{\mathrm{cyc}},{\mathrm{ur}}}
.
We now consider its specialisation
at the identity Hecke character over
L
.

By Corollary
K.2
we have

\big|\#S_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})[\gamma_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}-1]\big|_{\lambda}^{-1}=\big|\#{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(g)^{2}\cdot\delta_{p}^{2}\big|_{\lambda}^{-1},

while the
p
-adic Waldspurger formula gives

\big|\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}))\big|_{\lambda}^{-1}=\big|\frac{1+p-a_{p}}{p}\cdot\log_{A_{g}(L_{\overline{v}})}P_{L}\big|_{\lambda}^{-2}

(cf. Proposition
5.28
and
[
76
, §5.1.5]
).

So in light of (
K.6
) and Corollary
K.5
it follows that

\big|\#{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(g)^{2}\cdot\delta_{p}^{2}\big|_{\lambda}^{-1}\geq\big|\frac{1+p-a_{p}}{p}\cdot\log_{A_{g}(L_{\overline{v}})}P_{L}\big|_{\lambda}^{-2}.

To conclude the proof, note that

\#{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})=\#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g/L})[\lambda^{\infty}]\cdot\frac{\big[A_{g}(L_{\overline{v}})/A_{g}(L_{\overline{v}})_{{\mathrm{tor}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}\cdot P_{L}\big]^{2}}{\big[A_{g}(L)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}\cdot P_{L}\big]^{2}}

(K.7)

(cf.
[
76
, Prop. 3.2.1 & (3.5.b)]
) and

\big[A_{g}(L_{\overline{v}})/A_{g}(L_{\overline{v}})_{{\mathrm{tor}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}\cdot P_{L}\big]\cdot\delta_{p}=\bigg|\frac{1+p-a_{p}}{p}\cdot\log_{A_{g}(L_{\overline{v}})}P_{L}\bigg|_{\lambda}^{-1}

(K.8)

(cf.
[
76
, p. 398]
).

∎

Remark K.9
.

The
p
-part of the conjectural BSD formula for
A_{g}
over
L
predicts the upper bound as in Theorem
K.8
(b)
to be an equality.
The above upper bound is finer than the one arising from the Kolyvagin system of Heegner points. Specifically,

]
,
[

]
.

In combination with Theorem

we note the following.

Corollary K.10
.

Let

be an elliptic curve of conductor

and
p\nmid 2N
a prime. If

Moreover, an upper bound for #
\hbox{\cyr X}(E_{/L})[p^{\infty}]
over imaginary quadratic fields

as in Theorem

(b) holds.

of

K.3.2.
Main result

Theorem K.12
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field with degree
d
and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring.
Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime,
\lambda p
a prime of the
Hecke field
F
above
p
and
T
the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
A_{g}
.
Suppose that

•

Either
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
or
a_{p}(g)=0
,

•

If
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
, then the
\lambda
-adic Galois representation
\rho:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}T
satisfies

({\rm irr}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and (
ram
). If
a_{p}(g)=0
, then
N
is square-free.

If
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g)=1
, then
the
\lambda
-part of the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture for
A_{g}
holds, that is,
{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{g}({\mathbb{Q}})=d
,
\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})[\lambda^{\infty}]
is finite and

\bigg|\frac{L^{(d)}(1,A_{g})}{d!\cdot\Omega_{A_{g}}R(A_{g})}\bigg|^{-1}_{\lambda}=\big|\#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})[\lambda^{\infty}]\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(A_{g})\big|^{-1}_{\lambda}.

In the CM case the same holds for any ordinary or a supersingular prime
p\nmid 2N
.

Proof.

We consider the ordinary case, and an analogous argument applies for the supersingular case.

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
) and (
Heeg
) so that

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g_{/L})=1.

The existence is a special case of the main result of
[
51
]
.
Observe that such an
L
satisfies (irr
L
). Indeed,
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{L}}
contains a unipotent element of order a power
p
by (
ram
) and (
Heeg
), and one may then proceed just as in the proof of
[
129
, Lem. 2.8.1]
.

By Theorem
I.21
Kato’s main conjecture holds for
g
and the quadratic twist
g^{\prime}
. Hence, the assertion is a consequence of Proposition
K.11
.
∎

Remark K.13
.

(i)

The ordinary case generalises the result of Jetchev–Skinner–Wan
[
76
]
for semistable curves. The supersingular case coincides with
[
76
]
and Corollary
J.2
, albeit a different approach.

(ii)

For CM elliptic curves over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with analytic rank one, the
p
-part of the BSD formula for
p\nmid 2N
is due to Rubin
[
122
]
and Kobayashi
[
88
]
for ordinary and supersingular primes
p
respectively.
Their approach relies on the
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formula, and the non-vanishing of
p
-adic height of non-torsion points, which is an open problem in the ordinary non-CM case.
The above approach instead relies on the
p
-adic Waldspurger formula, uniformly treating the ordinary and supersingular cases. An independent approach in the ordinary case is due to Castella
[
40
]
.

Appendix L
p
-converse to the Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin theorem

This section presents some
p
-converse theorems.
We begin with a preliminary form of the strategy under the hypothesis (
inj
), and then refine it.

L.1.
p
-converse and Kato’s main conjecture

L.1.1.
A cyclotomic criterion

Proposition L.1
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform. Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime,
\lambda
a prime of the Hecke field
F
of
g
above
p
and
V
the associated
p
-adic Galois representation. Suppose that either

\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
or
a_{p}(g)=0
.
Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
) and (
Heeg
).
Suppose also the divisibility

\xi\big(H^{1}({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}],T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda)/\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\cdot{{\bf{z}}_{\gamma}(h)}\big)\big|\xi(X_{{\mathrm{st}}}(h))

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

for
h\in\{g,g\otimes\chi_{L}\}
(cf. Conjecture
I.6
(b)).
If

The localisation
H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L,V)\rightarrow\prod_{w|p}H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L_{w},V)
is an injection,

(inj)

then

\dim_{F_{\lambda}}H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L,V)=1\implies{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g_{/L})=1.

Proof.

We present the ordinary case.

The hypotheses (
inj
) and
\dim_{F_{\lambda}}H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L,V)=1
imply that

\dim_{F_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Im}}\big\{H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L,V)\rightarrow\prod_{w|p}H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(L_{w},V)\big\}=1.

Hence the Selmer groups

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})
and
{\mathrm{Sel}}_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(g_{/L})

are finite by
[
129
, Lem. 2.3.2]
.

Note that the lower bound for Selmer group predicted by the cyclotomic main Conjecture
I.8

holds by our hypotheses and Lemma
I.17
. Hence the exact sequence (
I.13
) in combination with the finiteness of

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{ord}}}(g_{/L})
implies that

\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}({\mathcal{Z}}(g_{/L})^{{\mathrm{cyc}}})\neq 0.

In turn the hypothesis (
nv
) holds by (
inj
).

We now resort to Proposition
I.18
, which yields the cyclotomic Greenberg main Conjecture
I.12
.
In view of the finiteness of
{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\rm{Gr}}(g_{/L})
the latter implies that

\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\rm{Gr,cyc}}(g_{/L}))\neq 0.

Hence the Heegner point
y_{L}\in A_{g}(L)
is non-torsion by the
p
-adic Waldspurger formula
(cf. Proposition
5.28
).
The Gross–Zagier formula
[
61
]
concludes the proof.
∎

Remark L.2
.

For non-ordinary prime
p\nmid N
, the
p
-converse is also a consequence of lower bound for the strict Selmer group as predicted by Kato’s main conjecture (cf.
[
23
]
).

L.1.2.
p
-converse I

Theorem L.3
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring.
Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime,
\lambda
a prime of the
Hecke field
F
above
p
and
T
the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
A_{g}
.
Suppose the following:

•

Either
a_{p}(g)=0
and
N
is square-free or
\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
,

•

\rho:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}T
satisfies
({\rm irr}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
if
g
is non-CM, and (
ram
) holds if
p=3
.

Then

{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g})=1,\ \#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})[\lambda^{\infty}]<\infty\implies{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,A_{g})=[F:{\mathbb{Q}}].

Proof.

Since
{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g})=1
, note that
\varepsilon(A_{g})=-1
by the proof of parity conjecture
[
106
]
.

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
) and (
Heeg
) so that

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g_{/L})={\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,g).

The existence of
L
is a special case of the main result of
[
51
]
.

As
L(1,g^{\prime})\neq 0
, the Selmer group
{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g^{\prime}})
is finite (cf.
[
79
, Thm. 14.2]
).
In view of the splitting

{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g/L})\simeq{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g})\oplus{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g^{\prime}})

it follows that
{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g/L})=1
and
\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g/L})[\lambda^{\infty}]
is finite. In particular, the hypothesis (

) holds.
By Theorem
I.21
(c) and Theorem
J.1

Kato’s main conjecture holds for
g
and the quadratic twist
g^{\prime}
in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Hence, Proposition
L.1
concludes the proof.
∎

.

Let

For

{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{g}({\mathbb{Q}})=r[F:{\mathbb{Q}}],\ \ \#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{g})<\infty\implies{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,A_{g})=r[F:{\mathbb{Q}}].

Proof.

In light of
[

,
26
]
it suffices to consider the non-CM case. We may suppose that

Let

and

the prime of
F
above

arising from

. Let

From now, pick
27
27

27

For all but finitely many
\lambda
, the residual representation
\overline{\rho}
satisfies (irr
Q
) (cf.
[
104
]
,
[

, Thm. 2.1]
).

an ordinary prime
p\nmid 2N
so that
\rho
satisfies (irr
Q
).
By the hypothesis, note that

, and the

Remark L.6
.

The semistable case goes back to

L.2.
p
-converse and Heegner main conjecture

L.2.1.
Kato’s main conjecture and Heegner main conjecture

Proposition L.7
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a non-CM elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring.
Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime,
\lambda
a prime of the
Hecke field
F
above
p
and
T
the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
A_{g}
.
Suppose that

•

\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
,

•

The
\lambda
-adic Galois representation
\rho:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}T
satisfies
({\rm van}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
), (
Heeg
) and
({\rm van}_{L})
such that

\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}(X(g)_{{\mathrm{tor}}})\big|\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}\bigg(\frac{H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}})}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}\cdot\kappa_{g}}\bigg)\cdot\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}\bigg(\frac{H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}})}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}\cdot\kappa_{g}^{\iota}}\bigg).

(L.1)

Then the
\lambda
-adic Kato’s main Conjecture
I.6
for
g
and the quadratic twist
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
imply the Heegner main Conjecture
L.2
for
g
over
L
, that is

\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}\bigg(\frac{H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}})}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}\cdot\kappa_{g}}\bigg)\cdot\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}\bigg(\frac{H^{1}_{{\mathrm{ord}}}(L,T_{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}\cdot\kappa_{g}^{\iota}}\bigg)=\xi_{\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}}(X(g)_{{\mathrm{tor}}}).

Proof.

By Proposition
I.20
and (
L.1
) we have

\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L}))\big|(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L})).

(L.2)

It suffices to show that this divisibility is an equality.

For a character
\alpha:\Gamma_{L}^{\rm ac}\rightarrow R^{\times}
with
R
the integer ring of a
p
-adic local field, we have

\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))\big|(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))

(L.3)

by (
L.2
). Here the Selmer groups arises from the
G_{L}
-representation
T\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}R(\alpha)
, and the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha)
is just the image of
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L})
under the map

{\rm Tw}_{\alpha}:\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}\rightarrow\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{ac}}

induced by
\gamma\mapsto\alpha(\gamma)\gamma
for
\gamma\in\Gamma_{L}
.
Note that the anticyclotomic Greenberg main conjecture for
g
is equivalent to that for the twist
g\otimes\alpha
.
Henceforth we consider the latter for a well-chosen

\alpha\equiv{\bf 1}_{L}\ \mathrm{mod}\ \varpi^{m},

where
m
is a sufficiently large integer and
\varpi
a uniformiser of
R
.

Let
\alpha:\Gamma_{L}^{\rm ac}\rightarrow R^{\times}
be a character such that

\phi_{\alpha}({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L}))\neq 0,

(L.4)

and

\alpha\equiv{\bf 1}_{L}\ \mathrm{mod}\ \varpi^{m}\text{ for $m>\mu(X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))$}.

(L.5)

The existence of
\alpha
follows from the non-vanishing of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L})
and
X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})
being a torsion
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
-module.
In view of (
L.4
) and (
L.3
) the desired main conjecture for
g\otimes\alpha
is equivalent to the formula

\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}(\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha)))=\phi_{{\bf 1}_{L}}(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},\mathrm{ac}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha)).

(L.6)

In the following we approach it using cyclotomic Iwasawa theory.

To begin, the
\lambda
-adic Kato’s main Conjecture
I.6
for
g
and the quadratic twist
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
imply the cyclotomic main conjecture over
L
:

\xi(X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))=(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))

(L.7)

(cf. Lemma
I.17
(i)).

Note that

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha)\equiv\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L})\ \mathrm{mod}\ {\varpi^{m}},

and so
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha)
is non-zero.
Hence, in the non-CM case the Euler system of Beilinson–Flach elements for
T\otimes\alpha
yields the divisibility

\xi(X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))|(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))

(L.8)

in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
(cf.
[
93
,
85
]
, see also
[
38
, §3.3]
).
In view of (
L.5
) we have

\mu(X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))=\mu(X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))
and

\mu({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))=\mu({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}))
.

(L.9)

Therefore
the divisibility (
L.8
) holds integrally in
\Lambda_{L,\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}
by (
L.7
). The integral divisibility in combination with the
equality (
L.7
) leads to the cyclotomic main conjecture

\xi(X^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))=(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))

(L.10)

(see also
[
38
, §6.1]
).

The hypotheses of Proposition
I.18
hold by (
L.4
).
Therefore the two-variable zeta element and (
L.7
) yield the cyclotomic Greenberg main conjecture:

\xi(X_{\rm{Gr}}^{{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha))=(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm{Gr},{\mathrm{cyc}}}(g_{/L}\otimes\alpha)).

Its descent leads to the sough-after Bloch–Kato formula (
L.6
) (cf.
[
38
, §2.3]
).
∎

Remark L.8
.

The above strategy is reversal of the strategy of
[
38
]
which established an Eisenstein case of Kato’s main conjecture via Heegner main conjecture and the two-variable zeta element.

L.2.2.
Heegner main conjecture

Theorem L.9
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring.
Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime,
\lambda
a prime of the
Hecke field
F
above
p
and
T
the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
A_{g}
.
Suppose the following.

•

\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
,

•

The
\lambda
-adic Galois representation
\rho:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}T
satisfies

({\rm irr}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, (
ram
) and (
sur
).

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
), (
Heeg
) and
({\rm irr}_{L})
.
Then Heeger main Conjecture
L.2

for
g
over
L
is true.

Proof.

The divisibility (
L.1
) holds by Theorem
I.26
. Hence
the assertion is consequence of Proposition
L.7
and Theorem
I.21
(c).
∎

L.2.3.
p
-converse II

Proposition L.10
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a non-CM elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring.
Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime,
\lambda
a prime of the
Hecke field
F
above
p
and
T
the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
A_{g}
.
Suppose that

•

\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
,

•

The
\lambda
-adic Galois representation
\rho:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}T
satisfies
({\rm van}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

Let
L
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying (
2.15
), (
I.9
), (
Heeg
) and
({\rm van}_{L})
such that (
L.1
) holds.

Then the
\lambda
-adic Kato’s main Conjecture
I.6
for
g
and the quadratic twist
g^{\prime}=g\otimes\chi_{L}
imply the p-converse over
L
:

{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g/L})=1\implies{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,A_{g/L})=[F:{\mathbb{Q}}].

Proof.

The
p
-converse is a consequence of the Heegner main conjecture (cf.
[
26
,
142
]
), and so the assertion of Proposition
L.7
.
∎

Proceeding as in the proof of Theorem
L.3
, we deduce the following.

Theorem L.11
.

Let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be an elliptic newform,
F
the Hecke field and
\mathcal{O}
the integer ring.
Let
A_{g}
be an associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-type abelian variety over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\mathcal{O}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{End}}(A_{g})
.
Let
p\nmid 2N
be a prime,
\lambda
a prime of the
Hecke field
F
above
p
and
T
the
\lambda
-adic Tate module of
A_{g}
.
Suppose the following:

•

\lambda\nmid a_{p}(g)
,

•

\rho:G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}T
satisfies (
sur
), (
ram
) and (
sur
).

Then

{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Sel}}_{\lambda^{\infty}}(A_{g})=1\implies{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(s,A_{g})=[F:{\mathbb{Q}}].

Remark L.12
.

A related
p
-converse for non-CM curves is due to Zhang
[
149
]
and Skinner–Zhang
[
132
]
, assuming
p
-indivisibility of Tamagawa numbers. The approach relies on the principle of level raising and rank lowering as well as
[
131
]
. The use of two-variable zeta element bypasses the auxiliary level raising.
